Scope: current working tree and exact frozen candidates; local synthetic data only. This is a separate technical review, not an organizational approval signature or permission to execute a production release.

## Findings against current source

No newly confirmed local P0 implementation regression required a source change. This is bounded verification, not proof of absence of all bugs.

## Fresh execution and limits

The full `bash supabase/tests/run-all.sh` returns exit 0 with **ALL SUITES PASSED**: 38 direct SQL groups, actual-handler PostgreSQL integration, legacy Play migration, financial concurrency, provider/HMAC harnesses and 25 webhook checks. `run-mv006-local.py` separately reproduces baseline MV005/MV006 defects and verifies prevention, idempotent source installation, historical preservation, ownership/restoration races and MV003 proof. The 36 focused commands include 34 Node checks, mobile TypeScript and whitespace validation. Archive validation checks all 22 original/dependency/new frozen archives using cached Deno dependencies.

The additional independent rehearsal reconstructs the exact 66 baseline, executes frozen A then corrected C to 73, and checks D locking, precommit interruption, role denial, RLS/ACL preservation, frozen SQL dependencies and 74 retry refusal. Historical backfill/referral fixtures are checked separately in `additional-sql-results.json`. Exact command exits and durations are machine-readable; no failed assertion was removed or changed to obtain PASS.

A fresh `/tmp/medveda-closure-pg` cluster uses Unix socket port 55492, TCP disabled, and a credential allowlist. It uses Auth/Storage/cron stubs; it is not a managed Supabase restore. Synthetic provider transports do not prove Razorpay/Play acceptance, deployed JWT/Auth/cache behavior, real Storage object authorization, notification delivery, alert routing, queue operation under real workload, or native device behavior.

Initial failures are retained under `evidence/setup-attempt`: socket creation/connect was sandbox-restricted; narrowly scoped local execution was approved. The new cluster initially lacked the `postgres` role, causing database jobs to fail before assertions. Creating that role locally corrected setup. My additional harness initially normalized CRLF inside the historical `normalize_phone` fixture; C correctly rejected the resulting function drift. Passing the untouched file to psql corrected the harness, without changing SQL guards/artifacts. These failures are not product fixes or successful assertions.

AUD-008/AUD-009 fixture/runner problems no longer block the complete suite. Capture retry and D recovery additionally pass current source/package tests. Prior Phase A/B/C preservation and dependency claims reproduce locally; original B alone does not supply all post-C choice/reporting semantics.

Prior production delivery, historical-finance and recovery gaps remain open. Medium/low analytics, order-menu, dependency/lint work remains outside this P0 task; no broad project CI, native release or P1 completion is claimed. The earlier instruction to keep all P1 stopped is now distinguished from this request's option for safe P1 local development: see the final decision. No P1/P2 work was started.

Existing files and prior evidence must match `initial-hashes.json` at finalization; `preservation.json` records the result. Only new audit/evidence files were created. There is no corrected release candidate because no release-content correction was necessary.

# Migration 74 safety review

**Safe in the tested isolated sequence; not authorized for production.** The version count 74 describes the intended final production ledger, not the number of repository migration files.

## Exact sequence and identities

| Barrier | Exact artifact / behavior | Ledger |
|---|---|---|
| Baseline | Retained 66 production versions through `20261104110000`, reconstructed with the documented production function fixtures | 66 |
| A | Frozen `sql/01-phase-a.sql`: MV003 `20261110100000`, MV005 `20261110120000`, MV006 `20261110130000` | 66→69 |
| B | Separately pinned verifier/webhook/website creation delivery and hosted checks; no database version | 69 |
| C | Frozen corrected `sql/03-phase-c-atomic-compatible.sql`: `20261108100000`, `20261109100000`, `20261111100000`, `20261111110000` commit together | 69→73 |
| D | Frozen `sql/05-p0-forward-proof.sql`, source `20261111120000_p0_classified_reversal_commitment.sql` | 73→74 |

This is an explicit staged dependency ledger, not chronological blanket application. There are 75 local migration files because the isolated non-Play installer is excluded. Do not run `supabase db push`, premark versions or install the future full ownership index.

D source SHA-256: `ee522a5c7a9b9361de69942d5e1607314747a3c8813dcbc046ed68ca1320240f`.

D wrapper SHA-256: `2c351d660a1b85e4fccfc70573f6ccba4db282e0fdb3d2d51895fc1997163661`.

Corrected C SHA-256: `93dc2a645aa3ec0b266c8fc9356243fa83026b007b73399e90ae0948d4a23b72`. Original C remains unchanged and unusable as supplied: mismatched hash normalization and two missing ARRAY keywords reproduce.

## SQL and security analysis

D replaces only `public.razorpay_entitlement_committed(text,text)`, a STABLE SECURITY DEFINER boolean SQL proof, with fixed `pg_catalog, public, pg_temp` search path. Existing schema references are qualified. It does not change settlement, RLS, ownership triggers, constraints, tables, historical money/entitlements or prices. Its only semantic delta from strong C3 is the pending reversal classification predicate: explicit `blocks_entitlement=false` may cease to hold exact commitment while the verified reversal awaits application. Parked facts always hold; NULL/true facts hold. The exception concerns the retained reversal-event vocabulary, not arbitrary provider messages. Service-role provider re-fetch/classification is the trust boundary; clients cannot classify their own reversals.

All exact order/payment/purchase identities, snapshots, capture-status consistency and unconditional compensation exclusion remain. Historical absence of a capture marker is permitted; an existing conflicting capture is denied. Source inspection plus actual role tests confirms anon/authenticated cannot execute the proof, and service_role can. Replacement and grants occur in one transaction. [PostgreSQL documents that CREATE OR REPLACE preserves ownership/permissions](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-createfunction.html); explicit revokes and grant are therefore material, not decorative.

The wrapper uses READ COMMITTED, ledger SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE lock, 2-second lock timeout, 15-second statement and idle-transaction timeouts. It requires `postgres`, five nonempty evidence references, exact 73-version set, exact source arrays for all seven A/C sources, old C3 body MD5 `49b01fbcc7a5e71bd527366fd342a6e9`, and expected owner/definer/volatility/path. It atomically replaces the proof and inserts one exact D source record, then checks final body MD5 `c213bcd1e3f131e1f730c96789e97296` and anon/auth/service access before COMMIT.

Approval GUC strings are pointers, not proof of organizational approval. The wrapper does not compare every baseline source, every routine ACL, arbitrary role membership, RLS policy, trigger or hosted configuration. Its three-role proof checks are not an exhaustive unexpected-role audit. Fresh independent full security/drift review remains an explicit operator gate. Ledger locking serializes cooperating migration operators; it does not drain financial traffic or all privileged writers. Quiescence is mandatory. Lock semantics were checked against [PostgreSQL explicit locking documentation](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/explicit-locking.html).

## Rehearsal evidence

`atomic.log` checks the 126 preimages, original failures, corrected success, four C rollback injections, drift/index failure, competing lock timeout, observer visibility, client termination and preserved rows. `proof.log` reproduces old C3 failure, installs raw D twice without financial changes, and verifies final exact-proof regressions. Raw CREATE OR REPLACE is repeatable; the guarded operator wrapper intentionally refuses retry after known commit.

`forward-ledger.log` checks D missing gates, owner/security and source-array drift, failures after replacement and ledger insertion, exact source commit to 74, all public row fingerprints unchanged, known-commit retry refusal and pinned handler compatibility. `independent-rehearsal.log` adds an exact A→C→D chain, real competing ledger lock, observer-old-state during uncommitted D, client termination before ledger insertion, unchanged policies/relation ACL/RLS flags, actual SET ROLE denials and frozen RPC/relation presence at 74.

The reported classified-reversal “deadlock” is a logical self-blocking commitment/recovery condition; it is not evidence of a PostgreSQL wait-cycle deadlock. Both financial concurrency and migration locking are tested separately.

## Failure procedure and unresolved production gates

Before COMMIT: stop on errors/drift/timeouts, disconnect or roll back, independently verify complete pre-state, then review retry. After known commit: do not rerun; verify full post-state and use a separately reviewed additive recovery if needed. Connection loss around COMMIT means UNKNOWN: independently inspect version/source arrays, proof/body/owner/ACL/path, constraints/triggers/RLS/queues/history before deciding. Synthetic precommit termination does not establish behavior for every real network failure.

No inverse SQL or financial rewind is approved. Keep protections and ingress holds. Current complete recovery, hosted identity/drift, runtime capacity/lock timing, managed cache/Auth/Storage acceptance, operator/observer/window approvals and financial/provider disposition remain prerequisites. Isolated safety is not production GO.

# Financial reconciliation requirements

**Historical financial issues are unresolved. No production financial row, entitlement, provider state or accounting history was changed.**

The prior authorized read-only snapshot at `2026-11-11T12:37:42.621010+00:00` reports two successful purchases linked to non-paid orders and one duplicate-success owner/product group. These are inherited aggregates, not fresh observations. Current counts, case overlap, provider validity and the responsible writer are UNKNOWN. Local prevention tests establish possible historic failure mechanisms, not the provenance of these actual cases.

## Private cases and acceptance

| Case | Required investigation | Acceptance criteria | Safe next action |
|---|---|---|---|
| P0-FIN-001 | First success/nonpaid contradiction; privately bind its order, payment, purchase, provider and immutable entitlement snapshot | Evidence establishes captured/refunded/disputed/never-captured state, explains every conflicting application status, reconciles money and wallet/referral effects, and approves an explicit entitlement/replay/future-reversal outcome. Independently verify any separately authorized forward correction. | Owner assigns finance custodian; authorize minimized read-only case extraction and provider lookup separately. |
| P0-FIN-002 | Second success/nonpaid contradiction; independently gather the same evidence, even if apparently similar | Same criteria as FIN-001; do not copy conclusions from another record. Resolve overlap explicitly. | Open a separate private case and correlate only through protected identifiers. |
| P0-FIN-G001 | All members of the duplicate-success group, including excluded-index historical key, every distinct payment/order, and every successful ownership row | Establish whether multiple real captures or duplicate application representations exist. Approve surviving access and each original payment's replay/refund/dispute behavior. Preserve all history; prove that future reversals cannot remove unrelated legitimate ownership or regrant reversed access. | Obtain provider-backed facts for every member; prepare a reviewed disposition matrix before proposing SQL or financial action. |

Case labels do not imply three users, three payments or nonoverlapping records. No arbitrary oldest/minimum-ID selection, automatic duplicate status conversion, refund, purchase deletion, settlement replay, access revocation or future unique-index installation is acceptable closure.

## Exact private evidence bundle per case

1. Authorized project/account identity, read-only observation time, operator/reviewer, private row IDs and immutable evidence hashes. Record order/payment/purchase/provider states, linkage keys, amount in integer paise plus stored rupees, currency, provider reference IDs, price/tax/wallet/discount facts, course and chapter/subject/year/bundle snapshot. Include relevant captures, compensation, inbox, job leases/attempts, external reports/refunds, wallet and referral ledger entries and known writer/version chronology. Missing logs remain unknown.
2. **Razorpay, when that provider is actually linked:** authorized merchant/account and mode; independently retrieved payment and order facts, captured/authorized/refunded totals, amount/currency and event times; refund IDs/status/amounts, dispute/chargeback current state and history, settlement/reconciliation references where available. Preserve provider API/dashboard provenance, retrieval time and evidence digest. A callback signature or application `paid` status alone is insufficient.
3. **Google Play, only where actually applicable:** genuine package/account linkage, protected purchase-token/order reference, product/offer, purchase/payment/acknowledgement/consumption state, void/refund/cancel facts; for external-choice transactions, protected external transaction/report/refund IDs and accepted responses. Establish whether the token belongs to a Play purchase or an external reporting flow. Do not invent Play evidence for a Razorpay-only website purchase. Native/Console evidence comes from the authorized owner, not synthetic test flags.
4. A timeline aligning provider facts and application transitions; exact case overlap, current entitlement accessibility, offline/downloaded-access implications, financial conservation and discrepancies. Distinguish missing evidence from proven inconsistency. State separately the disposition of funds, application statuses, access, future replay and reversals.
5. Named finance owner and independent security/database reviewer approve exact protected preimages, proposed deltas or evidence-backed no-change disposition, recovery reference, execution identity/window, abort predicates and postchecks. Unresolved contradictory states require a separately reviewed forward plan; a signed explanation alone cannot certify an unfixed financial invariant.
6. After separately authorized execution, independent read-only verification establishes approved outcomes, reconciled amounts/references/snapshots, preserved original history, valid owner access and foreign-user denial, deterministic replay/reversal behavior, and no new duplicate ownership. Record transaction/commit outcome and append-only case history. Unknown commit requires investigation, not blind replay.

## Privacy and status

Public audit artifacts contain opaque case IDs, aggregate counts, acceptance status and hashes of protected evidence bundles only. Customer names, emails, phone numbers, raw tokens, provider credentials, order/payment IDs, addresses and row dumps remain in access-controlled owner records. Use a protected case crosswalk; do not publish unsalted hashes of guessable personal identifiers. Minimize access and apply the owner's retention policy.

All three cases remain **OPEN / provider evidence missing**. MV005/MV006 prevent tested future errors locally; they do not repair historical accounting. A database restore cannot establish or rewind provider money. Genuine provider operations and any production correction require separate authorization.

# Full recovery and restore plan

**No complete current production recovery point is demonstrated. This is a preparation plan, not permission to create/export backups, change PITR or restore production. No private backup material was restored or exported in this task.**

## Known evidence and gaps

The retained October 23 report confirms a complete restore failed on missing managed Auth `audit_log_entries.ip_address` in a cached Supabase image. A partial application-only restore and minimal Auth IDs passed; working identities, sessions and Storage were not restored. The earlier file inventory establishes only presence/sizes of `roles.sql`, `schema.sql`, `data.sql`, and a checksum list. It cannot establish a current consistent point or complete coverage. Earlier reports observed disabled PITR/empty managed inventories; current settings are UNKNOWN and were not queried here.

Supabase states database backups do not include Storage object bytes; restoring metadata alone cannot restore deleted objects. See [official backup documentation](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/backups). Database, files and configuration therefore need a coordinated recovery manifest.

| Component | Required recovery material and verification | Current status |
|---|---|---|
| PostgreSQL application | Consistent schema/data, ledger source arrays, roles/memberships/ACLs, extensions/versions, functions/security paths, RLS/policies, triggers, valid constraints/indexes, sequences and financial queues | No current full point demonstrated |
| Managed Auth | Compatible managed schema/service version; complete permitted users, identities, credentials/MFA/session state; hooks/provider/redirect/email configuration and JWT/signing recovery references | Prior complete import failed; working restore unproven |
| Storage metadata | Compatible managed schemas; buckets, object/version mappings, private flags, policies and metadata-to-file joins at declared cutoff | Complete compatible restore unproven |
| Object bytes | Notes, images and all required buckets/versions; immutable inventory, sizes/checksums, encryption/key availability, cutoff and missing/orphan reconciliation | Matching recoverable copy not demonstrated |
| Runtime and config | Exact Edge archives/dependency locks/JWT settings, project/API/Auth/Storage settings, endpoints/domains/DNS, client release compatibility and default-off gates | Candidates pinned; complete recovery config unproven |
| Secrets and key access | Vault/escrow references, required recovery identities/MFA, Auth/storage/provider/cron/SMTP/signing/alert access; owner-approved availability and session policy | Recoverability unknown; no secret values collected |
| Schedulers and integrations | Job definitions, disabled-state restore, lease/idempotency/reporting state, monitoring/alerts/on-call routing, provider webhook routing and retry deadlines | Operational recovery acceptance missing |
| Provider ledger | Durable facts since cutoff for captures/refunds/disputes, Play purchases/voids and external reports; reconciliation and duplicate-operation prevention | Provider-backed recovery procedure not exercised |

## Proposed RPO/RTO contract — owner acceptance required

Require an accepted complete recovery point immediately before a future release window. Proposed steady-operation financial database RPO: **at most 5 minutes**; proposed critical Auth/application/private Notes service RTO: **at most 4 hours**, including reconciliation and acceptance before resume. These are proposed requirements, not achieved measurements or promised service levels. Owner must either accept them or approve explicit alternatives and loss/exposure limits before production execution.

For each component record UTC recoverable cutoff, consistency boundary, allowable loss window and source. Compute RPO from the incident cutoff to the latest proven recoverable component state; a database point does not upgrade older object/config copies. Measure RTO from incident declaration/decision through provisioning, restore, reconciliation, access verification and authorized availability. Require **zero unexplained financial differences after provider reconciliation**, even where local events were lost inside the accepted RPO. Record ongoing provider obligations and reporting deadlines separately from the recovery target.

## Authorized isolated rehearsal sequence

1. Owner names backup custodian, restore operator, independent observer, security/finance approvers and incident/resume authority. Supply explicit authorization for a **named existing backup copy**, its permitted data/components, isolated destination, access controls, evidence retention and cleanup. No such material was authorized for restoration here. Backup creation/export, PITR changes and production restoration require separate scopes.
2. Build a protected recovery manifest: backup identity/provenance, UTC cutoffs, consistency method, checksums/signatures, encryption/key references, compatible PostgreSQL/Auth/Storage/extension versions, object inventory, runtime/config dependencies and omissions. Validate catalogs and dump hooks in isolation. Fail on unknown source, mismatched bytes or absent component; never silently skip a managed-schema error.
3. Provision an explicitly approved disposable compatible Supabase stack. Block production/staging network routes and outbound payment/email/push/webhook traffic; use isolated endpoints and inert credentials/transports. Keep crons, financial workers and ingress disabled. Verify resource capacity, access logs and service-version compatibility before import. A plain PostgreSQL stub build is insufficient.
4. Follow the platform-supported role/extension/schema/Auth/Storage/application import ordering for that exact material. Resolve circular dependencies through a reviewed procedure and explicitly verify every FK and index after import. Do not manufacture Auth columns, drop managed protections, replace identities with IDs, or count NOT VALID constraints as proven.
5. Restore matching Storage metadata and object bytes/versions; verify every required object hash and authorization mapping, bucket privacy and signed-URL behavior. Inventory missing and unreferenced objects without deletion. Restore config/runtime and secure references within isolation, with schedules and external transports still off.
6. Check consistent counts and canonical hashes against the recovery manifest, financial references/conservation, migration/source identities, function bodies/owners/paths/grants, RLS/trigger/index state, sequences, leases/queues and immutable entitlement snapshots. Use permitted controlled accounts to verify login/token refresh, MFA, expiry/reset policy and account isolation; exercise private Notes owner/foreign/anonymous access, legitimate historic entitlements and critical content integrity. Do not send real email/push or charge/refund providers.
7. Reconcile protected provider facts since each cutoff: captured authorizations, refunds/disputes, pending deliveries, consumed/voided Play tokens, external reports and idempotency IDs. Model exactly-once business effects despite retryable deliveries. Missing local acknowledgements do not prove an external operation never happened. No blind settlement/report/refund replay or re-creation of provider payments.
8. Measure each stage, final recoverable cutoffs, RPO, RTO, unresolved gaps and recovery capacity. Retain privacy-safe signed results and protected evidence references. Owner and observer accept the full result or reject it. Cleanup/retention follows the separately authorized plan; production data copies never enter this public audit directory.

## Acceptance and owner approval checklist

- [ ] Named authorized copy and isolated target; provenance, integrity, compatible versions and complete dependency manifest accepted.
- [ ] PostgreSQL, managed Auth, Storage metadata, matching object bytes, configuration and key recovery all succeeded; zero skipped managed failures.
- [ ] All expected constraints/indexes/sequences/roles/policies/functions/ledger and historical rows validated against protected expected evidence.
- [ ] Working identity/MFA/session policy and owner/foreign/anonymous Notes controls verified with allowed accounts; no outbound side effects.
- [ ] Financial/provider reconciliation has zero unexplained differences; replay, late callbacks and future reversals preserve legitimate ownership.
- [ ] Measured component cutoffs and end-to-end RPO/RTO meet accepted targets; owner explicitly approves any residual risk/alternative.
- [ ] Security, finance, restore operator and independent observer signatures name exact evidence digests and incident/resume procedure.
- [ ] Retention/destruction and escalation contacts approved; future real restore and each ingress/worker resume require separate authorization.

For a real incident, independently approve target, recovery point, expected loss and provider reconciliation before any restore. Preserve incident evidence and keep financial ingress held until post-restore acceptance. Do not overwrite later provider facts or deploy vulnerable historical handlers as an automatic rollback. This gate remains **BLOCKED**.

## Independent offline checks

[independent-integrity.json](evidence/independent-integrity.json) records the manifest SHA-256, all checksum results, independent import graph traversal, literal SQL dependencies and workspace differences. The independent checker verifies:

- Every `checksums.sha256` entry and every manifest SQL, review-document and prior-artifact hash.
- Five archive hashes, exact archive membership, absence of path traversal/link entries, complete reachable local import graphs, external imports and closure digest calculations.
- Each archived source equals its pinned package source; per-archive single-function JWT config and Deno dependency files equal the frozen files.
- All eight migration source copies equal the current workspace originals.

Separate `archive-validation.json` records extracted cached Deno type checks across all 22 original Phase B/C, compatible and P0 archives, including configs/source/hash checks. The final-schema independent rehearsal verifies every literal RPC/relation dependency extracted from the five closures exists at ledger 74. These structural checks complement actual pinned handler/SQL tests; they do not certify all dynamic SQL, hosted external behavior or remote build reproducibility.

A fresh scoped `node scripts/scan-secrets.mjs --stdin` scan of every frozen candidate file, including archive bytes, exits 0 without pattern findings. See `release-secret-scan.json`. This limited scanner is not a complete secret-history or credential-rotation audit.

| Frozen archive | Gateway JWT | SHA-256 |
|---|---|---|

# Financial integrity findings

Date: 2026-11-11 UTC. P0 only. ## Evidence and state model

Previous read-only production observation `2026-11-11T12:37:42.621010+00:00` reported 66 ledger versions, two successful purchases linked to non-paid orders, and one duplicate-success owner/product group. See [docs/audit/full-project-2026-11-11_123321/evidence/production-readonly.json](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/docs/audit/full-project-2026-11-11_123321/evidence/production-readonly.json). These are inherited aggregate facts, not newly queried records. Exact current counts, original writer, merchant capture/refund facts and case overlap remain UNKNOWN. Do not infer fraud, correct canonical ownership or provider settlement from status strings.

Orders hold server-priced immutable entitlement/price snapshots. Successful payment records bind order/user/product/amount/provider references. Successful purchases authorize the recorded course/scope. Captures record independently fetched provider facts; compensation rows preserve non-entitling captured payments. Provider inbox, durable jobs, refund reporting and wallet/referral ledgers retain reversals/retries. A paid order or capture marker alone is insufficient proof.

The reproducible historical risk paths are: a late failed/cancelled write could downgrade a paid order before MV005; the old partial ownership index excludes a historical purchase key and can admit a new success beside it before MV006. These reproduce possible mechanisms, not the actual provenance of the reported anomalies. Existing contradictory successful purchases remain accessible and return manual-review/pending on settlement replay rather than rewriting accounting or queuing a speculative refund.

MV005 [supabase/migrations/20261110120000_mv005_razorpay_order_transitions.sql](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/migrations/20261110120000_mv005_razorpay_order_transitions.sql:4) prevents future terminal-state downgrades. MV006 [supabase/migrations/20261110130000_mv006_purchase_ownership_guard.sql](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/migrations/20261110130000_mv006_purchase_ownership_guard.sql:5) uses the wallet advisory-lock namespace and a fresh READ COMMITTED snapshot for all new keyed success transitions, including restoration. Transaction-snapshot isolation grants fail closed; metadata updates on existing historical successes and legacy NULL-key scopes remain compatible. The future complete unique index is excluded from release; its synthetic test is not authorization to alter real duplicates.

## Fresh local verification

`evidence/mv003-006.log`: baseline defect reproduction; migrations twice; unchanged financial/catalog fingerprints; all four entitlement scopes; paid/failed/cancelled/expired states; concurrent failure-vs-settlement in both orders; duplicate callbacks/webhooks; compensation; refund/open/won/lost dispute; generic ownership INSERT/UPDATE/ON CONFLICT races; SQL rollback; repeated restoration; historical access. `evidence/atomic.log` verifies preservation through A/C. `evidence/proof.log` and `forward-ledger.log` verify the new D migration preserves rows and exact proof.

New defect: C3's unconditional pending-reversal denial made a verified partial refund or won dispute block its own settlement transaction. [supabase/migrations/20261111120000_p0_classified_reversal_commitment.sql](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/migrations/20261111120000_p0_classified_reversal_commitment.sql:51) exempts only trusted nonblocking pending classifications; every identity/snapshot/capture/compensation predicate remains. Parked/unknown/blocking reversals deny commitment. Actual webhook→SQL partial/won recovery passes afterward. Full refunds and lost disputes cannot regrant.

No historical financial row was repaired, deleted, canonicalized or revoked. Synthetic test mutations occur only in disposable databases. Current source readers intentionally preserve legitimate legacy access; moving a genuine historical payment to duplicate could break replay or future reversal behavior and is not an automatic remedy.

## Privacy-safe reconciliation cases

Create private cases **P0-FIN-001** and **P0-FIN-002** for the two earlier success/nonpaid contradictions, and **P0-FIN-G001** for the duplicate-success group. Do not assume these are three separate users or nonoverlapping records. Public evidence contains aggregate counts only. Each case is OPEN / provider evidence missing.

Use this template separately for each case:

| Required field | Operator entry / requirement |
|---|---|
| Internal case identifier | One opaque internal case ID; no public customer/provider identifiers |
| Current database facts | Authorized read-only timestamp, target identity, private row keys/preimages and evidence hashes; order/payment/purchase states, exact references, amount/currency, scope snapshot, wallet/referral entries, captures, compensation, inbox/refund/dispute facts; aggregate public summary |
| Required provider evidence | Correct merchant/account and independently obtained exact order/payment capture/authorization, amount/currency, timestamps, settlement, refund IDs/amount/status, dispute/chargeback state; stable source and retrieval time; no invented facts |
| Conflicting facts | Explain successful purchase vs unpaid order or multiple independent successful payments for one ownership key; distinguish missing evidence from contradiction |
| Potential entitlement impact | Current accessible Notes/course/scope, surviving legitimate ownership after any proposed refund/reversal, replay behavior, downloaded/offline access and affected account |
| Resolution options | Evidence-backed no-change/hold, narrowly reviewed forward status correction, separately authorized provider financial disposition, or explicit replacement-entitlement policy; retain all history; never arbitrarily select oldest/minimum ID |
| Human approval | Named owner and independent financial/security reviewer, exact private preimages/counts/evidence hashes, approved entitlement/replay/future-reversal policy, recovery reference and bounded execution plan |
| Audit trail | Append-only protected case history: actor/time/reason, evidence hashes, approval IDs, proposed and actual deltas, provider references, transaction/outcome and observer; secrets and PII stay private |
| Post-resolution verification | Independent read-only exact financial conservation/reference/snapshot checks, Notes owner/foreign-user access, reversal/replay behavior, preserved payment/purchase/refund/dispute/wallet history and no new duplicate group |

Never run historical settlement replay or the future full unique index to make these cases disappear. Any real disposition is a new, separately authorized action.

# Payment commitment and entitlement security

Date: 2026-11-11 UTC. P0 only. Local synthetic PostgreSQL/provider evidence; no production/staging connection, deployment, financial transaction, credential change, worker/schedule activation, Android build upload or payment activation. Existing approved artifacts and financial history were preserved. **Local verification is not production protection or acceptance.**


MV003's current exact proof binds paid Razorpay order, successful Razorpay payment, successful committed purchase, user, product, amount, nonempty provider references, course and full chapter/subject/year/bundle snapshot. It validates matching existing capture facts, rejects every compensation state, and denies unresolved reversal evidence. Legitimate historical purchases need no invented capture marker. New D changes only classification of already provider-verified nonblocking pending reversals, leaving all exact-identity predicates and service-only ACLs intact.

[supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts:96) proves commitment before replay and after settlement. An authorization, a capture, a void SQL return, a granted marker, Google reporting completion or a client success screen cannot establish success. Missing/throwing RPC, mismatched snapshot, failed SQL and interrupted recovery remain pending/retryable. The trusted PDF path authenticates the user, invokes backend Notes authorization and only then signs an object URL: [supabase/functions/get-note-url/index.ts](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/get-note-url/index.ts:32), [supabase/functions/get-note-url/index.ts](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/get-note-url/index.ts:43), [supabase/functions/get-note-url/index.ts](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/get-note-url/index.ts:58). Existing private Storage policy checks pass in `readiness_validation`.

MV004 [supabase/functions/verify-payment/index.ts](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/verify-payment/index.ts:83) authenticates recovery and filters order ID plus verified owner before provider work. Wrong-owner/nonexistent requests share denial behavior. Signed callback remains HMAC-authenticated without gateway JWT. Mobile recovery pins the initiating account and permits one bounded refresh; account switching cannot navigate to success from stale work. No frontend authorization logic changed here.

| Scenario | Fresh evidence / result |
|---|---|
| Correct payment/order/user/product/INR amount/provider refs/scope/snapshot | Actual SQL/helper/callback/webhook passes, all four scopes; `mv003-006.log`, `proof.log` |
| Wrong user/product/course/scope/payment/order/amount/currency/provider | Exact SQL proof false; callback/webhook deny; foreign-user recovery denied before provider; retained grants unchanged |

| Authorization without capture / capture without settlement | No false success; owner recovery finishes capture-only state; missing committed purchase remains pending |
| Void/error/failed SQL transaction | Missing/throwing proof, forced SQL error and failed ownership write roll back; normal void return cannot unlock |
| Compensation/refund/dispute/reversal | Compensation in every state denies; open dispute suspends, won legitimate restoration succeeds, full refund/lost dispute stays terminal; early partial/won recovery now passes |
| Duplicate callback/webhook/concurrent settlement/restoration | Independent-session serialization and one durable successful ownership; replay idempotence; conflicting restoration rolls back |
| Interrupted recovery / account switch | Capture-only and post-commit retry pass without duplicate grant; expired/forged/missing identity and switched account denied |
| Unknown reversal/provider mismatch | Strict recognized-result lists retain non-2xx/inbox/worker retries; only durable compensation/reversal dispositions acknowledge |
| Legitimate historical purchase / legacy NULL-product scope | Backend access and restoration preserved, no history normalization or destructive replay |

Actual HMAC computation and tamper rejection execute locally. Auth/provider/Supabase client HTTP transports are synthetic; local SQL functions/roles/triggers/transactions are real. Hosted gateway/Auth/cache/Storage/native and live payment-to-Notes behavior remain UNKNOWN, not passed.

## Operator-ready isolated rehearsal checklist

1. Obtain separate explicit authorization for the named existing backup copy and the isolated target. Backup creation, settings changes, new exports and restore operations are not authorized here. Assign owner/operator/observer and secure evidence retention.
2. Pin backup identity/time/consistency boundaries, file sizes/checksums/signatures, encryption and key-access references. Check headers/restore catalogs in a sandbox without executing unreviewed dump hooks; verify provenance and that paths/commands cannot reach hosted/staging endpoints. Record omissions.
3. Provision an approved disposable compatible Supabase stack with the matching managed PostgreSQL/Auth/Storage schema and extension versions. Block outbound provider/email/push/webhook traffic; keep all workers/crons off, use isolated endpoints, no production secrets. Validate compatibility before data import.
4. Restore permitted roles/extensions and schema in documented platform-supported order, then managed Auth identities and application data with dependencies respected. Preserve circular-FK procedure and validate every FK afterward; do not count NOT VALID constraints as verified.
5. Restore Storage metadata and matching object bytes; validate object hashes, versions, bucket privacy/policies and database-object joins. Reconcile all missing/unreferenced objects without deletion.
6. Restore exact runtime/function/config/JWT and secure secret references only within approved isolation. Auth password/JWT/MFA/reset and private Notes object-read tests need controlled accounts and inert external transports; do not send real mail/push or transact.
7. Check table counts and canonical hashes, financial references/conservation/snapshots, exact ledger and routines/ACL/RLS/triggers/index validity, sequences, historical access and cross-user denial. Fail the rehearsal on any unknown managed-schema error or missing component.
8. Compare provider facts since the recovery cutoff in a protected reconciliation ledger. Hold ingress; classify captures, pending callbacks, authorizations, reports, refunds/disputes and consumed Play tokens; retain original idempotency IDs. Do not blindly replay settlement or refund a second time. Lost local queue acknowledgements do not prove provider work was absent.
9. Measure restore/reconciliation/verification durations and actual recoverable cutoff. Owner/observer sign RPO/RTO and explicit remaining gaps. Destroy or securely retain the private isolated copy only under its approved retention procedure.
10. Before real disaster use, separately approve target identity, backup point, expected loss, provider reconciliation and staged Auth/Storage/application/worker resume. Preserve incident evidence. A local synthetic PostgreSQL build does not prove this managed recovery.

# COMPLETE PROJECT DEEP AUDIT

Audit date: 2026-11-11 UTC. Scope: current working tree, including pre-existing uncommitted changes. **Audit only; no deployment authorization.**

**Verdict: PRODUCTION NO-GO.** The repository contains a substantial functional application and strong synthetic payment/database regression coverage. It does not establish an accepted production release. No completion percentage is justified: screens, backend contracts and operational prerequisites have different weights, and managed/native/provider journeys remain unverified.

## Evidence and scope

The audit preserved the initial Git inventory and hashes before executing checks. See [initial status](evidence/initial-git-status.txt), [initial file hashes](evidence/initial-files.json), [recent history](evidence/git-history.txt), and [source inventory](evidence/source-inventory.json). The discovery index covers 510 source/schema/workflow files and 58,232 lines, including table and RPC references. There are 74 migration files. Dependency/generated directories were excluded from the source inventory; installed versions and lockfiles were inspected separately.

Tests ran in `/tmp/medveda-full-audit-snapshot`, with existing node_modules shared by symlink without dependency installation and no `.env` files copied. Generated builds and rehearsal output stayed in disposable paths or this new report directory. Existing audit artifacts were inputs, never overwritten. The initial tree already contained extensive mobile/backend fixes and untracked release candidates. HEAD alone does not identify this release.

This is a broad source and automated regression audit, with deeper manual tracing of authentication, payments, entitlement, migrations and admin operations. It is **not** exhaustive interactive execution of every screen/component, a penetration test, a device lab run or certification that no further bugs exist. A file appearing in the inventory means indexed, not every branch semantically verified. Unexecuted UI, hosted Auth/Storage and genuine provider/native behavior remain explicitly unverified.

## Discovered architecture

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  Student[Student Android app
Expo 57 / RN 0.86 / React 19] --> Auth[Supabase Auth]
  Student --> API[Supabase PostgREST + RLS]
  Student --> Edge[Deno Edge Functions]
  Student --> Local[SQLite / SecureStore / AsyncStorage]
  Admin[Admin React 19 / Vite 6 / TypeScript] --> Auth
  Admin --> API
  Admin --> Edge
  Web[Static marketing + auth recovery website] --> Auth
  API --> PG[PostgreSQL
orders / payments / purchases / content]
  Edge --> PG
  Edge --> Storage[Private Notes + quarantine storage]
  Edge --> RZP[Razorpay API / checkout.js]
  RZP --> Hook[Signed webhook inbox]
  Hook --> PG
  Edge --> Google[Play Developer API / external reporting]
  Cron[Separate authenticated workers / cron] --> Edge
  Edge --> Push[Expo Push / Android FCM]
  Student --> Sentry[Optional Sentry]
```

The principal consumer UI is `mobile-app`, with a web target; `admin-panel` is a separate browser application. `website` is a static generator, not a shopping web application. Root HTML and `github-original-website` are additional legacy/public surfaces and must not be confused with the generated website. Supabase supplies Auth, relational storage, RLS, service-only financial RPCs, private object storage, Edge Functions and scheduled work. There is no active Firebase payment backend in the audited application path. Comments mentioning Firestore are historical compatibility context.

## Functional conclusions

Locally exercised subsets include auth/recovery parsing and network handling, course selection/races, search pagination/cache, purchased ownership/account isolation, store loading/error behavior, checkout validation, signed verification, native PDF lifecycle mocks, offline owner boundaries, quiz state and synchronization, admin mutation/pagination helpers, PDF validation and financial SQL. See [feature inventory](FEATURE_COMPLETENESS_MATRIX.md) for boundaries.

Paid checkout is deliberately disabled in production Android profiles. Play billing choice requires enrollment, genuine tokens, SKU/offer consistency, server gates, authorized transaction reporting and actual native/provider acceptance. The marketing website has no implemented catalog-to-checkout UI. Support form submission opens the user's email app; it does not send mail itself. Large PDF upload depends on a separately operating validator worker. Push depends on genuine FCM configuration and device acceptance. Operational delivery of those dependencies is not established by source existence.

Confirmed defects include workspace acknowledgement of unresolved reversals, targeted notification disclosure, admin analytics truncation, unusable order-status menu choices, duplicate-import CI failures and an outdated test mock. The original C execution wrapper also contains two independently reproduced defects. Full details and reproduction evidence are in [security and bug report](SECURITY_AND_BUG_REPORT.md).

That nonzero run is not a complete pass; the supplemental runs and final command ledger record what was subsequently reproduced. No approved release SQL or application source was changed to obtain a pass. Prior retained logs are not counted as fresh tests.

It changes 126 definition comparisons to trim whitespace consistently and fixes two ARRAY expressions, preserving migration bodies. Original B is compatible with legacy post-C traffic but does not implement the complete new choice-reporting path. The four-function compatible closure is a separate release candidate; its source/archive identity must be delivered explicitly. Current workspace reversal behavior differs from that candidate.

# RAZORPAY PAYMENT AUDIT

Audit date: 2026-11-11 UTC. Scope: current working tree, including pre-existing uncommitted changes. **Audit only; no deployment authorization.**

**Source implementation: VERIFIED. Live production activation: UNKNOWN unless separately observed; the production Android source build is VERIFIED disabled. End-to-end production readiness: BLOCKED.**

## Complete flow and trust boundaries

1. `OrderSummaryScreen` obtains a server quote and selects a permitted provider. Build/runtime policy lives at [mobile-app/src/config/paymentProvider.ts:26](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/src/config/paymentProvider.ts:26) and [mobile-app/eas.json:51](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/eas.json:51).
2. `paymentService` sends product identity, not an authoritative price. [supabase/functions/_shared/createOrderHandler.ts:105](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/_shared/createOrderHandler.ts:105) calls service-only `create_priced_order`; product, scope, course, discount, wallet and expiry snapshots are server-owned. Ordinary Android headers are rejected on the website route. Headers are routing hints, not platform attestation.
3. Server creates a Razorpay order with the exact paise amount and binds its ID to the internal order. Provider transport has bounded timeouts. [supabase/functions/_shared/razorpay.ts:10](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/_shared/razorpay.ts:10) requires explicit APP_ENV and corresponding live/test prefix. This is a mode guard, not evidence of the configured account or credential ownership.
4. Checkout HTML uses Razorpay HTTPS checkout.js, nonce-bound native bridge handling and same-project verification URL validation. No direct native Razorpay SDK is installed. The mobile guards and synthetic WebView harness pass; real UPI/app-switch/native checkout was not executed.
5. [supabase/functions/verify-payment/index.ts:75](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/verify-payment/index.ts:75) distinguishes a provider-HMAC signed callback from unsigned owner recovery. Recovery uses Auth.getUser and filters by authenticated owner. Foreign and nonexistent orders have the same response. Callback signature does not let a client change the stored product/owner.
6. [supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts:24](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts:24) re-fetches provider payment and order, checks capture, INR, exact amount and order binding, records capture, and invokes transactional settlement. Authorization alone is refused. Server capture is required; client success messages cannot grant purchases.
7. `razorpay_entitlement_committed(text,text)` requires matching paid order, successful payment and purchase, owner/product/amount/provider/order/scope snapshot and absence of incompatible capture, compensation or unresolved reversal facts. A void RPC return is never proof. A historical purchase may validly predate capture markers.
8. [supabase/functions/razorpay-webhook/index.ts:108](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/razorpay-webhook/index.ts:108) verifies raw body HMAC before parsing, records durable event identity/hash, then runs the same settlement. Previous webhook secret supports legitimate retry during rotation; signatures do not establish deployment/account configuration.
9. Purchases and signed Notes delivery use database ownership, independently of the success screen. See the Notes audit.

## Refund and dispute semantics

Full refund revokes success access and reverses supported accounting; partial refund retains access. Open/under-review disputes suspend access; won disputes restore only eligible ownership; lost disputes reverse. Closed/unclassified provider state needs a documented operator policy. Capture and compensation alone never qualify as an entitlement.

**HIGH — current workspace defect:** [supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts:239](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts:239) and line 314 return `ok:true` for `unknown_payment`. The webhook can acknowledge and the worker can finish a reversal job without resolved settlement. [Independent reproduction](evidence/unknown-reversal-reproduction.log) compares current helper with compatible archive: workspace succeeds, candidate returns 503. Do not treat broad workspace deployment as the reviewed upgrade.

## Direct production answer

**IS RAZORPAY CURRENTLY ENABLED IN PRODUCTION? UNKNOWN for the actual live service/account. VERIFIED OFF in the current production Android build configuration.** A deployed endpoint, a key-shaped string, healthy project status or a public download link cannot prove payment activation. No provider dashboard credentials, secret values, real checkout or real financial transaction were accessed.

The separate non-Play test APK/profile has isolated package/data/test endpoints. Its existence does not activate production sales. Staging points to another project with placeholder public configuration and was not used. Neither deployed-test nor live capture-to-Notes acceptance is claimed.

Google's current India program requires completed onboarding, per-app opt-in, Play alongside the alternative and reporting authorized transactions within 24 hours. MedVeda-specific compliance remains unverified. [Official India requirements](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/13306652?hl=en). Razorpay requires server signature verification and captured payment confirmation before fulfillment; the local candidate follows those boundaries. [Official integration guidance](https://razorpay.com/docs/payments/server-integration/nodejs/integration-steps).

| Environment | Verdict | Evidence / unresolved condition |
|---|---|---|
| Local synthetic | VERIFIED WORKING for tested scope | Actual callback/webhook/HMAC/settlement code, synthetic provider/Auth transport, real service-role PostgreSQL and ownership RPCs; fresh atomic and SQL logs |
| Deployed test | BLOCKED | No authorized complete test identity/provider fixture/native environment acceptance performed; no staging functions invoked |

## Flow traced

`NotesScreen / ChapterDetailsScreen → UnlockOptionsPanel → OrderSummary → provider order → checkout → signed callback/webhook or authenticated recovery → provider capture GET → SQL settlement → exact commitment proof → purchases read → signed Notes URL`.

[mobile-app/src/services/purchasesService.ts:72](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/src/services/purchasesService.ts:72) maps successful chapter/subject/year/bundle scopes. [mobile-app/src/hooks/usePurchases.ts:1](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/src/hooks/usePurchases.ts:1) filters by current course. [mobile-app/src/hooks/usePurchaseResource.ts:17](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/src/hooks/usePurchaseResource.ts:17) masks old-account data immediately, uses generation checks, bounded reads, context/auth subscriptions and resume refresh. Same-context read failure may retain last confirmed ownership with an explicit error; that cached UI state does not authorize storage.

[supabase/functions/get-note-url/index.ts:49](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/get-note-url/index.ts:49) verifies the actual Auth user and calls `user_can_access_note_with_aal`; unauthorized, blocked, inactive/unpublished or out-of-scope content is denied by the SQL contract. It signs private Notes objects for 120 seconds only after authorization. Real managed Storage signing/object bytes were not tested locally; local stubs do not prove object availability or hosted policy behavior.

[mobile-app/src/screens/payment/PaymentSuccessScreen.tsx:45](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/src/screens/payment/PaymentSuccessScreen.tsx:45) rechecks authenticated owner, paid order, successful purchase and matching amount. Route parameters cannot independently display confirmed success. Owner-switch regression passes.

## Safety and recovery matrix

| Scenario | Independently established local behavior | Remaining limit |
|---|---|---|
| Valid captured purchase | Matching purchase/owner/scope settles and SQL reader permits intended Notes | Real Storage signing and native rendering unverified |
| Authorization only | Refused as entitlement; choice reporting can queue without a purchase | Genuine authorization timestamp/report acceptance unknown |
| Fake client success | No grant without HMAC/provider facts/SQL proof | Modified clients cannot change server RPC authorization |
| Wrong amount/order/currency | Rejected; mismatched captures can queue compensation | Compensation execution deliberately never invoked remotely |
| Wrong owner/product | Recovery/commitment and storage ownership checks deny | Full managed Auth/IDOR penetration testing outstanding |
| Failed/cancelled checkout without capture | No purchase | Late captured charge is a different state and may recover safely |
| Replay/concurrent delivery | One committed grant, token/order binding and ownership locking | Existing historical duplicate rows intentionally preserved |
| Interrupted checkout | Owner-authenticated provider lookup retries | Real app termination/UPI switch not tested |
| Login/logout/restart | Persisted server entitlement plus auth/context refresh code and isolated regressions | Real device persistent session/restart journey unexecuted |
| Historical success | Retained reader access and fingerprints tested on synthetic contradictory/duplicate history | No automatic historical financial repair; production restoration not certified |
| Refund/dispute | Full revoke, partial retain, suspend/won restoration rules tested in SQL/candidate | Workspace unknown reversal acknowledgement remains a defect |
| Offline refund revocation | Denied server check deletes local access; recent confirmed owner can use grace while offline | Up to seven days delayed revocation is explicit local policy, not immediate offline enforcement |

Offline grace is defined in [mobile-app/src/services/offlineNoteVerification.ts:3](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/src/services/offlineNoteVerification.ts:3) and used by `offlineNoteAccess.ts`. This tradeoff requires an approved access policy and real device testing. A rooted device/local file extractor is outside the app's ordinary authorization boundary; screenshot blocking is not DRM certification.

No synthetic test here establishes that every real Notes object, product scope, legacy purchase or published row is correctly configured. Before activation, use a controlled accepted test account to prove exact product scope, private object delivery, refresh/library visibility, logout/restart, duplicate callback/webhook, interruption and reversal policy in the intended deployment. No production transaction is authorized by this report.

# DATABASE PUSH READINESS

Audit date: 2026-11-11 UTC. Scope: current working tree, including pre-existing uncommitted changes. **Audit only; no deployment authorization.**

**CAN I SAFELY RUN `supabase db push` RIGHT NOW? NO — BLOCKED.** Do not use a blanket timestamp sweep for this release.

## Pending files and intended sequence

The repository has 74 migrations. The pinned production baseline contains 66 through `20261104110000`; fresh production evidence, where obtained, is recorded in the execution ledger and must be rechecked immediately before any future operation.

| Pending filename | Role | Delivery constraint |
|---|---|---|
| 20261108100000_notes_choice_checkout.sql | C1 | After A1/A2/A3, inside atomic C |
| 20261109100000_billing_settlement_recovery.sql | C2 | Replaces commitment semantics; never expose alone |
| 20261109120000_isolated_nonplay_test.sql | Separate test installer | EXCLUDED from seven-migration production package |
| 20261110100000_mv003_razorpay_entitlement_commitment.sql | A1 | First; strong service-only proof |
| 20261110120000_mv005_razorpay_order_transitions.sql | A2 | Second; terminal/captured status protection |
| 20261110130000_mv006_purchase_ownership_guard.sql | A3 | Third; keyed ownership/advisory locking |
| 20261111100000_phase_c_final_mv003_commitment.sql | C3 | Restore final strong proof in same C transaction |
| 20261111110000_phase_c_play_entitlement_snapshot.sql | C4 | Final Play snapshot/proof |

Intended sequence is baseline 66 → atomic A1/A2/A3 → 69 → separately verified B delivery → atomic C1/C2/C3/C4 → 73 → separately approved compatible Edge delivery/resume. The excluded test installer explains 74 local files versus 73 final production versions. Its installation is not implicitly authorized. Future full ownership index under `supabase/maintenance` is also a separate operation. Timestamp order differs from the reviewed staged sequence; migration-ledger repair/pre-marking is not a safe substitute.

## Independently reproduced release behavior

[docs/audit/production-execution-20261111/03-phase-c-atomic.sql:34](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/docs/audit/production-execution-20261111/03-phase-c-atomic.sql:34) compares hashes of untrimmed function definitions against trimmed collector identities. Matching baseline fails at `accounts_share_network`. After only correcting that comparison in the disposable copy, [docs/audit/production-execution-20261111/03-phase-c-atomic.sql:169](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/docs/audit/production-execution-20261111/03-phase-c-atomic.sql:169) and line 1283 fail `FOREACH t IN ARRAY[...]` parsing. Correct syntax requires `IN ARRAY ARRAY[...]`.

The separate corrected wrapper has SHA-256 `93dc2a645aa3ec0b266c8fc9356243fa83026b007b73399e90ae0948d4a23b72`. Derivative validation checks only 126 trim expressions and two ARRAY keywords change; approved original artifacts remain untouched. It is a technical candidate, not a newly approved production package.

Fresh tests reproduced 126 exact baseline function preimages; atomic A 66→69; corrected C 69→73; seven exact ledger source arrays; strong final MV003 bodies/ACLs; refusal on post-success retry; missing gate refusal; each C-stage forced rollback; synthetic historical public-table row fingerprints unchanged. Fresh drift/interruption/lock evidence and any harness limitations are listed in `evidence/TEST_EXECUTION.md`; prior logs alone are not proof of reproduction.

The procedure uses 2-second lock and 15-second statement/idle transaction limits. C schema changes can block order readers. An independent observer sees strong A and 69 ledger versions while C2 is uncommitted; MVCC does not expose the weaker uncommitted body. This does not measure production lock duration, account for real traffic or prove a hosted network failure at COMMIT is recoverable.

## Exact blockers

1. Original execution wrapper is defective. Independently review the separate corrected candidate and incorporate its identity plus the compatible four-function upgrade into a newly approved execution manifest/runbook.
2. Confirm current target identity, exact migration source ledger, all function owners/search paths/ACLs, triggers, RLS, index validity and dependencies. A retained baseline is not a current drift check.
3. Demonstrate a current compatible restore covering application schema/data, managed Auth identities/config, Storage metadata AND object bytes, policies, secrets/config references and approved RPO/RTO. Existing partial restore evidence is insufficient.
4. Drain ingress and independently authorized financial workers across the transaction and compatible delivery boundary, with durable provider retries. The Buy Now flag does not stop a cron-authenticated worker.
5. Keep complete C atomic: C2 must not become a committed externally visible weaker proof. Never infer compatibility merely from an unchanged RPC signature.
6. Deliver pinned source/import/JWT closures, not a broad workspace deployment. The current workspace helper still acknowledges unknown reversal outcomes; original B omits new choice-reporting behavior.
7. Name operator, observer, incident owner, window and post-check/resume conditions; review advisory lock ordering and hosted workload capacity.
8. Resolve unknown commit outcome by an independent exact full-ledger/function/ACL/column/index/data inspection. Never rerun blindly after a lost connection, and never rewind financial history to force a retry.

New nullable reporting fields are excluded from canonical old-row fingerprints. That is scoped evidence, not certification of the live data or a full restore.

Existing success-on-unpaid contradictions and duplicate successes must be investigated using provider evidence and approved forward reconciliation. Do not delete, deduplicate, replay accounting or revoke historical access automatically. Keep additive guards after failure; review every old-handler rollback against the final schema. # SECURITY AND BUG REPORT

Audit date: 2026-11-11 UTC. Scope: current working tree, including pre-existing uncommitted changes. **Audit only; no deployment authorization.**

**No CRITICAL exploitable vulnerability was independently demonstrated. That is not a clean bill of health.** HIGH findings below block launch. All reproductions use synthetic data and never attack or mutate production. The current deployed release is separately behind the reviewed local financial guards.

## Confirmed defects

### AUD-001 — HIGH — Current workspace acknowledges unresolved financial reversals

Location: [supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts:239](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts:239).

- Reproduction: Invoke actual helper with authenticated synthetic provider refund/dispute and RPC result `{status:unknown_payment}`.
- Expected: Keep the event/job retryable until the reversal resolves.
- Actual/root cause: Returns `ok:true`; webhook can acknowledge and worker can finish unresolved work. The separate compatible candidate returns 503.
- Impact: Unresolved refunds/disputes can stop receiving provider retries; durable inbox helps only if a worker actually revisits it.
- Recommended fix: Ship an independently reviewed complete candidate closure; reconcile workspace with fail-closed result classification in a separate change.
- Required regression: Assert unknown/null/unrecognized refund and dispute outcomes produce retryable failure across helper, webhook and worker.
- Evidence: [unknown-reversal-reproduction.log](evidence/unknown-reversal-reproduction.log).

### AUD-002 — HIGH — Targeted notification becomes globally readable

Location: [supabase/functions/send-push-notification/index.ts:94](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/supabase/functions/send-push-notification/index.ts:94).

- Reproduction: Invoke actual handler with synthetic admin aal2 and one targetUserIds entry; capture inserted notification. Inspect active-row read policy.
- Expected: Only intended recipients read a targeted message.
- Actual/root cause: Push tokens are filtered, but the inserted notification is active and carries no recipient boundary.
- Impact: Potential disclosure if an admin uses the exposed targeted API for private/payment-related messages. Current admin composer sends broadcasts, so it does not itself demonstrate a real disclosure.
- Recommended fix: Use recipient-scoped notification rows and RLS, or explicitly reject targeted requests until supported.
- Required regression: Read targeted message as recipient, another authenticated user and anonymous role; only recipient/admin should succeed.
- Evidence: [targeted-notification-reproduction.log](evidence/targeted-notification-reproduction.log).

### AUD-003 — MEDIUM — Admin analytics truncates orders at server row cap

Location: [admin-panel/src/services/analyticsService.ts:28](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/admin-panel/src/services/analyticsService.ts:28).

- Reproduction: Run actual service against bounded API mock: 1,001 paid INR 1 orders, first response limited to 1,000.
- Expected: Report 1,001 orders and INR 1,001 revenue.
- Actual/root cause: One unpaginated read reports 1,000 and INR 1,000. No stable ordering or continuation.
- Impact: Incorrect financial dashboard totals/top products above configured API limit; production threshold not stress-tested.
- Recommended fix: Prefer audited server aggregate RPC or correct complete pagination with deterministic ordering.
- Required regression: More than one page, mixed paid/refunded rows, course filter, exact totals and stable top product ties.
- Evidence: [admin-defect-reproduction.log](evidence/admin-defect-reproduction.log).

### AUD-004 — MEDIUM — Admin order menu offers forbidden status mutations

Location: [admin-panel/src/components/OrdersTable.tsx:79](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/admin-panel/src/components/OrdersTable.tsx:79).

- Reproduction: Choose Paid, Refunded or Pending as a change on a different-status order. Trace updateOrderStatus to admin_set_order_status.
- Expected: Offer permitted actions, report provider-owned statuses as read-only.
- Actual/root cause: RPC explicitly permits only cancelled/failed on an unpaid unsettled order; menu offers created/paid/refunded and omits cancelled.
- Impact: Misleading nonfunctional controls; backend appropriately rejects escalation, so this is not a payment bypass.
- Recommended fix: Model server states/actions separately and expose only allowed unpaid cancel/fail operations.
- Required regression: Menu actions by current state/provider; rejected mutation surfaces useful error; no direct paid/refunded write.
- Evidence: [admin-defect-reproduction.log](evidence/admin-defect-reproduction.log).

### AUD-005 — LOW — Admin duplicate imports fail mandatory CI lint

Location: [admin-panel/src/components/RouteErrorBoundary.tsx:2](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/admin-panel/src/components/RouteErrorBoundary.tsx:2).

### AUD-007 — MEDIUM — Original atomic C wrapper has invalid FOREACH syntax

### AUD-010 — MEDIUM — Mobile dependency tree contains moderate advisories

## Confirmed production gaps and data anomalies

Fresh read-only `production-readonly.json` shows 66 ledger versions, no `razorpay_entitlement_committed` RPC, and deployed verifier/webhook bundles without the commitment call. This is a **HIGH deployment/financial acceptance gap** relative to the audited candidate; a pending-code fix does not protect production. Bundle feature-string inspection is structural evidence, not a remotely executed exploit. The new handler/proof deployment must be delivered as an approved compatible unit.

Aggregate production inspection found **2 successful purchases on non-paid orders** and **1 duplicate-success owner/product group**. Root cause, provider capture legitimacy and correct accounting are UNKNOWN. Preserve records and access; inspect provider evidence privately before an approved forward repair. These are confirmed inconsistencies, not proof of fraud or permission to replay/delete financial history.

## Security controls verified within scope

Actual local HMAC code rejects forged and malformed signatures; raw webhook bodies are authenticated before parsing; owner recovery authenticates with Auth.getUser; server-set prices and immutable order snapshots prevent client repricing; service-only financial RPC grants, ownership checks and RLS/SQL regression suites deny tested cross-user access. Final keyed ownership guard and settlement/token locks pass independent-session tests. Admin reads/writes require server membership and configured MFA; no service-role token or private key was found by the scoped current-file pattern scan (1,084 files). Public Supabase publishable keys are not service secrets.

Source inspection covers SECURITY DEFINER search paths/owners/ACLs, payment/content policies, trusted upload validation/quarantine, bounded provider requests, idempotence and recovery boundaries. Fresh production metadata records relevant financial policies/grants/functions without exposing private user records or secrets. RLS enabled alone does not prove all policies correct; service-role intentionally bypasses it and therefore requires handler authentication.

## Suspected risks / unresolved verification (not confirmed exploits)

- Complete secret history/rotation, high-entropy credentials, private dependency archives and every generated artifact were not comprehensively scanned. Prior evidence documents a historical test-key incident; rotation status remains unknown.
- API-body size ceilings, rate-limit trusted-IP behavior and high-concurrency provider outages need hosted tests. CORS is not an authorization control.
- Real managed Auth JWT/gateway behavior, MFA enrollment/refresh, password hook/config and Storage object ACL acceptance remain untested.
- Large PDF worker runtime, capacity, IAM, backlog alerting and malicious file corpus/device rendering require operational tests. Trusted structural validation is not proof of malware-free PDFs.
- Push success counts HTTP-accepted messages without proving per-ticket/device receipt; delivery, credentials and dead-token handling need controlled device tests. Do not claim delivery based solely on HTTP 200.
- Seven-day offline entitlement grace permits delayed revocation while offline; obtain explicit policy acceptance.
- Remaining unpaginated list surfaces (referrals, notification/catalog diagnostic reads) may truncate at volume; analytics truncation alone was reproduced here.
- Debug signing in ignored generated Gradle is unsuitable for direct local store submission; EAS remote signing is a distinct path and was not freshly verified.

No source/test code, auth gate, RLS policy, approved SQL or financial data was changed by this audit.

## Consumer frontend

`AppNavigator.tsx` uses validated routes/lazy screens, session/course state, maintenance/update gates and recovery navigation. Screens have implemented data repositories, loading/error/empty states; isolated regressions exercise list failure behavior, latest-response wins, timeout/cancellation, owner/context isolation, search, auth submit handling, store catalog, unlock options, purchases and PDF lifecycle. These are test-scoped passes. Complete interactive navigation, every responsive breakpoint, keyboard/screen-reader behavior, browser console and memory profiling were not performed.

Current production app payment provider is explicitly disabled in [mobile-app/eas.json:51](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/eas.json:51). App config rejects production profile overrides to paid providers; package/runtime checks prevent standard Android from using isolated non-Play checkout. Therefore inaccessible Buy Now in this profile is intentional gating. It is not evidence that purchases have been launched successfully. Existing owned content reads remain separate from permission to begin a new sale.

Notes/PDF uses server-authorized short-lived URLs; native reader refresh/retry/foreground lifecycle is tested through mocks. Actual large/complex PDF render, download interruption, Android process death, screenshot handling, low memory and offline storage recovery need device tests. Offline SQLite is deliberately unsupported on web. Web reader uses browser iframe, with fewer native lifecycle controls; expired signed URLs and account switching need browser acceptance.

Support form opens mailto and requires an installed mail client and final user Send action ([mobile-app/src/screens/contact/ContactScreen.tsx:42](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/src/screens/contact/ContactScreen.tsx:42)). There is no backend mail delivery service. Videos are free content through authorized URL resolution, not Notes payment entitlements. Quiz questions hide answer keys and score through server submission; offline attempts wait for synchronization/verification rather than inventing a trusted local score.

## Android build / store readiness

Technology is Expo 57.0.32, React Native 0.86.3, React 19.2.3, TypeScript 6.0.x, Hermes/New Architecture. Config retains `in.medveda.app`, MedVeda branding, version 1.2.2 and disabled-payment runtime. Generated Android source exists but is Git-ignored; it is supporting evidence, not a release artifact identity. Local Gradle `release` uses template debug signing. Do not submit a direct local build from that configuration. The EAS remote credential path must be checked on its own; prior reports of remote signing metadata were not reproduced here.

SDK-equipped compilation and final artifact checks remain absent: release dependency resolution, minification/resource shrinking, mapping upload, merged permissions, signing/cert identity, target SDK, supported ABIs, 16 KB native alignment, device smoke and Play versionCode acceptance. The attempted wrapper stopped before compiling. No claim that native source is unbuildable follows from that failure.

The `expo-iap` dependency remains installed even when its config plugin is omitted for disabled production. Billing permission in a final merged artifact does not prove sales enabled. The locally declared retained billing chain and prior native artifacts do not establish the resolved graph of a new release. Google's Billing 7 extension ended November 1, 2026; this release needs supported Billing 8+ and genuine program acceptance. [Current official timeline](https://developer.android.com/google/play/billing/deprecation-faq).

Local Firebase Android config and OTA signing certificate were absent in the inspected source snapshot. Remote file/env credentials may exist elsewhere and are UNKNOWN. Genuine FCM registration/delivery and OTA integrity must be accepted in the intended build. Optional Sentry is enabled only when DSN exists ([mobile-app/src/config/sentry.ts:31](/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/src/config/sentry.ts:31)); a source import does not prove crash delivery/alerting. Existing old AAB artifacts predate this working tree and cannot certify it.

## Admin dashboard

Login/MFA/membership gating and route protection are implemented; content CRUD connects to RLS-controlled Supabase data. Notes use trusted upload plus atomic attachment; large files require quarantine validation worker. Product pricing/publishing, users, orders, referrals/settings/audit, review flags, analytics, notifications and Play diagnostics all have concrete service wiring. Admin helper tests/build pass. Hosted create/edit/upload/readback and mobile propagation were not exercised because they require writes.

Confirmed broken behavior: order menu offers forbidden Paid/Refunded/Pending writes (AUD-004); analytics omits subsequent result pages (AUD-003). CI lint fails (AUD-005). Targeted API notifications violate recipient isolation (AUD-002). Server rejects unauthorized admin financial status escalation correctly; the misleading UI does not imply an authorization bypass.

## Website

The generated website has marketing/course/about/download/FAQ/contact/legal/delete-account guidance and auth recovery/confirmation pages. It is not an implemented website checkout/catalog/library application. Root legacy HTML and generated website duplicates need a documented hosting source; actual Vercel routing/deployment identity is UNKNOWN. Static link/build acceptance does not verify live account confirmation, reset emails, external links, app-store availability, accessibility or visual layout. Those remain acceptance work.

# FEATURE COMPLETENESS MATRIX

Audit date: 2026-11-11 UTC. Scope: current working tree, including pre-existing uncommitted changes. **Audit only; no deployment authorization.**

Status applies to the named implementation and stated test boundary, never automatically to production. VERIFIED WORKING means the specific local behavior has executable evidence; it does not certify an entire managed/native journey. IMPLEMENTED BUT UNTESTED denotes source wiring without an executed functional acceptance. PARTIALLY WORKING denotes tested subsets with outstanding integration/native/operational dependencies. BLOCKED identifies a required unmet gate. BROKEN identifies confirmed defects. UNKNOWN means insufficient evidence; NOT IMPLEMENTED and NOT APPLICABLE are explicit boundaries.

No percentage is calculated. Inventory groups overlap and are not consistently weighted. Every listed UI surface has source evidence; see the discovery JSON for per-file lines, referenced tables and RPC calls. Backend financial/schema evidence is in the SQL/rehearsal logs and production metadata. Prior reports are not used to promote a status to verified.
