# PRODUCTION READINESS GO NO GO

Audit date: 2026-11-11 UTC. Scope: current working tree, including pre-existing uncommitted changes. **Audit only; no deployment authorization.**

**PRODUCTION NO-GO.** Local financial safeguards have meaningful test evidence, but mandatory deployment, security, recovery, native/provider and operational gates remain unresolved. Nothing in this audit authorizes deployment, migration, payment activation or publication.

## Fresh non-mutating production observations

At the timestamp recorded in [production-readonly.json](evidence/production-readonly.json), the management API identified `xbvenkfmesugrbjuufyw` as MedVeda, ACTIVE_HEALTHY, region ap-northeast-2. All SQL ran inside an explicit READ ONLY transaction with a bounded timeout and rollback; only schema/config allowlist and aggregate historical counts were returned. Private credentials stayed in memory. No deployed function endpoint was invoked.

- **66 ledger versions**, last `20261104110000`; eight repository migration files are absent from this ledger. `razorpay_entitlement_committed`, Notes-choice preparation and authorization recording RPCs are absent. Settlement returns void.
- Deployed `verify-payment` **v68**, `razorpay-webhook` **v50**, `create-order` **v23**, `get-note-url` **v20**. Seven downloaded bundle fingerprints are recorded. Verifier/webhook lack the new commitment-proof call structurally. Bundles differ from the reviewed source candidates; do not infer production has local fixes.
- 17 deployed functions listed. Choice/reporting/non-Play test candidates are not in this deployment inventory. Existing Play worker v3 is deployed, which does not mean its schedule is active.
- Safe app_config: `min_app_version=1.1.0`, `require_ucb_for_razorpay=false`. The latter is a legacy gating choice, **not a universal payment off switch** and not evidence of live activation.
- Aggregate history: **2 success purchases on non-paid orders**, **1 duplicate-success owner/product group**. Four active products priced INR 1, seven INR 2, two INR 5. No records/prices were modified.
- Active cron includes analytics/session purges and **release-wallet-credits**. `play-consume`, `play-reconcile`, `play-voids`, `play-catalog-sync` are inactive. This is job configuration, not execution/heartbeat/alert evidence. Quiescence planning must include the active financial wallet job.
- Financial RLS/policies/grants, indexes/constraints, trigger definitions, routine hashes/owners/config/ACLs are recorded. Current cloud backups, restore, Auth provider settings, Storage object bytes, provider credentials, webhook dashboard subscriptions, FCM, EAS signing and secret/config values remain UNKNOWN.

## Required area verdicts

Verdict is production release readiness, not whether a package compiles. PASS would require accepted evidence for the entire named area; no area receives an unqualified PASS solely from local tests.

# REMAINING WORK PRIORITY CHECKLIST

Audit date: 2026-11-11 UTC. Scope: current working tree, including pre-existing uncommitted changes. **Audit only; no deployment authorization.**

**Keep production sales disabled and preserve existing financial history until the mandatory gates below are accepted.** This is a remediation plan, not approval to perform changes.

## P0 — Critical production blockers

- [ ] Resolve targeted notification recipient isolation (AUD-002); verify database reads as recipient, foreign user, anonymous and admin.
- [ ] Resolve unknown refund/dispute acknowledgement (AUD-001) across complete verifier/webhook/reporting-worker closures. Preserve durable retry and fail-closed commitment semantics.
- [ ] Independently review/re-pin the corrected C wrapper and four-function upgrade in a new manifest/runbook. Original artifacts must stay immutable. Exclude isolated test installer and future ownership index unless separately reviewed.
- [ ] Confirm current deployment/ledger/owners/ACLs/RLS/indexes/triggers/config immediately before any future operation. Production currently lacks new proof RPC/handler path.
- [ ] Obtain current complete recovery evidence: application + managed Auth + Storage metadata and object bytes + config/secret recovery, tested compatible restore, RPO/RTO and provider reconciliation after restore.
- [ ] Privately reconcile two success-on-nonpaid purchases and one duplicate-success group against provider facts. Preserve history/access; approve any forward repair separately.
- [ ] Approve bounded operational window with operator, independent observer, incident owner, ingress and ALL financial worker quiescence. Include active `release-wallet-credits`; client Buy Now gates do not stop cron.
- [ ] Accept exact post-check and unknown-commit decision procedure before execution. No blind reruns or financial rollback.

## P1 — High-priority functional/security acceptance

- [ ] Repair duplicate import lint failures (AUD-005), admin forbidden status choices (AUD-004), analytics truncation (AUD-003), stale Play mock (AUD-008) and final-schema SQL fixtures (AUD-009) in separately authorized changes.
- [ ] Investigate referral SQL variation: first broad run passed, supported-socket rerun failed P10/H5 with `joined` values. Determine fixture/query-order contamination versus real referral-list defect; do not waive failed assertions.
- [ ] Rerun CI gates and relevant regressions with supported local fixtures; retain exact commands and failures as well as successful reruns.
- [ ] Deliver required A → B → atomic C → compatible Edge sequence only under separate authorization and accepted recovery/drift/drain gates. Validate ledger 66→69→73 and exact source/function/config contracts before resume.
- [ ] Accept hosted Auth (signup/email/password reset/MFA/token refresh/recovery) and private Storage authorization/object delivery with controlled accounts; no production test writes are implied by this report.
- [ ] Verify provider account ownership, KYC, correct APP_ENV/live mode, webhook URL/events/rotation/retry history and required server gates privately. Never publish credentials or use a real charge merely to infer configuration.
- [ ] For paid Play distribution, obtain completed India onboarding/per-app opt-in, active SKU/offer evidence, genuine native choice/token issuance, exact report/time/tax acceptance, Play payment/consumption/reversal acceptance and operational reporting deadline controls.
- [ ] Build exact current disabled-payment native release in an SDK-equipped environment; verify remote signing/versionCode/runtime, merged permissions, resolved Billing 8+ graph, R8 mapping, ABIs/16 KB alignment and privacy/config accuracy.
- [ ] Run real-device startup/login/course/search/Notes/PDF/download/offline/quiz/purchases/restart/network-loss/accessibility flows. Separately accept controlled payment/recovery/replay/reversal journeys before paid activation.

## P2 — Medium-priority bugs and operational improvements

- [ ] Review five moderate dependency findings with supported version constraints; avoid blind `npm audit fix --force` or Expo downgrade.
- [ ] Measure API/list behavior beyond one page for referrals/notifications/catalog diagnostics; review indexed query plans with realistic data.
- [ ] Establish queue-age/deadline/parked/capture-without-entitlement/compensation/void-cursor alerts, scheduler heartbeat, independent alert transport and operator response drills.

- [ ] Accept seven-day offline grace and reversal policy; verify no cross-account downloaded-content access and native process-death cleanup.
- [ ] Audit browser runtime/accessibility/responsiveness and measure web bundle loading/memory. Current web bundle export is about 5.1 MB; no performance SLA has been demonstrated.
- [ ] Verify admin content create/edit/publish/delete and small/large PDF upload propagate to consumer app under RLS without stale ownership/catalog presentation.
- [ ] Decide whether support needs server mail delivery; current handoff requires user's email app. Document website as marketing/auth surface unless commerce is intentionally added.

- [ ] Document the hosting source among root HTML, original website and generated website; remove ambiguity only through a reviewed change.
- [ ] Keep generated/native/local evidence distinct from versioned release artifacts, and identify complete working-tree release hashes.
- [ ] Improve action labels, status descriptions, empty/error messages and external-email handoff text; complete manual screen-reader/layout review.
- [ ] Consolidate obsolete comments/test paths and ensure all new safeguards/tests are explicitly exercised in CI.

## Safest order and completion rule

Preserve evidence/history → repair and test P0 code defects → accept full recovery → freeze/review exact release identity → resolve CI and required native/managed/provider acceptance → refresh production drift → separately authorize bounded controlled delivery with traffic/workers drained → validate exact contracts/history → separately approve resume/activation only after all required policy/provider/monitoring gates pass.

A successful synthetic test cannot replace managed restore or genuine provider/native acceptance. A healthy deployment cannot replace financial correctness. No production GO is justified until mandatory security, financial, recovery and deployment gates have complete accepted evidence.

# MedVeda Android production audit — 2026-11-10

## A. Executive Summary

**Decision: NO-GO for the requested production Android paid-Notes experience.** New purchases are deliberately disabled in the production configuration and existing inspected Android artifact. Enabling a flag alone would not produce a verified, compliant payment release.

Scope: `/home/ganeshp8545/AyurPulse/mobile-app/`, Android package `in.medveda.app`, and only the shared production backend resources needed by that app. Production identity was confirmed through the Management API: **MedVeda**, `xbvenkfmesugrbjuufyw`, `ACTIVE_HEALTHY`, region `ap-northeast-2`. Backend health is not a verdict on payment correctness.

Audit totals:

| Measure | Result |
|---|---|
| Android screens inventoried and source reviewed | **39**: 37 stack routes and 2 application gates |
| Feature/check items assessed | **80**, explicitly numbered in section I |
| Confirmed findings | **12**; includes intentional requirements mismatches, production data inconsistencies, and reproduced defects |
| Severity | **P0: 0; P1: 5; P2: 6; P3: 1** |
| Additional unresolved concerns | **10**, W01–W10; not counted as confirmed bugs |
| Existing mobile suites | **55 passed**, local only |
| Selected shared-backend suites | **18 passed**, local only |
| Additional deterministic checks | 5 behavioral reproductions and 1 contrast calculation |
| TypeScript / dependency consistency | Passed; no TypeScript diagnostics or top-level dependency errors |
| Native build / Android visual execution | Blocked; SDK paths absent; no build generated |
| Live payment settlement | **NOT VERIFIED** |

Highest priority evidence:

1. Production EAS profile sets the provider to `disabled`; runtime also prevents direct Razorpay checkout for `in.medveda.app`. Buy Now does not start a production Razorpay order.
2. Active production catalog prices are ₹1–₹5, rather than the expected ₹49/₹149/₹499/₹599. This is a confirmed discrepancy, not proof that anyone has authorized those expected prices as a replacement.
3. Deployed payment code differs materially from local source. The deployed settlement helper returns success after a void SQL RPC, although current live SQL can return normally after queuing compensation without granting access. The newer local commitment check is absent from deployment.
4. Deployed unsigned recovery does not authenticate or authorize the requester. Knowledge of an order UUID can trigger recovery/provider work and obtain payment-state information. No production exploit was attempted.
5. Production has **2 successful purchases attached to failed orders**, and **1 duplicate successful user/product group**. All 13 paid orders have successful purchases; that narrower check passes, but overall consistency fails.

Evidence boundaries: production reads verify the particular aggregates, configuration, policies, SQL definitions, and deployed source retrieved during this session. They do not verify a new financial transaction, merchant capture configuration, webhook delivery, native rendering, or a particular installed Play Store version. Local mocks are explicitly distinguished throughout. No customer identifiers, credentials, signatures, tokens, signed URLs, or private keys are included.

### Audit controls and baseline

- Only this report was written by this audit. No implementation edits, package installation, prebuild, build, deployment, payment endpoint invocation, Auth mutation, database mutation, or permission changes were performed.
- The workspace already contained extensive tracked modifications and untracked audit/backend/mobile files before the audit. They were preserved and are not attributed to this task.
- No staging environment files or credentials were loaded; no staging service was contacted. Other-environment literals encountered inside shared application configuration were inspected only to assess production isolation. Staging-target test suites were not executed.
- Database calls used the confirmed production project's `/database/query` Management API with `read_only: true`, explicit `BEGIN TRANSACTION READ ONLY`, a local statement timeout, SELECT queries, and COMMIT. Financial/customer data checks returned aggregate counts only. Deployed function bundles were inspected in memory, without saving copies.
- Network initially failed under sandbox DNS. Authorized read-only production requests succeeded with network escalation. No approval rejection occurred.
- `rg` is unavailable; bounded `find`, Python searches, and direct file reads were used instead.

## B. Razorpay Payment Audit

### Actual integration and runtime

There is no `react-native-razorpay` native SDK dependency. The retained integration uses Razorpay Checkout JavaScript in `react-native-webview`, with local HTML generated by `src/screens/payment/checkoutHtmlTemplate.ts`. Absence of a native Razorpay SDK is therefore not itself a defect.

`src/config/paymentProvider.ts:19` derives the effective provider from Expo extra, platform, application ID, and distribution safeguards. `eas.json:42–53` sets the production provider to `disabled`. Direct Android Razorpay is allowed only for a distinct isolated test package; it is not available to `in.medveda.app`. The test package is outside this audit's allowed application and was not run.

`usePurchase.buy` (`src/hooks/usePurchase.ts:17`) displays a purchases-unavailable alert when disabled. `UnlockOptionsPanel`, Store, and product cards show unavailable text or suppress Buy. `AppNavigator.tsx` conditionally excludes OrderSummary and PaymentCheckout when disabled. This is intentional disablement, not an unexplained dead button. It nevertheless fails the requested operational purchase business model.

### Actual execution-path ledger

Paths below are relative to `mobile-app/` unless explicitly prefixed `supabase/`. “Source” means traced, not production transaction-tested.

| Transition | File / function | Backend and records | Validation, error behavior, expected / observed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Login → session | `src/features/auth/authService.ts`, `signInWithIdentifier`; `LoginScreen.onLogin` | Supabase Auth; `login-with-mobile`; `profiles` | Email/password or normalized mobile/password; auth transport deadline; profile/course destination. Production Auth settings inspected; actual login writes blocked. |
| Session → course/year | `src/services/courseState.ts`, `resolvePostLoginRoute`, `saveCourseYear`; `SelectCourseYearScreen.performSave` | `profiles`, `courses`, `years`; `set_my_course_year` | Pair validation and server confirmation; stale year-response guards; errors shown. Local tests pass. |
| Course → subjects/chapters | repositories and `YearsScreen`, `SubjectsScreen`, `ChaptersScreen.openChapter` | `years`, `subjects`, `chapters` | Active/course filters and published-ancestry RLS. No orphan subject/chapter mappings found in aggregate checks. |
| Chapter → Notes/product | `NotesScreen`, `productsService.fetchBuyOptionsForChapter` | `notes`, `products`, `purchases` | Combines chapter/subject/year products and matching bundles; free preview is server flag. Network failure can masquerade as no options, MV010. |

Capture not tested live. Commitment-success mismatch MV003. |

Deployed helper does not distinguish those outcomes. |
| Purchase → Notes UI | `usePurchases.refresh`; `purchasesService.isChapterUnlocked`; `NotesScreen` | User's successful `purchases` | Chapter/subject/year/bundle matching. Older refresh can overwrite newer scopes, MV008. Notes receives an ownership snapshot in navigation params, W01. |
| Notes → PDF | `secureNotesService.getSecureNoteUrl:23`; deployed `get-note-url` v20 | `user_can_access_note_with_aal`; `notes`; private `notes` bucket | JWT identity, blocked profile/published ancestry/free preview or owned scope; 120-second URL. Bucket and live functions/policies verified; authenticated retrieval not exercised. |
| Restart/login → access restoration | secure session adapter, Splash; `fetchOwnedScopes`; My Purchases focus load | Durable `purchases`, not only local callback state | Design supports reauthentication/restart/reinstall restoration. **NOT VERIFIED on a device/account**. No new login created. |

### Production pricing

Aggregate read: `SELECT scope,product_type,active,price,count(*) FROM products GROUP BY 1,2,3,4`.

| Scope | Expected | Active actual production catalog | Count |
|---|---:|---|---:|
| Chapter | ₹49 | ₹1 × 2; ₹5 × 1 (`notes`) | 3 |
| Subject | ₹149 | ₹2 × 2 and ₹5 × 1 (`notes`); ₹2 × 3 (`other`) | 6 |
| Year | ₹499 | ₹1 × 2 (`notes`); ₹2 × 1 (`other`) | 3 |
| Bundle | ₹599 | ₹2 × 1 (`notes_bundle`) | 1 |

All 13 returned products were active. Client product queries filter scope/active, not `product_type`; mixed `other` catalog types therefore merit validation before re-enabling purchases. No catalog prices were changed. No assumption is made that these low prices are malicious or unintended rather than historical testing configuration.

### Failure, retry, cancellation and recovery

- Payment transport bounds request and body read to 35 seconds. Callback verification retries with 0/1.5/3-second delays. Recovery uses six attempts with 24 seconds of deliberate delay, in addition to network request time; it is not a strict 24-second total deadline.
- Checkout guards repeated callback handling, uses nonce/order/signature-shape checks, tracks settled/recovering state, intercepts Android back, handles WebView renderer loss, and recovers on resume. These pass local behavior suites; real UPI app switching and renderer termination remain unverified.
- `PaymentFailedScreen` represents ambiguous confirmation as pending and polls only while focused. Signed callbacks with provider-not-captured must remain pending; old deployed verdicts omit the newer `pending` field, so updated clients depend on their ambiguity fallback.
- `PaymentSuccessScreen:24–50` independently rechecks the current authenticated user, paid order, successful purchase and equal amount. Route parameters alone cannot manufacture success in this source. The inspected older artifact cannot be assumed to contain that updated screen.
- `recoverMyPendingPayments:306` reads the signed-in user's seven-day Razorpay history, limits to 10 rows, chooses up to 3 without purchase rows, and deduplicates concurrent recovery per user. It remains callable when new purchases are off. Calling verification can settle/write production records, so **no recovery endpoint was invoked**.
- Automatic completion after process death relies on a working webhook or later user recovery, not JavaScript callback survival. Production cron shows no active payment reconciliation worker job. Delivery/retry/backlog guarantees are not proven.
- Completed and refunded orders require authoritative checks. Local source has newer refunded/commitment safeguards; deployed verifier predates them. See MV003/MV004.

**LIVE_PAYMENT_SETTLEMENT: NOT VERIFIED.** No order was created and no real or test provider payment was executed against production.

## C. Notes Unlock Audit

The application's entitlement record is **`purchases` with `status='success'`**. There is no need to assume a separate `entitlements` table. `payments` records successful provider verification; `orders` contains immutable purchase/amount/scope snapshots. Scope is copied into purchase records through settlement SQL, rather than supplied by a client success message.

Live `user_can_access_note` enforces:

1. A non-null user and existing, non-blocked profile.
2. Active note and published course/year/subject/chapter ancestry.
3. Either a free preview in the student's course, permitted admin access, or a successful purchase **for that user and course** matching the exact chapter, subject, year, or bundle year membership.

`user_can_access_note_with_aal` adds the trusted administrator MFA path. These SECURITY DEFINER note checks are callable by service role only; they are not exposed to a mobile client for choosing arbitrary users.

The client uses the same broad scope types, but must not be trusted as the authorization boundary. A forged `unlocked` route can influence appearance; the server still checks entitlement before signing a remote PDF. Offline files are separately validated by `PdfViewerSession`, `mayOpenOfflineNote`, and the owner marker.

| Required invariant | Evidence / result |
|---|---|
| Only trusted verification unlocks | Source and production SQL ACLs support this; no client write policy found for purchases. Live financial execution NOT VERIFIED. |
| Client success alone cannot unlock | Local verdict/security/Notes tests pass; authoritative purchase read and server PDF check exist. |
| Failed or cancelled order never has access | **FAIL — production has 2 failed orders with successful purchases and successful payment rows.** History/root cause unresolved; do not call them unpaid without provider evidence. |
| Pending/authorized payment grants no access | Deployed helper explicitly requires provider `captured`; local tests only. No live pending scenario executed. |
| Duplicate callback/webhook idempotency | Unique provider payment reference; unique purchase order; SQL locking observed. No duplicate order entitlements or provider payment refs found. Real concurrent delivery unverified. |
| Repeated Buy cannot charge twice | Disabled production route cannot create a new mobile charge; local in-flight guard tested. Provider/order idempotency not proved by that fact. |
| Crash cannot lose verified purchase | Durable backend path exists; delivery and recovery execution NOT VERIFIED. |
| Interrupted callback recovery | Available in source; deployed unauthenticated recovery MV004; endpoint not invoked. |
| Logout/login persistence | Server purchase records survive client logout by design; device/user execution NOT VERIFIED. Offline copies intentionally deleted on logout. |
| Restart persistence | Encrypted persisted session plus server reads; native execution NOT VERIFIED. |
| Reinstall persistence | Server account entitlements should restore after reauthentication; local downloads/preferences do not. NOT VERIFIED on reinstall. |
| Cross-user protection | Live own-user RLS and note predicates verified. No cross-user record fetch/exploit was attempted. Recovery authorization exception MV004 does not itself transfer entitlement. |
| Chapter scope | Exact chapter + course predicate in live SQL; local scope tests. |
| Subject scope | Exact subject + course predicate; local scope tests. |
| Year scope | Exact year + course predicate; local scope tests. |
| Bundle scope | Only years in stored `year_ids` + course predicate; live definition and nonempty bundle catalog validated. |
| Refund/reversal policy | Source/SQL implement revocation/suspension, full refund and dispute transitions; partial refunds need explicit policy. No production refund invoked. Offline grace delays enforcement while disconnected. |
| Completed order recognized | 13 paid orders have successful purchases; success screen checks both. Global consistency still fails. |
| Messages reflect entitlement | Updated screen defends success; deployed verify response can falsely say unlocked, MV003. |

All 28 note metadata paths use historical public-style URLs, but each matched an existing object in the **private** Notes bucket. Those strings do not establish public access. No file was downloaded or signed URL generated. There are 22 preview notes and 6 non-preview notes; missing/empty paths: 0. The UI's old “first PDF” fallback is used only when preview flags are absent; current production has the flag. Exactly one preview per chapter was not established by merely counting previews.

Offline Notes are supported, not hypothetical. Their access grace is seven days since last successful verification; rollback-clock checks are present. Owner changes/signout attempt to wipe local copies; opening checks owner and current entitlement where online. A known 401/403 clears authorization and cancels the download. Other transient failures may use the grace period. This means offline revocation is bounded rather than immediate; document that intended policy. PDF refresh on resume and fresh-link retry are locally tested. Native PDF rendering, corrupt-file behavior on real hardware, download persistence, and screenshots/recording protection remain unverified.

### Constraints, RLS and function privileges

- Orders, purchases, payments, products, Notes, profiles, captures and provider events have RLS enabled. RLS is also enabled on the inspected course/content/quiz/notification/push/referral/wallet/analytics/config tables. Enabling RLS alone is not a full policy test.
- Orders, purchases and payments expose own-user SELECT or trusted admin SELECT. No ordinary client write policy appeared on these financial tables. Production `create_priced_order` and `settle_razorpay_payment` are service-role-only, SECURITY DEFINER with a fixed `search_path`.
- Settlement SQL takes a user advisory lock followed by order row lock. It checks snapshot completeness, amount arithmetic, stale/reused referral discount, existing owned product and reversal facts, then grants or queues compensation. No concurrency mutation was run.
- Unique indexes exist for provider order, provider payment, purchase order, Play token and external transaction identities. **The one-success-per-user/product partial unique index explicitly excludes one historical purchase ID.** The identifier is deliberately omitted; its exception and duplicate aggregate are sufficient evidence for MV006.
- Financial table FKs connect orders/products/users and purchases/orders/products/content. Auth deletion uses differing retained/null/cascade policies; no deletion was tested. Scope IDs can be nulled by referenced content deletion; settlement's snapshot checks are therefore important.
- Storage restrictions are **RESTRICTIVE** policies, combined with permissive admin/thumbnail policies. `note_quarantine_private` is not an all-storage public grant. `notes_service_reads_only` limits ordinary Notes object reads; Notes bucket `public=false` is confirmed.
- Products and Notes have restrictive published-ancestry policies. Product metadata can be read before purchase to allow offers; that does not grant paid PDFs. `has_content_access` allows course browsing or applicable historical purchase scope, not arbitrary cross-user ownership.
- Useful indexes exist on subject/chapter foreign keys, active Notes per chapter, course/year filters, order users, purchases scope IDs and provider identities. Current catalog is small. No production EXPLAIN ANALYZE, load benchmark or exhaustive index tuning was performed. Purchase reads by user/status lack an obvious dedicated general-purpose user/status index in the returned list; assess actual plans before adding one.
- User quiz submission is an authenticated SECURITY DEFINER RPC with `search_path=public, pg_temp`. Quiz question metadata is exposed through `get_public_quiz_tests` without trusted answer keys; server scoring persists to `verified_quiz_attempts`.

### Deployment/migration compatibility

Latest production migration version: **20261104110000**. The live history also includes **20261104100000**. The local November 8 Notes-choice and November 9 settlement recovery migrations are absent from the retrieved live history. `razorpay_entitlement_committed` is absent from live function discovery. `payment_jobs` and `ucb_external_refunds` exist; newer local choice/recovery tables were not all present under the inspected names.

| Deployed function | Version | Gateway JWT | Observed |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `create-order` | 23 | true | Active legacy endpoint; server identity and priced-order RPC observed |
| `verify-payment` | 68 | false | Active; old unsigned recovery and void-RPC success behavior |
| `razorpay-webhook` | 50 | false | Active; raw-body HMAC and shared older settlement helper observed |
| `get-note-url` | 20 | true | Active; entitlement RPC + 120-second signer |
| `get-video-url` | 20 | true | Active; main Android video opening uses stored YouTube URL directly |
| `login-with-mobile` | 18 | false | Active; actual login not invoked |
| `delete-account` | 19 | true | Active; destructive invocation prohibited |
| `play-payment-worker` | 3 | false | Active endpoint metadata; execution unverified |
| `create-order-play-choice` | — | — | Absent from returned deployed function list |

Production config reads found `require_ucb_for_razorpay=false`, `min_app_version=1.1.0`. Do not infer current Play enrollment from that database flag. Cron has active analytics cleanup, payment-session cleanup and wallet credit release; Play consume/reconcile/void/catalog jobs are disabled. No cron command/secrets were printed.

The deployed verifier and webhook source was extracted from function bundle source maps, not assumed from this repository. Deployed source line numbers cited later refer to those extracted files and can differ from local files. Downloading a deployed bundle is a read-only management action; payment verification/recovery endpoints were never called.

Shared interactions reviewed include BottomNav and SideDrawer destinations, route validation, CustomButton/input accessibility, cards, error/loading/empty states, confirmation dialogs, account/course cache keys, download ownership, notification routing, session lifecycle and update/maintenance gates. The `.web.tsx` PDF implementation and website/admin application functionality were excluded from Android functionality scoring.

Intentionally limited features: digital invoices are described as future work; contact submission is an email handoff; video playback is external YouTube/browser; notification reads/preferences are local; learning recency/streak is substantially local. Their absence as server features is not automatically a defect. No redesign was attempted.

## F. UI/UX Findings

Confirmed: disabled purchase messaging is consistent with production gating, but payment sales are unavailable (MV001). Store and unlock options confuse network errors with empty/unconfigured data (MV009/MV010). Purchase history ordering is wrong for PostgreSQL ISO timestamps (MV012). Active scope/price text uses white on accent at approximately **3.17:1** with `FONT.body=16`; MV011.

The contrast benchmark is 4.5:1 for ordinary text; large text has a separate 3:1 threshold. The reported colors/font are source values, not an Android screenshot measurement. See [W3C contrast guidance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/contrast-minimum.html). The unavailable badge remains pressable and opens an explanatory alert, so the active-style contrast remains relevant, although native screen-reader rendering is unverified.

Accessibility positives in source: shared CustomButton declares role/label/disabled state and a 55-unit minimum height; login field labels and password visibility button exist; list virtualization and keyboard resize are configured; safe-area wrappers protect left/right/bottom; study navigation uses native headers. These do not establish whole-app TalkBack compliance. Purchase price `Text onPress` controls lack a semantic button role and explicit disabled state; test with TalkBack before release.

**BLOCKED — ENVIRONMENT OR ACCESS:** visual inspection at 320dp/360dp widths, low-resolution phones, large font/display scaling, landscape/multi-window, Android system back across every route, keyboard overlap, real dialog focus, TalkBack traversal, color-adjustment settings and actual scroll overflow. `ChapterDetailsScreen` uses a non-scroll root while rendering all buy options, making clipping with four tiers/long titles a source-supported concern (W09), not a reproduced device defect. App config intentionally specifies dark appearance; no light-mode implementation was assumed missing.

## G. Stability and Performance

- Confirmed purchase-refresh race MV008 can cause stale lock/owned state. Store's request chain and Home's course-scoped loaders also lack consistent generation guards (W02). Onboarding, `useLoadResource`, search load, PDF and checkout contain more explicit cancellation/generation handling and relevant local tests pass.
- Ownership lookup failures are logged by `usePurchases` without an exposed error state. A new session can look unowned during transient errors; stale previously loaded scopes can remain after a failed refresh. Authoritative PDF checks mitigate remote access bypass but not confusing lock indicators.
- Auth transport aborts at 15 seconds including response body. Notes URL requests have a 10-second signal. Payment requests have a 35-second transport/body deadline. Not every catalog/notification/Supabase read has an explicit deadline; a stalled request can outlive a spinner expectation.
- `useLoadResource` ignores late results after route change/unmount, but generally does not abort server work. This is state cancellation, not transport cancellation. Error/empty/list suites cover several, not every, loader.
- Download manager and SQLite outbox support recovery, owner checks, resumable transfers, interruption cleanup and serialized sync. Production writes were not exercised. Existing sync test passes; retry exhaustion for non-quiz entities marks them synced after five failures, so it is not a guaranteed-delivery ledger for learning state.
- Offline quizzes intentionally retain answers and await server scoring; client score is not trusted. Existing tests cover editing, submissions and hook lifecycle. An unfinished quiz session is primarily in-memory; app kill during a test is not proven resumable.
- Native PDF uses `trustAllCerts=false`, `cache=false`, 45-second loading fallback, fresh URL retry, and foreground/auth invalidation. Local file validation has separate access checks. Existing lifecycle tests do not prove native PDFium reliability or memory use on low-RAM devices.
- Video opening does not gate payment: tries native YouTube, then HTTPS, then browser and exposes retry/copy actions. Production URL metadata exists; link reachability and playback licenses/content availability were not probed.
- Search lazily builds nine sources, paginates catalog scans at 250 rows, caches by user/course for 15 minutes, and caps visible section results. Live catalog size is small; actual large-catalog latency and memory remain unmeasured.
- Store fans out products per year and subject; indexed small-catalog queries are plausible but there is no measured performance PASS. Notifications and purchase history are unpaginated. No load traffic was generated.
- Global Supabase AppState listener is module-lived; per-view PDF/checkout/notification listeners mostly clean up. No profiler/leak run was available. Push registration is marked attempted per session even when acquisition fails, so resume/retry robustness needs device verification (W10).
- Secure session storage encrypts session data with a random AES key kept in SecureStore. A key write precedes encrypted payload persistence; interruption between them may invalidate the previous session (W03). Source secret-literal scan found no embedded service-role JWT, `sb_secret_` key or PEM private key; this limited signature scan is not proof that no possible secret exists.

## H. Android Release Readiness

### Current source configuration

Package is `in.medveda.app`; Expo/app/package version **1.2.2**; EAS production uses production environment/channel, AAB, remote version source and autoIncrement. Production/local environment URL matches `https://xbvenkfmesugrbjuufyw.supabase.co`. Actual remote EAS secret overrides and the currently Play-distributed installed artifact were not retrieved.

**Production configuration points to the correct backend. A blanket statement that production source/bundle can contain no other-environment references cannot be made:** shared `paymentProvider.ts`, `paymentService.ts` and configuration retain other-environment branches/literals. No such alternate project literal was detected in the inspected older AAB JavaScript or app config. No alternate environment was contacted or loaded.

Source declares Internet and notification permissions; generated local manifest includes VIBRATE and explicitly removes broad external storage/media and overlay permissions. Cleartext is disabled, backups disabled, orientation adaptive and keyboard mode resize. There is no direct manifest `com.android.vending.BILLING` permission in the inspected generated manifest.

Expo SDK 57.0.32 / React Native 0.86.3 / React 19.2.3 and installed dependencies satisfy the declared top-level dependency tree. `expo-iap@3.4.4` is still installed and referenced. Plugin inclusion is conditional on provider `play`; this is not complete removal of Billing source/dependencies. No package was installed to test compatibility.

Generated local `android/app/build.gradle` has **versionCode 1** and **debug signing for release**. That generated/ignored checkout is not authoritative proof that EAS uses debug signing: remote version/signing configuration can replace it. A raw local release from this folder must not be presumed upload-ready (W05). No APK/AAB was generated.

R8/minification and resource shrinking are enabled in app config and generated properties; plugin selects the optimizing ProGuard default; native keep rules are deliberately narrow. Local Android release selftest passes. Current source's native module compatibility under R8 is unverified without an SDK build/device run.

Local `google-services.json` and OTA signing certificate are absent; app config permits an EAS Firebase file override and conditional certificate. Thus local absence is a blocker to confirming push/OTA setup, not proof production lacks them. FCM strings exist in the old AAB's manifest; token acquisition/provider delivery remains unverified. The app scheme is `medveda`; reset landing is HTTPS and production Auth allows its website host. Direct scheme allowlist is absent, which need not break this HTTPS-landing design.

### Existing artifact inspected read-only

`mobile-app/build/closed-testing-1.2.2-13/MedVeda-1.2.2-13.aab`

- SHA-256: `eed4a3e9d6995c7bda2abfca5b40eb1f808db13e126b1fe6c97229d9955a2fac`.
- ZIP integrity: pass. `jarsigner -verify`: exit 0, `jar verified` found. This verifies archive signature integrity, not authorized Play upload certificate identity or Play acceptance.
- Embedded app version 1.2.2, Android package `in.medveda.app`, runtime `1.2.2-disabled`; provider `disabled`, standard distribution, production target.
- Embedded JavaScript contains the production project reference; inspected alternate-project literal absent. Credential values were not printed.
- R8 metadata exists. Billing dependency-group metadata, Billing mapped classes and manifest Billing permission were absent in this artifact. This narrower artifact result does not negate retained Billing dependency/code in the **current source**.
- Filename suggests build 13; manifest versionCode/target SDK were **not reliably decoded**, so the filename is not treated as authoritative versionCode proof.

**Native build: BLOCKED.** `ANDROID_HOME` and `ANDROID_SDK_ROOT` point to a nonexistent directory. Java 21 is available, but that is insufficient. Gradle/adb/emulator were not started because no usable SDK/device was established, and builds/caches would violate this audit's sole-write restriction. No signing or publishing performed.

### Google Play payment policy

Digital Notes/PDF access is an in-app digital product; replacing Billing with Razorpay does not itself establish compliance. Official India documentation describes enrollment, additional terms and alternative billing **alongside** Play billing for eligible users, with required UX, support and transaction obligations. The in-app integration guide requires the user-choice APIs/flow, external choice token persistence, and backend transaction reporting within 24 hours of completion. [Google India program requirements](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/13306652?hl=en), [Android alternative billing integration](https://developer.android.com/google/play/billing/alternative/alternative-billing-with-user-choice-in-app).

No Play Console enrollment, per-app/per-market approval, submitted billing declaration, accepted terms, current regional eligibility, merchant review, or end-to-end reporting receipts were available. Build flags for enrollment/test acceptance default false. Production `require_ucb_for_razorpay=false` and disabled worker schedules do not evidence policy compliance. The retained alternative flow's newer endpoint is absent from production.

Separate verdicts: **technical scaffolding exists; requested production payment functionality FAILS; Google Play payment-policy readiness NOT VERIFIED**. No payment flow was enabled. Obtain actual Console evidence and validate the exact program applicable at release, rather than assuming one enrollment covers every market/distribution.

## I. Test Execution Evidence

### Status vocabulary

- **PASS — PRODUCTION VERIFIED:** a specified production read returned supporting evidence; never a blanket app PASS.
- **PASS — LOCAL TEST ONLY:** source/static/harness/fixture behavior; no live payment implication.
- **FAIL — REPRODUCED:** an observed data/config mismatch or deterministic reproduction; evidence level is stated.
- **WARNING — POTENTIAL ISSUE:** source concern without full reproduction.
- **BLOCKED — ENVIRONMENT OR ACCESS:** requires writes, device/SDK, account access or missing authority.
- **NOT VERIFIED — INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE:** plausible path without adequate evidence.

### Commands and safeguards

Executed read-only discovery with `find`, `cat`, bounded `sed`, Python file searches, `git status --short`. No global recursive website/admin review was performed. A broad initial parent instruction-file search produced no useful result; later searches were scoped.

From `mobile-app/`:

```sh
./node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit --incremental false
node node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc --noEmit --incremental false
npm ls --depth=0 --ignore-scripts
```

TypeScript exited 0 without compiler diagnostics. The shell printed a user-scope bus warning; it was not a TypeScript failure. Dependency command exited 0 with no unmet/invalid dependencies. No lint package is installed in mobile `.bin`, and no mobile lint script is declared; ESLint **BLOCKED**, no installation attempted.

Suites were run through a Python subprocess loop executing `node mobile-app/scripts/<name>` from repository root, timeout 75 seconds per suite, CI mode, no dotenv auto-load. Before running, scripts were inspected for filesystem/network/child-process side effects. Staging-target, isolated-package suites, wrappers, and smoke/build scripts that write files were excluded. `check-staging-build.cjs` is a validation helper imported by certain production config tests; it was not used to load or contact staging.

One exploratory aggregate referred to nonexistent `quiz_attempts` and returned HTTP 400/undefined relation; it was corrected using discovered `verified_quiz_attempts` and `quiz_scores`. This was a read-only query failure, not an application test failure. One oversized deployed-bundle text scan was interrupted and replaced with bounded source-map extraction; no endpoint was mutated. Production queries were independently read-only transactions, not a single cross-query snapshot, so concurrent changes remain possible.

### Production query evidence sufficient to reproduce key findings

All must remain inside a transaction with READ ONLY, timeout and the exact confirmed production project. Queries below return no customer identifiers:

```sql
BEGIN TRANSACTION READ ONLY;
SET LOCAL statement_timeout = '15s';

SELECT scope, product_type, active, price, count(*)
FROM public.products GROUP BY 1,2,3,4 ORDER BY 1,4;

SELECT o.status, count(*)
FROM public.purchases p JOIN public.orders o ON o.id=p.order_id
WHERE p.status='success' AND o.status<>'paid' GROUP BY o.status;

SELECT count(*) AS paid_without_purchase
FROM public.orders o WHERE o.status='paid'
AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.purchases p
                WHERE p.order_id=o.id AND p.status='success');

SELECT count(*) AS duplicate_user_product_groups FROM (
  SELECT user_id, product_id FROM public.purchases
  WHERE status='success' GROUP BY 1,2 HAVING count(*)>1
) d;

SELECT count(*) AS snapshot_mismatches
FROM public.purchases p JOIN public.orders o ON o.id=p.order_id
WHERE p.status='success' AND
ROW(p.scope,p.chapter_id,p.subject_id,p.year_id,p.year_ids,p.course_id,p.amount)
IS DISTINCT FROM
ROW(o.scope,o.chapter_id,o.subject_id,o.year_id,o.year_ids,o.course_id,o.amount);

SELECT id, public FROM storage.buckets WHERE id='notes';
SELECT version FROM supabase_migrations.schema_migrations
ORDER BY version DESC LIMIT 12;
SELECT jobname, active FROM cron.job;
COMMIT;
```

Additional inspections selected schema columns, constraint/index definitions, RLS modes/predicates, specific function ACLs/definitions and selected non-secret app/Auth settings. No Auth rows, payment identifiers or raw provider-event payloads were returned in the report.

### Detailed issue records

**MV001 — P1 — Buy Now unavailable in production.** Files/functions: `mobile-app/eas.json:42–53`, `mobile-app/src/config/paymentProvider.ts:19–23`, `usePurchase.buy` and `AppNavigator` conditional registration. Safe reproduction: evaluate production config (release-disabled suite), inspect effective provider, select an unowned product in local harness. Expected: an approved production order/checkout flow; actual: unavailable alert/hidden buy and omitted checkout routes. Root cause: deliberate production-off gate and application-ID restriction. Student impact: cannot buy paid Notes; no money is moved by this blocked route. Fix: complete policy/server/catalog requirements before a narrowly scoped enablement. Regressions: in-package production flow, off-state UI, wrong package/environment rejection, existing purchase access with gate off. Deployment risk: **high**, enabling financial functionality. Status: confirmed requirement mismatch, not accidental logic regression.

**MV002 — P1 — Price discrepancy.** Query evidence in B/D/I; mobile display functions `getProductsByChapter/Subject/Year`, `productsService.fetchBuyOptionsForChapter`, `OrderSummary.loadQuote`. Source locations `mobile-app/src/repositories/productsRepository.ts:40` onward and `mobile-app/src/screens/payment/OrderSummaryScreen.tsx:67`. Safe reproduction: grouped read shown in I. Expected requested tiers; actual low active catalog values. Root cause: catalog configuration/history unknown. Impact: potentially unexpected charges if sales re-enabled, not a confirmed live over/undercharge from this audit. Fix: product-level scope/price approval, currency/paise validation and controlled future catalog update. Regressions: all tiers, discounts/wallet arithmetic, UI/server exact amounts, mixed product types. Deployment/data risk: **high**; preserve existing order snapshots. Status: production discrepancy confirmed; business intent unverified.

**MV003 — P1 — False entitlement success across deployed code and live SQL.** Deployed `supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts`, `settleCapturedPayment` original source lines approximately 57–71; deployed verifier `settle` lines 16–38. Live SQL corresponds to `supabase/migrations/20261104100000_razorpay_snapshot_discount_retry.sql:417–490`. Local newer helper at `supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts:124` checks a commitment RPC missing live. Safe reproduction: in-memory deployed source + valid synthetic provider facts + successful void RPC returns `ok:true`; inspect live SQL's `compensation_pending`/reversal return paths. Expected: success only for a matching committed active entitlement. Actual: a non-error void return is treated as success; compensation can be a non-error return. Root cause: incompatible deployed helper and SQL outcome contract. Impact: captured customer may receive “unlocked” while denied access; updated source's success screen limits false presentation but older clients/deployed API remain affected. Not proof a particular live customer experienced it. Fix: compatible committed-outcome return/check across callback/webhook/recovery and SQL; never patch only the caller to invoke an absent RPC. Regressions: stale discount, already-owned, refund-before-capture, compensated replay, wallet shortfall, duplicate webhook, crash after SQL commit. Deployment risk: **high**; coordinated backend rollout and controlled acceptance required.

**MV004 — P1 — Recovery lacks authentication/ownership.** Deployed `supabase/functions/verify-payment/index.ts:51–82`, v68, `Deno.serve` recovery branch; gateway `verify_jwt=false`. Local replacement `verify-payment/index.ts:78` onward already contains identity checks, but is not deployed. Safe reproduction: read deployed branch: accepts UUID and `recover:true`, rate-limits, service-role selects order by ID without user predicate, fetches provider capture then settles. Expected: unsigned recovery requires verified JWT matching order owner. Actual: no caller authentication; knowledge of UUID is sufficient. Root cause: retained legacy public recovery contract. Impact: state disclosure and externally triggered financial recovery/API work; entitlement still belongs to stored user. No arbitrary-user entitlement or forged capture is established. Fix: JWT ownership for recovery, maintain trusted HMAC callback path, safe old-client messaging. Regressions: no JWT, expired JWT, wrong user, deleted user, signed callback without JWT, rate-limit behavior. Deployment risk: **medium/high**, old-client recovery compatibility. Status: confirmed deployed missing check; unauthorized production invocation BLOCKED.

**MV005 — P1 — Two failed orders remain successfully entitled.** Production `orders`/`purchases`/`payments`; mobile readers `purchasesService.fetchOwnedScopes`/`fetchMyPurchases`, `PaymentSuccessScreen` at `mobile-app/src/screens/payment/PaymentSuccessScreen.tsx:38–45`. Safe reproduction: aggregate join in I returns two `failed` orders with `success` purchases; both also have matching successful payment rows. Expected: consistent authoritative order/payment/entitlement state. Actual: source ownership reader accepts them while success screen rejects non-paid order. Root cause: historical settlement/status overwrite or repair sequence unknown. Impact: confusing restoration, refund and support outcomes; financial truth cannot be inferred from `failed`. Fix: private provider-side reconciliation and approved record repair with immutable audit log; do not revoke legitimate paid access just to force counts to match. Regressions: terminal status monotonicity, late failures after capture, reconciliation replay. Risk: **high** for data repair. Status: production consistency failure confirmed; causation unverified.

**MV006 — P2 — Duplicate successful user/product group.** Production `purchases_one_success_per_product` definition excludes one historical row; `purchases` aggregate finds one duplicate group. Mobile `isProductOwned` in `mobile-app/src/services/purchasesService.ts:84` onward treats either as owned. Expected: documented unique/canonical successful ownership; actual: duplicate exception remains. Root cause: explicit historical index exception, not proven webhook race. Impact: duplicate history/amount interpretation, future reconciliation and revocation ambiguity; no duplicate order/provider-reference rows found. Fix: investigate whether both were truly paid, define canonical/refund/retention handling, then remove exception only with approved migration. Regression: simultaneous purchases, duplicate provider callback, unique ownership and refund of one duplicate. Risk: **high** for financial record changes. Status: production verified duplicate; no records modified.

**MV007 — P2 — Complete Billing removal claim fails.** `mobile-app/package.json:33`, `src/services/playBillingService.ts:324–352`, `paymentProvider.ts:19–28`, `app.config.js` conditional `expo-iap` plugin. Safe reproduction: installed dependency tree and source imports/config branches. Expected user-described removal; actual: retained Billing runtime/package and routes. Root cause: support for gated future Play/user-choice flow. Impact: architecture/release claim mismatch; presence alone is not proof of an active charge or policy violation. Fix: explicitly choose the compliant distribution strategy before removing/retaining native code. Regression: package/manifest/DEX/dependency scan and OTA/native runtime compatibility. Risk: **high** if native module removal changes runtime or breaks approved billing. Status: confirmed source mismatch; inspected old AAB had zero Billing mapped classes/dependency metadata/permission.

**MV008 — P2 — Purchase response race.** `mobile-app/src/hooks/usePurchases.ts:21–29`, `refresh`. Safe reproduction: let initial fetch A wait; resolve newer refresh B with a valid new scope; then resolve A with old scopes. Actual last state becomes A. Expected: latest response for current account wins. Root cause: no sequence/user/unmount guard. Impact: paid students can see locked state; stale grants may look owned, although server PDF authorization remains decisive. Fix: request generation + account guard, proper refresh loading/error handling. Regression: out-of-order refresh, owner switch, unmount, failed refresh after successful purchase/refund. Risk: **low**, UI state only, but test access behavior. Status: actual-hook deterministic reproduction.

**MV009 — P2 — Store silently converts network errors to no catalog.** `mobile-app/src/screens/store/StoreScreen.tsx:59–94,167`, load effect. Safe reproduction: rejected `fetchYears`; render after microtask. Expected connection error + retry; actual “No Academic Years Found” and no `onRefresh`. Root cause: catch only logs, then loading ends with empty blocks. Impact: stranded student and misleading availability; no direct financial mutation. Fix: error state/retry and generation guard, retain prior data explicitly. Regression: network failure at every fanout stage, successful retry, course changes while pending. Risk: **low**. Status: rendered local fixture reproduction.

**MV010 — P2 — Product lookup failure reported as no configured options.** `mobile-app/src/components/UnlockOptionsPanel.tsx:47–57,80–81`, load effect. Safe reproduction: reject product service and render. Expected error/retry, actual “No purchase options set up for this chapter yet.” Root cause: no separate error state. Impact: purchase discovery blocked/misleading, including when payment eventually enabled; no charge initiated. Fix: separate error/empty and retry. Regression: each of four parallel source failures, empty valid catalog, successful retry and changed chapter. Risk: **low**. Status: rendered local fixture reproduction.

**MV011 — P2 — Low contrast on action text.** `mobile-app/src/components/UnlockOptionsPanel.tsx:149–155`, `styles.price`; colors in `src/theme/colors.ts`; size in `src/theme/typography.ts:16`. Safe reproduction: compute relative luminance for `#FFFFFF` on `#E2703D`: about 3.17:1 at normal 16-unit body size. Expected 4.5:1 benchmark for normal active text; actual lower. Root cause: foreground/background pairing. Impact: readability/accessibility, no payment/data loss. Fix: minimal darker background or high-contrast foreground; validate current button semantics and unavailable interaction. Regression: contrast calculation and native large-font/TalkBack/small-screen check. Risk: **low** style change. Status: source-calculated defect; native visual test blocked.

**MV012 — P3 — History date ordering subtracts strings.** `mobile-app/src/services/purchasesService.ts:43,49`, `fetchMyPurchases`. Safe reproduction: repository fixture returns older then newer PostgreSQL `createdAt` ISO strings. Expected newest first; actual comparison yields NaN and original order is preserved. Root cause: Firestore-style numeric timestamp assumption; repository has no explicit creation ordering. Impact: confusing payment history; no entitlement/charge mutation. Fix: normalize valid ISO dates to epoch (with invalid-date fallback) or order the server query explicitly. Regression: ISO/numeric/legacy/missing dates. Risk: **low**. Status: actual-service fixture reproduction.

## L. Safe Fix Plan — recommendations only, not implemented

1. **Preserve the current off gate while resolving payment/security defects.** Establish the exact intended release/source/artifact and allowed billing program. Keep recovery available to legitimate owners; do not disable all verification as a shortcut.
2. **Fix deployed recovery authentication and settlement outcome together with schema compatibility.** Prepare reviewable minimal changes requiring JWT ownership for unsigned recovery, immutable provider-order mapping, and a committed matching purchase/payment result before returning success. Preserve valid signed callbacks. Plan older-client behavior. Local missing commitment RPC means deploy ordering must be explicit; never deploy just a helper calling absent SQL.
3. **Prove the revised contracts locally before any proposed deployment.** Add meaningful cases for grant, compensation-only normal SQL return, refunded replay, ownership, late callback/webhook race, stale/expired discount, wallet shortfall, duplicate capture and process-loss recovery. This audit did not add or rewrite tests.
4. **Reconcile the two failed/success order combinations and duplicate user/product group.** A privileged operator should obtain provider evidence privately and prepare narrowly scoped, idempotent repair statements with before/after aggregate assertions, backup and review. Do not revoke paid users or delete records to make counts agree. Production repair requires explicit authorization.
5. **Approve the product catalog.** Review exact course/chapter/subject/year/bundle memberships, `product_type`, intended ₹49/₹149/₹499/₹599 tiers, discounts and wallet rules. Prepare a catalog change proposal; preserve historical purchase snapshots. Actual catalog writes require authorization.
6. **Restore trustworthy Notes ownership UI.** Add generation/current-user guards to `usePurchases`, refresh the Notes screen from authoritative scopes when focus/account changes, expose purchase-load errors and preserve server authorization as the decisive gate. Verify all four scope types and offline revocation grace.
7. **Repair core error states.** Add Store/product-options error/retry states, cancellation of obsolete course results and nonmisleading stale-data display. Normalize history dates. Evaluate empty selectable courses with the product owner rather than inventing a content requirement.
8. **Make minimal accessibility corrections.** Improve action contrast/semantics and ensure scrolling for content that can exceed a small screen. Validate actual TalkBack, large text, Android back, keyboard and landscape on approved hardware.
9. **Prepare release evidence without publishing.** Provision Android SDK/device and approved Firebase/signing/OTA settings; check target SDK/versionCode/native compatibility on the exact artifact. Any fresh build/sign/upload needs separate authorization under the user's restrictions. Do not mistake source TypeScript tests or an old artifact for a new release check.
10. **Complete payment-program evidence and controlled acceptance.** Obtain relevant Play Console enrollment/approval and reporting-worker evidence, validate regional choice behavior and reporting/refund obligations. Any live/provider transaction or production recovery that can write remains separately authorized; no financial smoke test is implied by this plan.
11. **Only then propose a concrete rollout.** Present exact reviewed migration/function/app changes, deployment order, compatibility plan, rollback/compensation handling, aggregate monitors and acceptance evidence for user approval. Do not automatically enable checkout or deploy.

Fix sequence follows payment/security → entitlement integrity → database/catalog correctness → core functionality → UI → noncritical efficiency. No staging project is needed or authorized for this plan; use isolated deterministic local tests unless the user separately changes the scope.

## M. Final Production Decision

**NO-GO for the complete requested production payment-enabled Android release.** This is not a conclusion that every free-learning screen is broken. It is driven by confirmed disabled checkout, production price discrepancy, deployed settlement/auth gaps and contradictory financial state, together with missing native and policy acceptance evidence.

The inspected backend contains the core ingredients for trusted, scoped Notes access: service-only settlement, durable user purchases, server-side scope checks and private signed PDFs. Those are meaningful production read findings. They do not satisfy the requested financial lifecycle proof or erase deployment drift.

Final scope/accounting: **39 screens; 80 feature/check items; 12 confirmed findings (0 P0, 5 P1, 6 P2, 1 P3); 10 unresolved concerns.** Razorpay is not operational for new purchases in `in.medveda.app`; Buy Now is disabled/unavailable. Payment-to-Notes settlement and login/restart/reinstall restoration are **NOT VERIFIED**. Database consistency **FAILS** the specified order/status and unique-ownership predicates. Billing is **not completely removed from current source**, although the inspected older AAB lacked Billing artifact indicators. Release payment-policy compliance is **NOT VERIFIED**.

`docs/audit/MEDVEDA_MOBILE_PRODUCTION_FULL_AUDIT_2026-11-10.md`


SOURCE: docs/audit/MEDVEDA_MV003_LOCAL_FIX_2026-11-10.md
# MV003 — local entitlement commitment fix — 2026-11-10

Status: **FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED**. Production remains **NOT FIXED / NOT VERIFIED** by this patch: nothing was deployed. Android payment processing and Buy Now remain disabled.

## Scope and production contract evidence

Read the complete production audit and the existing November 4 Razorpay and November 9 billing analyses. No separate MV003/MV004 analysis file was present. Existing extensive uncommitted work was retained. Only the six files listed below were changed by this task.

Independently inspected only production project `xbvenkfmesugrbjuufyw` through the Management API database query endpoint. The request used `read_only: true`, `BEGIN TRANSACTION READ ONLY`, `SET LOCAL statement_timeout='15s'`, schema/function SELECTs and `ROLLBACK`. No customer/financial rows, credentials or provider endpoints were requested. The initial sandbox DNS failure was followed by an authorized network escalation; no approval rejection occurred.

Observed latest migration: `20261104110000`. Live `settle_razorpay_payment(text,text,text,bigint,timestamptz)` returns `void`; `razorpay_entitlement_committed` is absent. Live settlement body exactly equals the retained `20261104100000_razorpay_snapshot_discount_retry.sql` body. Its trimmed UTF-8 SHA-256 is `bf2751af97cf599d2ca9ed204572c2314c6e3d9162135adc633cf330afbdf3d3`.

The observed columns of `orders`, `payments`, `purchases`, `payment_captures`, `payment_compensations` and `provider_events` contain every column used by the new RPC. In particular, it does **not** use `blocks_entitlement`, Notes-choice tables, or any other November 8/9 additions. The migration changes one function and its execution grants; it contains no financial DML, table changes, settlement replacement, access-reader change or historical repair.

## Behavior

The RPC requires a paid Razorpay order, matching successful Razorpay payment and successful purchase, exact owner/product/amount/provider-order/payment identities, and the complete matching immutable course/scope snapshot. Chapter, subject, year and bundle snapshots are validated. It uses the order snapshot rather than today's mutable catalog.

Existing capture rows must match order/provider-order/amount and cannot be in compensation/reversal states. Any compensation row blocks proof, including parked/refunded rows. Pending or parked refund/dispute reversal facts block proof conservatively. Applied reversal handlers' durable purchase/order/capture states determine current access, allowing applied partial refunds and resolved disputes to retain legitimate access.

The RPC is `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER`, uses fixed `search_path=pg_catalog,public,pg_temp`, qualifies all application tables, contains no dynamic SQL, revokes PUBLIC/anon/authenticated execution, and grants execution to service_role. Migration execution must use the trusted production migration owner.

The helper checks commitment before replaying settlement and again before success. A granted marker or a normal void return alone never proves access. Missing RPC, SQL/transport error, missing purchase and mismatches fail closed with retryable responses. Compensation/reversal remains terminal for the callback; the webhook acknowledges durable terminal outcomes without claiming entitlement. Retryable fulfillment failures remain non-2xx for webhook retry.

Already committed historical purchases do not require an older capture marker. They skip settlement replay, preventing an expired/missing old discount reservation from turning a legitimate purchase into compensation. For the newly authenticated capture request only, the helper conditionally completes a `captured` marker and its captured-event inbox entry; it does not rewrite historical orders, payments, purchases or balances. The marker update cannot overwrite a concurrent compensation/reversal state.

Provider HMAC, captured-payment, order, amount and INR checks remain. Signed callbacks still work without JWT. Existing unsigned recovery ownership checks remain. Handler source and gateway settings were not changed.

## Exact changed files

1. `supabase/migrations/20261110100000_mv003_razorpay_entitlement_commitment.sql` — new standalone additive RPC.
2. `supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts` — commitment proof, safe historical replay and capture-delivery completion.
3. `supabase/tests/mv003-entitlement-selftest.cjs` — actual helper/callback/webhook/HMAC with real local SQL and synthetic provider transport.
4. `supabase/tests/run-mv003-local.py` — isolated production-baseline build, repeatability/data fingerprints, tests and database cleanup.
5. `supabase/tests/settle-timestamp-selftest.cjs` — one fixture correction: an explicitly uncommitted capture uses `committed=false`; all earlier user edits retained.
6. `docs/audit/MEDVEDA_MV003_LOCAL_FIX_2026-11-10.md` — this report.

## Exact verification commands and results

From the repository root, with a disposable PostgreSQL 17 cluster on the explicit `/tmp/mv003` Unix socket, port 55443:

```sh
PGHOST=/tmp/mv003 PGPORT=55443 PGUSER=postgres python3 supabase/tests/run-mv003-local.py
node supabase/tests/settle-timestamp-selftest.cjs
node supabase/tests/recovery-selftest.cjs
node mobile-app/scripts/notes-entitlement-selftest.cjs
node mobile-app/scripts/payment-verdict-selftest.cjs
node mobile-app/scripts/release-payments-disabled-selftest.cjs
git diff --check
```

All commands pass. The runner builds only retained migrations through `20261104110000`, plus the new MV003 migration. It applies the new migration twice with unchanged pre-existing order/payment/purchase/product/wallet/referral fingerprints. It then runs the actual-handler integration suite and `supabase/tests/razorpay_snapshot_retry_checks.sql`. It drops its unique scratch database in `finally`. It forwards only PATH and explicit local PG connection settings; no production credentials enter tests. Provider HTTP, identity verification transport, rate limits and Supabase client transport are synthetic; settlement and commitment SQL, handler branches and HMAC code are real. Tests load no staging environment and make zero real HTTP/provider requests.

All requested deterministic local cases pass:

| Required scenario | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Captured payment with committed entitlement | Actual SQL grant and callback success; all four scope types. |
| Missing purchase | RPC false; actual callback/webhook retry even with granted marker and non-error void RPC. |
| Ownership mismatch | Purchase/payment owner proof denial and callback/webhook denial. |
| Product/course/scope/amount mismatches | SQL snapshot and payment identity mutation cases; actual callback/webhook failures. |
| Compensation-only normal SQL return | Actual account-deleted compensation path; callback never unlocks; webhook acknowledges durable compensation. |
| Missing commitment RPC | Actual SQL function temporarily renamed locally; callback/webhook fail closed; transport PGRST202 also tested. |
| SQL error during proof | Actual throwing SQL function and XX000 transport failure; retryable failures. |
| Duplicate callback/webhook | One purchase; repeated signed deliveries succeed idempotently; captured-event inbox marked applied. |
| Concurrent settlement | Two independent PostgreSQL sessions; one durable purchase and matching commitment. |
| Refund/reversal | Real full-refund SQL and replay; all four blocking capture states; all compensation states; pending/parked reversal facts. Retained SQL also checks partial refunds. |
| Historical purchase without capture marker | Proof remains true; signed callback skips settlement despite an expired/missing historical discount reservation. |
| Interrupted settlement | Capture-only owner recovery; interruption after SQL commit and retry without duplicate purchase. |
| Valid signed callback without JWT | Actual HMAC implementation and callback handler return success. |
| Invalid signature | Actual HMAC rejection before provider lookup or settlement. |

Local required tests: **none blocked**. Live/provider/device acceptance is **NOT VERIFIED**, outside this task's authorization. Intermediate test runs caught synthetic fixture constraint violations and a changing duplicate webhook body; fixtures were corrected and the final passing run supersedes them. No production operation was used to debug tests.

## Remaining risk and deployment handoff

Deployment is still required. Review and apply only the standalone MV003 migration first, verify its service-only ACL and schema-cache visibility, then roll out the matching shared-helper delta in both callback and webhook bundles. Do not run a blanket migration push or deploy the entire dirty workspace: the pre-existing November 8/9 local reporting/refund/choice code includes other undeployed dependencies and needs separate deployment review. This patch introduces none of those dependencies. Preserve the existing gateway configuration that permits trusted HMAC callbacks without user JWT. Missing proof during rollout fails closed and causes retry rather than false unlock.

The production-compatible RPC intentionally treats unclassified pending/parked reversals conservatively. Resolution of those facts is necessary before a fresh verification can claim success; existing Notes access readers are unchanged. It does not repair or revoke the audit's historical inconsistent purchases. Real capture/webhook delivery, scheduled retry coverage and Android UPI/device behavior still need separately authorized acceptance after a reviewed deployment. A successful proof is a database snapshot; subsequent refunds/disputes can revoke access normally.

# MV004 — payment recovery authorization — 2026-11-10

| Scope | Status |
| --- | --- |
| MV003 LOCAL | **FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED** |
| MV003 PRODUCTION | **NOT FIXED** |
| MV004 LOCAL | **FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED** |
| MV004 PRODUCTION | **NOT FIXED** |

Production statuses reflect the supplied production audit and MV003 report. No deployment or new production verification was performed. Both fixes remain local. New purchases and Buy Now remain disabled.

## Root cause and preserved work

Read both requested reports and inspected Git status before editing. The workspace already contained extensive tracked and untracked changes. Preserved those changes, including the pre-existing verifier parsing/refund handling, Android purchase gates, and all MV003 implementation files.

The audited deployed verifier allowed unsigned `recover:true` requests based on an order UUID alone. It could query Razorpay and invoke settlement without authenticating or checking ownership. The pre-existing local version used Supabase Auth but selected the order by ID alone, checked ownership afterward, and disclosed existence through different 403/404 responses. Therefore, the presence of a local ownership check was insufficient evidence of a complete fix.

Android already sent a cached access token. It lacked an explicit bounded 401 refresh path and could silently retry authentication failures. Recovery results were not consistently pinned to the initiating account. These behaviors could leave users without sign-in guidance or navigate to success after an account change.

## Implementation and authorization evidence

`verify-payment/index.ts` retains the mixed callback/recovery endpoint and the existing `verify_jwt=false` gateway configuration. Unsigned recovery requires a syntactically valid Bearer header and calls the existing `getUserFromAuthHeader`, which delegates to `supabaseAdmin.auth.getUser(token)`. It does not decode claims as proof or accept a client-provided user ID. Auth rejection or transport exceptions fail closed with HTTP 401 and `code: AUTH_REQUIRED`.

After authentication and rate limiting, the recovery query applies **both** `.eq("id", internalOrderId)` and `.eq("user_id", recoveryUser.id)` before `maybeSingle()`. An absent row produces the same HTTP 404 JSON for wrong-owner and nonexistent orders. Database failures produce a generic retryable 503. No returned order/payment/provider/owner details are included in these denials. The provider lookup and shared settlement invocation occur only after a matching row is returned. Invalid UUIDs return 400 without database/provider/settlement calls.

The existing IP and order limits, thresholds and recovery fail-closed behavior remain. Recovery order buckets now include the verified user ID, preventing another authenticated user from consuming an owner's order bucket by guessing its UUID. The IP bucket still applies. Missing/invalid authentication does not reach the order limiter or order query; Supabase Auth retains its own protections.

Signed callbacks still work without JWT. Their required fields and actual HMAC are verified before the order lookup, preventing an unsigned caller from probing order existence by omitting `recover:true`. The stored provider-order ID must match before invoking the unchanged MV003 helper. Raw-body webhook HMAC logic is unchanged. Provider capture status, payment/order identity, amount/currency checks and committed-entitlement proof remain in the retained shared helper.

The Android recovery service sends the session access token only to the fixed configured verification endpoint. It validates that the session and synchronous current account match the initiating account before requests, after responses, and after refresh. A 401 permits one `refreshSession()` and one authenticated retry within that recovery call. Missing session, refresh error/exception/timeout, changed account, invalid refreshed session or a second 401 returns `{success:false,pending:true,authRequired:true}` with sign-in and do-not-pay-again guidance. The outer recovery loop stops on that result; it also stops on explicit terminal verdicts. Network-only exhaustion remains pending.

Payment requests retain their 35-second abort deadline including body reads. Waiting for session/refresh is bounded to 35 seconds, complementing the existing 15-second Auth fetch abort deadline. A late session/refresh result cannot continue the timed-out recovery. Razorpay's retained 15-second network deadlines remain unchanged.

`PaymentFailedScreen` keeps authentication trouble in the pending view, displays sign-in guidance and the existing order/payment identifiers, stops interval and foreground retries, and keeps My Purchases/support actions. It never labels this authentication outcome Payment Failed or offers Try Again/payment creation. It pins its original account and rejects a late success after an account switch. Checkout forwards the authentication pause/message and pending identifier to that screen and guards late success by account identity. Existing focus/unmount guards and in-flight coalescing remain.

`recoverMyPendingPayments` passes its original user ID into each recovery request and retains its existing per-user coalescing and post-response identity check. Server orders remain the durable recovery record after Android process loss. This patch adds no new local financial-state storage and does not mutate or discard pending server records.

## Investigation and evidence boundary

Read the full production audit, MV003 and MV004 reports; independently inspected Git status, actual handlers, settlement migrations, constraints and triggers. The workspace already had extensive modifications and untracked files. Only the ten files listed below belong to this task. MV003's standalone proof migration and original runner, MV004's verifier/mobile implementation, and entitlement readers are unchanged.
