Management API database requests specified `read_only: true`, `BEGIN TRANSACTION READ ONLY`, a 15-second statement timeout, SELECTs and ROLLBACK. Outputs contain schema or aggregate/boolean evidence, never customer or financial identifiers. Latest migration remains `20261104110000`. Live orders permit `created`, `pending`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `paid`, `refunded`; provider `authorized`/`captured` are not additional order statuses.

Live `orders_guard_client_writes` restricts student transitions, but bypasses its transition checks for service-role/migration/admin callers. Other current order triggers protect price, identity, tax and entitlement snapshots, not terminal payment status. Order/payment/purchase uniqueness constraints protect duplicate keys, not contradictory statuses. There is no order status-history trigger in the inspected trigger set. The inspected audit/history table inventory contained referral-settings audit and external-refund records, not a durable order transition journal.

Read deployed `create-order`, `verify-payment` and `razorpay-webhook` bundles **through the Management API**, in memory, without invoking their execution endpoints. The deployed creation bundle contains three `orders.update({status:"failed"})` patterns and no `.eq("status","created")` filter pattern. Verification/webhook bundles contain the shared settlement path and lack the MV003 commitment RPC reference. Pattern inspection supports the current source investigation; it does not identify the historical actor or prove which deployed version handled those records.

Current local creation has four unconditional failure writes before this patch (including the pre-existing local legacy-choice binding branch). Client `markOrderStatus` does not write live production orders. Signed failure/unrelated webhooks are acknowledged without setting order failure. Captured callbacks, webhook and event-retry worker use the shared provider-verified helper and service-only, row-locked SQL settlement. Settlement can legitimately accept a later capture for a failed/cancelled unpaid attempt; stale discount/wallet rules may instead queue compensation. The retained SQL and helper enforce provider capture/order/amount/currency, snapshot, ownership, idempotency and transactional grant checks. Full refunds and lost disputes revoke purchases; open/won disputes suspend/restore; partial refunds retain access under existing policy.

An initial sandbox DNS failure required escalation for authorized read-only network access. A multi-statement query initially returned only its last result through Management API; the definitive query aggregates all sections into one JSON result. A SELECT invoking the service-only Notes predicate was denied by the audit database role. No grants/roles were changed or bypassed. That failed read was replaced with minimized purchase evidence. No live PDF authorization or provider state is claimed.

## Root cause conclusion

**Confirmed prevention defect:** privileged late failure updates can regress an already settled Razorpay order because terminal status is not guarded in SQL and creation failure writes have no current-status condition. The isolated production-baseline test settles a synthetic order using actual SQL, overwrites it to failed, and verifies successful payment/purchase records remain, commitment becomes false, and existing Notes access remains true. This reproduces the MV005 contradiction without provider/network mocks standing in for SQL.

**Historical cause remains unconfirmed.** Both production orders were updated after their purchase and verified-payment timestamps, consistent with a later order change. There is no retrieved status history identifying an actor, original state or failure callback. A later privileged write, older code, manual historical repair, or another historical process remains possible. Neither fraudulent entitlement nor a specific callback/webhook race is established.

A second concrete hazard exists in the current helper: its proof fails on failed-order/success-purchase history and it would replay settlement, potentially rewriting order/accounting state or compensating an old discounted purchase. MV005 now routes those contradictions to manual review instead.

## Local changes and transitions

The standalone migration installs only a fixed-search-path, service-definer BEFORE UPDATE OF status trigger for Razorpay orders, without financial DML or access-reader changes. There is no privileged/admin bypass. PostgreSQL's order row lock ensures an updater waiting behind settlement sees the terminal row. It rejects paid -> created/pending/failed/cancelled, refunded -> any other status, and changes back to unpaid states when an existing capture fact is visible. It raises generic SQLSTATE 23514 without identifiers. Same-status metadata updates and existing historical contradictions are left alone.

Unpaid created/pending/failed/cancelled -> paid remains available through the existing verified settlement RPC; the trigger itself never proves payment or grants access. Paid -> refunded remains available. Refunded capture replays stay revoked. Existing failure/discount/compensation and refund/dispute routines are preserved. Authorized payments remain unentitled; captured facts alone remain unentitled until atomic settlement and MV003 commitment succeed.

All four creation failure updates now include `.eq("status","created")`, allowing a late error to affect zero rows safely after concurrent settlement. No pricing, order-creation or provider behavior changed.

The helper checks failed/cancelled orders for an existing successful purchase before invoking settlement or queuing compensation. Contradictions return a retryable 503 with an internal manual-review reason; lookup errors also fail closed. Existing verifier behavior keeps these outcomes pending and advises My Purchases/support before paying again. Webhooks/retry workers retain retry behavior rather than acknowledging a fictional repaired entitlement. Existing authenticated capture-fact recording still occurs before this check; no orders, payments, purchases, wallets or compensation rows are rewritten by that branch. Successful historical purchases remain accessible through unchanged readers. This is a hold for owner review, not automatic reconciliation.

Actual provider, Supabase Auth transport and native Android acceptance remain unverified; mock transports are not live financial evidence. The final suite was rerun after changing the historical-review outcome from 409 to retryable 503 so it cannot become a terminal client payment failure. No required deterministic local test remains blocked.

## Read-only historical reconciliation plan

Use `supabase/tests/mv005-production-readonly.sql` to repeat the exact minimized status/reference/snapshot/history/refund checks under read-only production access. The file contains only SELECTs, read-only transactions and rollbacks. Its two result sets should be read independently in psql; Management API callers must request/collect each result explicitly. Do not invoke verification/recovery/worker endpoints to obtain evidence, since those write.

For **each** case independently, a trusted owner should privately map its internal order key and linked payment/purchase using the same failed/success join, storing identifiers only in an access-controlled reconciliation manifest outside this report. Record immutable row preimages, hashes, schema/function versions and evidence timestamps. Recompute exact owner/product/amount/provider-order/payment and scope matches; inspect all capture/inbox/legacy/refund/compensation/wallet/referral/review records. Obtain old logs or owner-held historical records to identify a status change if available; current timestamps alone are not history.

In Razorpay Dashboard, verify merchant account and live mode, linked order/receipt, payment identity, INR amount in paise, actual captured amount/status/time, payment attempts, refunds (including cumulative partial amounts), disputes/chargebacks/reversals and relevant settlement evidence. Save redacted evidence and a private reference to original exports. Verify both current and historical provider facts; a successful application payment row is insufficient. No provider credentials or Dashboard API operations were used in this session.

For both A and B, the current safe intended state is **hold financial history unchanged and preserve the successful purchase pending owner/provider verification**. If independently verified captured, correctly priced and unreversed, the proposed final state is order paid with its existing successful payment/purchase untouched. If full refund/lost dispute or mismatched capture is established, stop the paid-status proposal and prepare a separately authorized reversal/compensation decision; do not silently erase or revoke a purchase to improve aggregates. Unresolved evidence means no repair.

## Separately reviewable repair proposal — NOT EXECUTED

This is a proposed procedure, deliberately outside migration execution and test runners. It requires separate explicit owner approval after Dashboard evidence for **each** record. Approval must bind the private manifest, exact keys, expected preimages, intended action and audit destination; approval of the local prevention patch is not approval of financial repair.

For a provider-confirmed, unreversed captured payment only, the narrow proposed repair is `orders.status: failed -> paid`. Do not alter payment/purchase status, identifiers, snapshots, amounts, timestamps, wallet/referral balances or old event rows. Do not replay settlement or create a capture marker as a substitute for evidence. Two approved keys maximum; if only one is proven, obtain approval for that one, rather than requiring both to be rewritten together.

Preconditions and execution safeguards for an approved operator:

1. Preserve encrypted/restricted full row preimages and linked financial/provider evidence, hashes and backup restore references before writing. Establish a reviewed append-only audit destination before approval; there is no assumed existing order repair journal.
2. Prevent conflicting operational repair/retry work through an approved maintenance coordination window. Begin a SERIALIZABLE transaction with bounded lock/statement timeouts. Acquire existing wallet advisory locks in deterministic user order, then order locks in deterministic key order, then linked payment/purchase/capture/compensation/event locks in a documented consistent order. Re-read all facts and preimages inside the transaction. SERIALIZABLE failure, changed rows, new reversal evidence or missing evidence aborts; do not automatically retry a repair.
3. Select by the approved exact order keys, never by the broad MV005 status predicate alone. Require the expected count, failed status, exact immutable preimage, exactly one matching successful payment/purchase and independently approved provider evidence. Include NULL-safe owner/product/amount/snapshot/provider identity checks, no compensation or unresolved reversal, and exact expected counts. Abort if any assertion fails. Do not assume an absence check/row lock alone prevents newly inserted reversal evidence.
4. Use a parameterized update of **status only**, constrained to the approved key and expected failed status plus all expected-preimage predicates. Inspect RETURNING privately and require precisely the approved row set, no extras. Record actor, approval/evidence hashes, before/after preimages, reason and transaction identity in the approved audit destination atomically. If atomic audit recording is unavailable, do not run the repair. No runnable production repair is included in this patch.
5. Before COMMIT assert successful payment/purchase identities and amounts are unchanged and the targeted contradiction count falls by exactly the approved number. Require independent operator review under the approved procedure. After commit perform read-only verification in a fresh snapshot: exact approved statuses, unchanged financial/entitlement fingerprints, new aggregate counts and durable audit evidence. Reconfirm no unreviewed reversal has arrived. The safe status-only repair requires no entitlement insertion or deletion.
6. On any pre-commit discrepancy, ROLLBACK. After commit, do not restore an inconsistent failed state or disable the prevention guard automatically. Preserve evidence and stop related repair work. A new provider reversal must follow a separately approved compensating refund/dispute process, with immutable audit history. If the repair itself was erroneous, obtain owner approval for an evidence-based compensating change; do not blanket-restore database backups or delete financial rows. The new guard intentionally rejects paid -> failed.

Historical MV005 remains **NOT FIXED** until an approved repair and its independent read-only verification actually complete. No repair statements were executed.

## Exact task file list

3. `supabase/functions/_shared/settle.ts` — hold historical contradictions for review before accounting replay.

## Deployment requirements, risks and blockers

Nothing was deployed. Review a clean, narrow patch rather than deploying the dirty workspace or applying every pending migration. The MV005 standalone trigger uses the inspected production schema and does not require November 8/9 additions. Review/apply it separately; deploy the conditional creation-handler delta and manual-review shared-helper delta with the retained MV003/MV004 fixes. MV003's service-only commitment RPC must precede bundles using it. Bundle every intended caller of the helper (verification, webhook, event worker), inventorying their pre-existing undeployed dependencies before rollout. MV004 Auth/owner filtering and trusted HMAC gateway behavior must remain intact. No mobile source change is needed for MV005; keep all payment enablement gates disabled.

Privileged code that previously regressed terminal status now receives 23514; application error paths must use conditional updates. A concurrent capture-fact INSERT alone does not serialize all writers: the definitive settlement row lock and final paid status prevent terminal regression, and capture-only records remain recoverable. The trigger is not a provider verification mechanism, generic database repair engine or protection against a superuser disabling triggers. Existing privileged settlement RPCs still require trusted provider-verified callers. No universal cross-table rewrite/constraint is added that would invalidate historical purchases or interfere with transactional insertion order.

Historical-review responses remain retryable and may continue webhook/worker retries until an owner resolves the evidence; existing retry parking/monitoring needs operational review. A racing historical-check read may conservatively return pending while another transaction finishes settlement; a later retry can prove the now-paid row. Existing pending/parked reversal facts still block MV003 proof. Refunds against already inconsistent historical orders require the separately reviewed reconciliation procedure, rather than assuming the normal paid-order reversal predicate repairs them.

Remaining production blockers: reviewed rollout, private Dashboard evidence for both historical cases, owner-approved repair/audit destination, production read-only post-repair verification, real delivery/retry and authenticated/device acceptance. No financial or entitlement data was changed to make the audit pass. MV006 was not started.

# MV006 — successful purchase ownership — 2026-11-10

**MV006: FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED (prevention). PRODUCTION: NOT FIXED.** Historical canonicalization and approved deployment remain outstanding. No production records were changed. New purchases remain disabled.

## Scope and preservation

Read the production audit and MV003, MV004 and MV005 local reports. Inspected Git status before editing and fingerprinted 1,317 pre-existing tracked/untracked files. Every pre-existing file, including all MV003/MV004/MV005 changes, remains byte-for-byte unchanged. This patch consists entirely of new files listed below. No website/admin implementation was accessed for modification. No staging configuration, service, database or credentials were used. No migration/function was deployed, payment endpoint invoked, real Razorpay transaction created, or financial reconciliation performed. MV002 was not started.

## Production evidence and root cause

Only project **xbvenkfmesugrbjuufyw** was queried. Management API requests used `read_only: true`, explicit `BEGIN TRANSACTION READ ONLY`, a 15-second statement timeout, schema/aggregate/boolean SELECTs, and `ROLLBACK`. The reusable query is `supabase/tests/mv006-production-readonly.sql`. Its minimized result is retained in `docs/audit/evidence/mv006-20261110/production-readonly.json`. No customer identities, raw provider payloads, payment references or secrets are in these artifacts. The excluded index identifier is redacted.

Fresh observations:

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Latest migration | `20261104110000` |
| Duplicate successful keyed user/product groups | 1 |
| Successful purchase rows in that group | 2 |
| Excluded historical purchase rows in that group | 1 |
| Distinct linked orders / provider payment references | 2 / 2 |
| Duplicate purchase order keys / non-null payment references globally | 0 / 0 |
| Successful purchases without product key | 0 |
| Both affected order / purchase statuses | paid / success |
| Both exact successful payment identity/owner/product/amount matches | true |
| Both immutable entitlement snapshots match linked orders | true |
| Capture markers / compensations / inbox reversal facts / external refund rows, each case | 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 |
| Each legacy event ledger | `payment.captured` |

The partial unique index is valid and unique on `(user_id,product_id)` for `status='success'`, **except one purchase ID**. The other indexes preserve purchase-order and provider-payment idempotency. Live purchase triggers enforce client-write restrictions and order snapshots; none enforces the excluded row's ownership collision. Function-definition hashes for creation, quotation, settlement, legacy settlement, refund, dispute, Notes authorization and AAL authorization exactly matched the locally rebuilt production baseline (eight functions). Thus the inspected retained function bodies are independently tied to the observed live schema, rather than assumed current.

**Confirmed prevention defect:** the exceptional purchase does not participate in ownership uniqueness. When its indexed counterpart is refunded, another privileged INSERT can grant success beside the still-successful excluded purchase. A status/key UPDATE of the excluded purchase can likewise evade the index. The local pre-fix test reproduces the first sequence using actual schema, separate order/payment records, real settlement/refund functions and synthetic users. It does not establish which historical writer created production's duplicate.

The normal current settlement wrapper already holds the per-user wallet advisory lock, locks the order, and checks all successful purchases, including the excluded row. It routes an additional captured payment to `already_owned` compensation rather than a new entitlement. Consequently this is a missing database backstop on other/future privileged write paths, **not a newly demonstrated race in the current verified settlement wrapper**. No provider-reference or order duplication, double charging, fraud, or historical actor is established by this investigation.

The retained September index migration contains an author's historical testing/capture narrative. That comment is a provenance lead, not independent current provider evidence; it does not prove merchant identity, original business intent, present refund/dispute status or the correct canonical row. The reason and financial disposition of the exception remain unconfirmed. No customer/account claims or payment references from that comment are reproduced here.

## Purchase model and exact implementation

`purchases.status='success'` is the existing authoritative Notes ownership predicate. Orders retain immutable pricing/scope snapshots; payments retain verified provider financial records. Existing settlement can record a paid non-entitling `duplicate` purchase, and the current wrapper normally instead records a capture plus durable compensation for a new already-owned payment. A capture/paid order alone never proves entitlement. No separate entitlement table is introduced.

The standalone MV006 migration adds one fixed-search-path, VOLATILE SECURITY DEFINER trigger function and one BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger. It executes after existing purchase snapshot triggers. For a **new keyed success** (including restoration or owner/product change), it acquires the same `wallet:<user>` transaction advisory lock as creation/settlement/refunds and checks *all* successful purchases for the key, including the excluded historical ID. A conflict raises generic SQLSTATE `23505`, rolling back the caller's complete transaction. It never silently converts a row to duplicate, grants compensation, selects a canonical purchase, or changes historical data.

The guard preserves:

- Existing successful ownership with unchanged user/product, including metadata updates on both historical successes. It deliberately does not retroactively enforce a global invariant on those rows.
- Non-success transitions, existing full/partial refunds, disputes and reversals. A won dispute already selects `duplicate` when another success exists; legitimate restoration without another owner remains successful.
- Same-order INSERT/ON CONFLICT idempotency: an existing non-null purchase order is still protected by the unchanged unique order index, so the attempted insertion cannot create another row. UPDATE conflict branches still pass through the guard.
- Legacy NULL user/product records and their established scope-based restoration. No product is guessed from content scope. NULL-key rows remain outside keyed uniqueness, as before; current verified settlements supply both keys and production currently has no successful NULL-product rows.
- Existing provider-payment and purchase-order unique indexes, exact MV003 commitment proof, MV004 authenticated recovery, and MV005 terminal-order protection. No shared helper, handler, mobile implementation or entitlement reader is changed.

New keyed grants require **READ COMMITTED**, the existing normal settlement isolation. The VOLATILE function performs its ownership SELECT after the advisory lock with a fresh statement snapshot. REPEATABLE READ and SERIALIZABLE new keyed grants fail closed with `40001`; otherwise an old transaction snapshot could miss ownership committed while waiting. This is an explicit rollout compatibility condition. Existing metadata and reversal operations remain available. Privileged direct or bulk writes may encounter deadlocks from row/lock ordering and must roll back/retry under an approved procedure; the guard never bypasses uniqueness to resolve them. A superuser disabling triggers is outside the ordinary writer contract.

For all new keyed ownership outcomes, at most one successful purchase is admitted. Existing historical duplicates remain grandfathered, unresolved evidence. Both currently yield the same existential ownership outcome; no arbitrary canonical winner is invented. Production-wide one-row uniqueness requires the separate reconciliation and constraint step below.

## Restoration and Notes authorization

The mobile repository still filters the authenticated user's successful purchases; the service restores durable scopes after login/process reload. Multiple historical successful rows remain visible as historical purchase records and do not cause scope/access loss. Refunding one leaves access through another success; reversing all removes access. Compensated/new duplicate payments do not manufacture another successful scope. The UI is not used to enforce uniqueness or hide history.

Paid PDF signing continues to use trusted backend user/course/scope ownership. Actual local `user_can_access_note` checks prove preserved historical access and denial after full reversal. The existing mobile entitlement suite proves that server denial overrides a stale unlocked route. No live signed URL was requested. The MV004 suite covers account-pinned recovery, bounded token refresh and restart/history recovery behavior; native Android login/reinstall/UPI execution remains unverified, as in the earlier reports.

## Read-only reconciliation proposal — not executed

The two production purchases both match their respective paid order and successful payment snapshots. They have separate provider references and only historical application capture-event evidence. This supports retention and investigation; it does not establish two current unreversed captures or authorize a refund. Missing application refund rows do not prove no provider-side reversal occurred.

A trusted owner/operator must privately map this one duplicate group to its exact purchase/order/payment keys in an access-controlled manifest. Keep identifiers out of public reports. For each row record preimages, timestamps, immutable scope and amount, provider and order links, capture/inbox/legacy events, compensation/refund/dispute facts, wallet debits/credits, referral effects and review flags. Compare scope snapshots across the pair, including bundle arrays and course: if they differ, choosing one row may lose legitimate Notes rights and the simple canonicalization proposal must stop. Verify current account and unrelated entitlements without disclosing identities.

Manual Razorpay Dashboard checks (no API writes or new payments):

1. Confirm the correct merchant account and live mode privately. Find each stored payment and linked provider order/receipt; compare INR amount in paise and timestamps to the private manifest.
2. Confirm authorization versus capture, captured amount/time and all attempts. Separate application payment references are not independent proof of two captured funds movements.
3. Inspect full and cumulative partial refunds, processing/failed refunds, disputes/chargebacks and their outcomes, reversals and settlement adjustments. Check provider exports and current state, not only old webhook logs.
4. Preserve dated/redacted screenshots or exports with hashes and restricted links to originals. If available, retrieve historical deployment/repair logs and owner-held explanation for the exception. Provider retention gaps require explicit uncertainty; do not infer a history from timestamps or a migration comment.

**Proposed canonical strategy:** preserve all evidence pending approval. If both purchases represent the same validated scope and provider evidence supports continued ownership, approve exactly one unreversed, snapshot-correct purchase as the active ownership anchor. Choose by provider/evidence/business policy first; chronology can be a documented tie-breaker only after those checks. Retain both paid orders and distinct payment rows unchanged. The other purchase may be approved for the existing `duplicate` non-entitling state, with its original successful state, reason, canonical link, provider evidence, actor and approval preserved in an append-only reconciliation audit. Do not delete, merge, reassign provider references, rewrite amounts/snapshots/timestamps, or call a valid historic payment failed.

Whether a second legitimate historic payment should be refunded, retained as a duplicate payment, or handled another way is a separate owner/provider decision. No automatic refund is proposed. Do not relabel it refunded without actual provider proof. Resolve what happens if the chosen canonical payment is later refunded while another valid historic payment remains: current readers do not automatically promote `duplicate` rows. The owner must approve either provider-backed replacement ownership with an audit link or a policy that the noncanonical payment is resolved without continuing entitlement. Until this policy and scope preservation are settled, keep the historical successes and do not activate the complete unique index. Ambiguous, disputed, mismatched or incomplete evidence means no repair.

**Historical replay is another required precondition:** demoting a successful historical purchase to `duplicate` makes its exact MV003 commitment false. Both production rows lack capture markers. A later verified capture replay could then enter settlement and queue `already_owned` compensation (or encounter a stale discount), even if an owner intended to retain that historic payment. Before any canonicalization, separately review and test terminal non-entitling handling for those exact audited historical payments, or resolve their provider-backed financial disposition first. Preserve MV003's requirement that only the exact active purchase proves entitlement; another purchase's ownership must never prove this payment committed. Do not manufacture a `granted` marker or run recovery as a reconciliation shortcut. This patch leaves both successes intact and does not implement or authorize that future historical-routing change. The future constraint approval must explicitly attest that replay and future-refund policies are resolved.

## Required safeguards for any future production repair

This document does not authorize production repair. Separate **owner approval** must bind the exact private manifest, affected keys/counts, expected preimages, provider evidence hashes, canonical choice, scope-preservation checks, intended status changes and audit destination.

- Before writing, take verified restricted backups/row preimages with hashes and restore references; preserve all linked financial records and immutable provider evidence. Establish a durable append-only reconciliation audit destination. If atomic audit recording is unavailable, stop.
- Coordinate retry/refund/reconciliation workers under an approved operational window. Use a bounded SERIALIZABLE repair transaction, deterministic existing wallet/user advisory locks, then deterministic order and purchase/related row locks. Lock ordering must be reviewed against refund/referral routines. No automatic retry after serialization failure or changed evidence. This status-only canonicalization does not create new success grants; a proposal that does must obey the new guard's READ COMMITTED contract and requires its own review.
- Re-read exact approved keys inside the transaction. Require precisely the approved two-row group and expected owners/products/statuses/scopes/order/payment relationships, no unexpected row or new reversal/compensation fact. Row locks alone do not prevent every new provider-event insertion; coordinate writers and use serialization/preimage assertions. Abort on any mismatch.
- Use parameterized, exact-key and expected-preimage predicates. Require exactly the approved affected-row set/count (normally one approved success-to-duplicate row), retain all financial records, and atomically log before/after images, actor, approval/evidence/canonical links, reason and transaction identity. Never run a broad duplicate-group cleanup. No runnable repair DML is supplied here.
- Before commit, verify one intended active ownership, identical preserved Notes scope, unchanged orders/payments/provider references/amounts/wallet/referral fingerprints and the exact audit row count. Require independent operator review. After commit repeat read-only checks in a fresh snapshot, confirm only the approved duplicate count changed, inspect audit durability and check newly arrived provider reversals.
- Any pre-commit discrepancy means ROLLBACK. After commit stop on unexpected evidence; do not automatically promote a duplicate, restore conflicting success, disable the guard, delete audit rows or restore a whole database backup. An erroneous canonical choice requires a separately approved compensating transaction with current provider evidence. A reviewed transfer would demote the old anchor before promoting the validated replacement atomically under the guard's isolation/locking contract. New financial reversals follow the existing refund/dispute path and their own approval scope.

## Migration safety and future deployment order

The deployable prevention migration contains no financial DML and no new unique-index build. It can be applied over the known historical duplicates and legacy data without first rewriting them. Keep the exceptional index intact. A reviewed operator must inventory any additional live BEFORE triggers and confirm the guard remains last after snapshot mutation, normal writer isolation is READ COMMITTED, current indexes/grants match the inspected baseline, and no unrelated pending migration is included.

1. Prepare a clean narrowly reviewed artifact from this dirty workspace. Do not run blanket `db push`, `db reset` against hosted projects, or deploy all pending functions.
2. Under separately authorized rollout, install MV003's service-only commitment RPC before deploying any helper that calls it. Include the reviewed MV004 owner-only recovery and MV005 transition/conditional-write/manual-review changes in their documented order and bundles. Preserve HMAC callback gateway behavior and disabled purchase gates.
3. Apply only the reviewed MV006 standalone guard migration and validate its function owner, fixed search path, no client execution grant, trigger ordering, old indexes and unchanged history. No additional Edge Function deployment is needed specifically for MV006. Inventory other callers such as event workers before any combined rollout.
4. Conduct the separate private reconciliation/owner decision above. No reconciliation SQL is part of the automatic migrations.
5. Only after approved reconciliation and fresh preconditions pass, separately review `supabase/maintenance/mv006_future_unique_ownership.sql`. It is deliberately **outside `supabase/migrations/`**, requires an explicit session approval reference, locks purchases with bounded timeouts, and aborts on any remaining keyed successful duplicates. The session setting is an operational interlock, not authentication. Restricted migration credentials and verified owner approval are still required.
6. The future script builds a complete success-only unique index without the ID exception and retains the old index. It contains no repair DML. Creation fails if an unexpected index of that name exists. The table lock prevents concurrent changes during its precondition/build transaction; timeout/error rolls everything back. Review acceptable production lock duration before execution. Retiring the old index requires its own reviewed change; do not blindly drop it.
7. Obtain separately authorized read-only deployment verification and real Auth/provider/device acceptance. Production financial endpoint invocations can write and are not authorized by this report. Keep new purchases disabled throughout this task.

Rollback of an uncommitted migration is transactional. After a committed prevention rollout, do not routinely remove safeguards or deploy old false-success/anonymous-recovery code as rollback. Investigate compatibility errors with purchases still disabled and retain financial evidence. Any narrowly scoped schema rollback requires owner review; it cannot reconcile historical data or reverse external funds movements.

# MV008 — purchase ownership refresh race — 2026-11-10

**MV008 LOCAL: FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED.**
**MV008 PRODUCTION: NOT VERIFIED / NOT DEPLOYED.**

MV003–MV006 local regressions PASS. Testing prices ₹1, ₹2 and ₹5 are UNCHANGED. Buy Now remains DISABLED. Production changes: **NONE**. No staging service, hosted database, payment endpoint or provider was contacted. No website/admin code, settlement, verification, database policy, purchase/order/entitlement record or catalog price was changed. No native build, package installation or deployment was performed. MV009 was not started.

## Investigation and root cause

Inspected the production audit and MV003, MV004, MV005 and MV006 reports, Git status, Android instructions, ownership/history services and all consumers. Read the required Expo SDK 57 reference at https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v57.0.0/ before implementation. Existing tracked/untracked work was retained. `rg` is unavailable; local searches used `grep`, `find` and Python instead. No environment file or hosted credentials were loaded.

The original `usePurchases.refresh` awaited `fetchOwnedScopes()` and unconditionally called `setOwnedScopes`, then unconditionally cleared loading. It had no request generation, captured-account check, auth subscription, course invalidation, timeout or unmount cleanup. Its catch only logged an error. Refresh did not set loading true again.

The repository reads successful purchases for a user ID. Scope helpers accept chapter, subject, year and bundle scopes; server PDF authorization remains a separate authoritative operation. The bug was in asynchronous presentation state, not payment verification or the backend ownership predicate.

Relevant paths traced:

| Path | Finding and local treatment |
| --- | --- |
| Initial hook mount | Initial request could finish after a focus/manual request. Both now use the same generation guard. |
| Login/session bootstrap | A hook mounted before synchronous auth initialization could remain empty. Auth-state subscriptions now trigger a read on identity initialization/change. Late bootstrap sessions still cannot overwrite an already received auth event. |
| Logout/account switch | Old responses and previously loaded scopes could survive. Context changes immediately clear displayed data and invalidate pending generations, including logout/login to the same ID. |
| Course/year changes | Ownership was not associated with selected context. Course/year subscriptions clear the previous context and reload; scopes exposed for ownership checks are filtered to the current course. Other years within that course remain eligible for legitimate bundle/year checks. |
| Focus/concurrent refresh | Chapters, Chapter Details, Store, Home and Search already refresh ownership on focus. Every request now participates in latest-request-wins. Notes now also refreshes ownership on focus. |
| App resume | Shared purchase resource now refreshes on AppState `active`. Late background reads cannot beat the new generation. |
| Purchase confirmation | Returning from payment refreshes ownership on focus. My Purchases retains its existing bounded authenticated recovery/Play restore calls; a confirmed result refreshes through the guarded loader, only for the initiating account while focused. No recovery/payment behavior was changed. |
| History | Home and My Purchases had independent unconditional response updates. They now share the guarded purchase resource. History rows, status filters and date sorting behavior are otherwise preserved. |
| Notes | Route `unlocked` could retain stale ownership indefinitely. Notes now derives its display/access hint from current scopes, rather than trusting that route value. |
| Search discovery | Async discovery could complete using obsolete ownership badges after a new ownership overlay. It now rejects obsolete completion and masks sections computed from a previous overlay. |
| Errors/unmount | Errors were silent and old `finally` could clear newer loading. Errors are explicit; same-context last confirmed data is retained, and all obsolete/unmounted updates are ignored. |

Separate catalog-loading races/error states in Store and other screens are outside MV008. Store's catalog loader was not redesigned or repaired. Cached search history/catalog data is not a source of PDF authorization. MV012 history timestamp normalization is also unchanged.

## Exact implementation

`usePurchaseResource` is a small shared read-only hook for ownership and purchase history:

- Every refresh increments a generation and captures `[authenticated user ID, course ID, year ID]`.
- Only a still-mounted, current-generation request with the same current context can update data, loading or error.
- Auth and course/year changes invalidate pending work immediately and clear data from the previous context. There is no cross-account ownership cache.
- The service receives the captured user ID explicitly, rather than choosing an account later during the read. Default no-argument service calls remain compatible.
- Newer refreshes and unmount settle the obsolete waiting promise and clear its deadline. Underlying read transport is logically abandoned, not forcibly aborted. Its eventual response/rejection cannot update UI or produce an unhandled rejection.
- Each current read has a 15-second deadline. Timeout is an explicit refresh error. Retry can recover; a late timed-out response cannot restore obsolete scopes.
- Same-context refresh preserves last confirmed data while loading and on failure. The UI explicitly states that last confirmed ownership may be out of date and offers retry. A successful empty response replaces prior scopes, including revoked/refunded ownership. No grants are fabricated or unioned into server results.
- All ownership consumers show checking/error status. Notes pull-to-refresh also refreshes ownership. Scope filtering is memoized so Search effects remain stable between actual changes.

The change affects ownership hints only. Remote PDF opens/downloads still use the existing server-authorized signer; downloaded files retain the existing offline-access checks in the viewer/download manager. A stale owned badge or retained scope cannot bypass server denial. The new Notes test exercises that denial explicitly. No raw public PDF fallback or new local entitlement store was added.

## Deterministic race and isolation evidence

The original hook was extracted with `git show HEAD:mobile-app/src/hooks/usePurchases.ts`; its starting file was clean in Git. Running that actual original source through the existing TypeScript/React harness produced:

```text
BEFORE: newer B confirmed scopes = 1
BEFORE: stale A overwrote scopes = 0
PASS original hook MV008 defect reproduced deterministically
```

The replacement actual-hook suite proves:

1. A slow/B fast: B's confirmed purchase remains owned after A returns empty.
2. B slow/A fast: A cannot grant ownership or finish B's loading; B's result wins.
3. Multiple simultaneous refreshes: only newest success/error/loading is accepted.
4. Actual auth-state startup/login/logout/switch notifications; late bootstrap ignored.
5. Logout during a pending ownership read clears ownership; login loads the correct captured account.
6. Account switch clears confirmed scopes and rejects the previous account's pending response, even if the new account read fails.
7. Logout/login to the same ID cannot revive a request from the old session.
8. Course switch clears pending context, rejects previous-course results and filters returned scopes by course. Year switches invalidate requests without removing valid multi-year scope semantics.
9. API timeout and API failure retain same-context confirmed ownership with an explicit error and correct loading state.
10. Retry recovers; a successful empty response removes old ownership; a late timed-out success cannot reverse that result.
11. Initial failure cannot fabricate ownership or claim a successful empty load.
12. Unmount rejects late success/error/finally updates and clears auth/course/AppState listeners and timers.
13. Payment-confirmation/focus/resume refreshes show the confirmed purchase and later server revocation accurately.
14. Chapter, subject, year and bundle fixtures unlock only covered chapters/products.
15. History uses the same ordering/account/unmount guards.
16. Actual Notes mount/focus requests race safely. A locked route cannot hide a confirmed purchase; an unlocked route cannot grant another account access. Signer denial prevents PDF opening despite current UI ownership.
17. Actual My Purchases recovery refresh beats pre-confirmation history reads; a previous account's recovery completion cannot trigger a refresh for the next account.

These are **20 named passing tests** because timeout/failure and the four scope types are counted separately. The Search suite additionally proves obsolete async discovery cannot restore stale ownership badges. Tests use synthetic deferred promises, deterministic event callbacks and manually advanced deadline callbacks. No payment is created or confirmed with a real provider.

# MV009 — Store network, empty and retry handling — 2026-11-11

**MV009 LOCAL: FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED.**
**MV009 PRODUCTION: NOT DEPLOYED / NOT VERIFIED.**

MV008 and MV003–MV006 local regressions PASS. Intentional testing prices **₹1 / ₹2 / ₹5 are UNCHANGED**. Buy Now is **STILL DISABLED**. Production data changed: **NONE**. No staging service, hosted Supabase database, payment endpoint or provider was contacted. No website/admin code, backend logic, migration, payment/order/purchase/entitlement record, dependency or price was modified. Nothing was built or deployed. MV010 was not started.

## Investigation and confirmed root cause

Inspected Git status first. The workspace already contained extensive tracked and untracked changes, including Store's MV008 ownership UI. Preserved that work. Reviewed the production audit and the MV003, MV004, MV005, MV006 and MV008 reports, Android instructions, Store/navigation/card components, course/auth state, list/ownership hooks, catalog services/repositories and existing regression harnesses. Read the exact Expo SDK 57 reference required by `mobile-app/AGENTS.md`: https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v57.0.0/. No package installation was needed. `rg` is unavailable; searches used find, Python and bounded reads.

The original Store initializes `blocks=[]`, sets `loading=true`, and executes `fetchYears(courseId)`, followed by a nested Promise.all of year products, subjects and subject products. Its catch only logs the error; finally unconditionally sets loading false. FlatList then displays **“🔍 No Academic Years Found”** for the unchanged empty blocks. Consequently, years, subjects, year-product and subject-product failures all become a false empty academic catalog on initial loading. A refresh/course-change failure can instead leave a previous course's blocks visible without a freshness warning.

Additional paths:

- `fetchYears` uses an offline cache fallback and shares pending year reads for up to one second. Store supplied no fallback callback. A cached empty response can therefore conceal a failed live read, and retry can reuse pending work.
- Subjects also use a cache fallback, without Store observing fallback freshness.
- Store has no catalog retry/pull refresh, deadline, request generation, account/course snapshot key or unmount cleanup. Old successes and finally callbacks can overwrite a newer course's result/loading state.
- `useCourseYear.ready=false` prevents the original request indefinitely when context loading fails or stalls.
- Repositories already throw Supabase errors. The defect is their interpretation and lifecycle in Store, rather than a database/catalog mutation problem.

Deterministic reproduction ran the actual start-of-task Store source with a rejected synthetic `fetchYears`:

```text
PASS reproduced before fix: rejected network request renders No Academic Years Found
```

The preserved source and reproduction log are session-local at `/tmp/mv009/StoreScreen-before.tsx` and `/tmp/mv009/before-reproduction.log`.

## Implemented behavior and exact changes

`storeCatalogService.ts` builds a complete live snapshot using the existing active-years and active-subject repositories plus the existing product service. Store now bypasses the years/subjects offline fallback and years pending-request cache; other screens' shared cache behavior is unchanged. Every new catalog attempt calls the repository afresh with an explicit course ID. Years and subjects retain their order sorting; product selection retains the original first-product behavior and exact price values.

All stages publish together. If any subject or product read fails, no partial snapshot is presented as a complete catalog and no failed product read is converted to a zero-price offer. A genuine empty subjects response still renders “No subjects in this year yet.” Genuine successful missing products remain null and have no purchase offer. Complete same-context data can remain visible on a refresh failure. This intentionally avoids introducing a new partial-catalog UI.

`useStoreCatalog.ts` owns explicit **loading / retrying / success / empty / error** states:

- Each active attempt has a 15-second deadline, including course resolution when needed. A course-read error or missing selection produces actionable messaging instead of an indefinite spinner or an unscoped catalog read. Context retries force a new profile read.
- Account ID, course ID and context readiness identify the snapshot. Auth/course subscriptions immediately invalidate previous work. A render also masks a mismatched snapshot before effects run. Year-only changes within one course retain the all-years catalog; MV008 independently refreshes year-scoped ownership.
- Each accepted attempt increments a generation. Only a mounted, active, current-generation request with the current context can publish data/error/loading. A-B-C-A switches cannot revive the first A request.
- A synchronous pending guard coalesces repeated Retry/refresh taps for the active context. A failure or timeout releases the guard so Retry starts fresh work. Context switches supersede pending work immediately.
- Same-context complete data stays visible during refresh and on failure. Successful empty reads replace old data. Data from a previous account/course is never retained.
- Cleanup cancels deadlines, settles abandoned waiting promises and removes subscriptions. Late results, rejections and finally callbacks cannot update state. A context read completing after its deadline cannot start catalog reads for that expired attempt.
- Error messages distinguish connection failure, timeout, authorization/JWT/permission failure, and identifiable HTTP/PostgREST/Postgres server failures. Unknown errors use a connection/retry message. Authorization failures instruct the user to sign in again and retry; Store does not sign out users, modify sessions or loop authentication retries.

`StoreScreen.tsx` reuses `ErrorState` with its **Try Again** action. Loading/retrying has a spinner and visible text. The empty-years component is reachable only for successful empty state. Populated and successful-empty lists support pull-to-refresh. Retained rows show **“catalog may be out of date”** during refresh and after failure. Initial failures show the error without an empty list.

Store's subject-to-Chapters route retains subject/year IDs and titles. MV008's purchase ownership status and focus refresh are retained. The year purchase pill explicitly disables while purchasing or when the provider is disabled; Store's buy callback also returns immediately for the disabled provider, including direct callback invocation in tests. No payment configuration, payment backend or product-card implementation was changed.

# MV010 — Notes purchase options errors, retry and isolation — 2026-11-11

**MV010 LOCAL: FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED.**  
**MV010 PRODUCTION: NOT DEPLOYED / NOT VERIFIED.**

MV009 and MV008 remain **FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED**, with their local regressions passing; neither is deployed/production-verified by this task. MV003–MV006 local regressions **PASS**. Their previous production rollout/reconciliation requirements remain outstanding. Testing prices **₹1 / ₹2 / ₹5: UNCHANGED**. Buy Now: **STILL DISABLED**. Production data changed: **NONE**.

## Scope, investigation and preservation

Inspected Git status first and preserved the extensive pre-existing tracked/untracked work. Reviewed the production audit and MV003, MV004, MV005, MV006, MV008 and MV009 reports; inspected actual purchase-option consumers, Notes, Chapter Details, ownership hooks/services/repositories, course/auth subscriptions, purchase gating, and shared loading/error UI. Followed `mobile-app/AGENTS.md` by reading the exact [Expo SDK 57 reference](https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v57.0.0/) before writing code. No dependencies were installed. `rg` is unavailable; discovery used find, grep, bounded reads and Python.

A start-of-task SHA-256 snapshot covers 1,718 pre-existing files, excluding dependency trees, Git internals and generated build/cache directories. Exactly four pre-existing files changed in this task, listed below; **1,714 remain byte-for-byte unchanged**. This includes prior audit reports, all backend source/migrations/tests, payment configuration and logic, authentication implementation, ownership hooks, NotesScreen and secure PDF authorization code. No Git reset, stash, checkout, clean or commit was used.

No staging configuration/credentials were loaded and no staging service was contacted. No hosted Supabase, provider or financial endpoint was invoked. No website/admin code, production records, prices, settlement/webhook/authentication logic or backend deployment files were changed. Existing local backend suites were executed without editing them. All mutation fixtures were synthetic and confined to a disposable local database. No APK/AAB, prebuild, release or deployment was performed. MV011 was not implemented.

## Root cause and complete flow

The original `UnlockOptionsPanel` initializes `options=[]`, starts a fetch, logs rejection without recording error, then clears loading in finally. A rejected request therefore renders **“No purchase options set up for this chapter yet.”** That message is indistinguishable from a successful empty catalog. The original effect only watches chapter/subject/year props; it neither subscribes to selected course/account changes nor resets prior options on failure/navigation. It has an unmount flag but no deadline or Retry.

The captured original component was replayed through the actual TypeScript/React harness with a synthetic rejected fetch:

```text
PASS reproduced original MV010: rejected request renders no purchase options
```

`productsService.fetchBuyOptionsForChapter` fans out four live repository reads: chapter, subject, year and course bundle products. Promise.all already rejects a failed tier. Bundles are filtered by year membership. Repository errors propagate, but the original product repository converts a missing/null payload with no reported error into `[]`. That is another route to a false empty tier. There is no persistent catalog fallback in these production reads. Existing isolated-test-build branches remain intact and were not contacted.

`ChapterDetailsScreen` had a separate `useLoadResource` loader. Its explicit error branch already prevented ordinary rejection from becoming empty, but it lacked a deadline, account/course subscriptions and duplicate-attempt control. A null repository payload still looked successfully empty. It now uses the same panel/loader as Notes. Notes embeds the panel only while locked; its independent Notes-list error/preview behavior remains unchanged. No other buy-options consumers were found.

Ownership remains independent of catalog availability: MV008's account/course-filtered `usePurchases` determines current Notes access and refreshes on focus/login/resume. Chapter Details keeps its ownership-status component and hides offers for an owned chapter. Notes retains its focus refresh, preview flags/fallback, secure-open/download flow and server-authorized PDF signer. Video and quiz routes remain free and usable regardless of product failures. The disabled payment-provider gate remains authoritative, with an additional panel callback guard against buying disabled or stale options.

| `mobile-app/src/components/UnlockOptionsPanel.tsx` | Use the guarded hook; show specific error guidance and accessible Retry; render empty copy only for successful empty status; warn about retained data freshness; disable stale/disabled purchase callbacks. Optional title supports the existing Chapter Details heading. |
| `mobile-app/src/services/productsService.ts` | Validate complete four-tier arrays and required product/membership fields, reject malformed partial snapshots, retain scope ordering and bundle membership, and exclude foreign-course products. Prices pass through unchanged. |

## Error / empty / retry and freshness verification

| Condition | Verified behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Initial request | Loading state; no empty-catalog message. |
| Complete nonempty response | Success with chapter, subject, year and eligible bundle options in the original order. |
| All four tiers successfully empty | Empty status and the original no-options message. |
| Network rejection | Connection guidance and Retry; never empty. |
| API/server error | Service-unavailable guidance, including HTTP 503, PostgREST and PostgreSQL codes. |
| Unauthorized/expired JWT/forbidden | Sign-in guidance then Retry; no authentication changes, automatic sign-out or retry loop. |
| API timeout or hung read | Explicit timed-out message after a bounded 15 seconds, including course initialization. Late completion cannot publish. |
| Partial response | Any failed tier, missing array, malformed product/membership or explicit HTTP partial response rejects the snapshot. Completed subsets are withheld. |
| Retry | Calls the live service/repositories afresh; no catalog cache or pending-read sharing. Rapid duplicate taps produce one current attempt. |
| Prior complete same-context options | Retained while retrying/on failure with “may be out of date” warning; stale purchase actions are disabled. A later complete response replaces them. |
| Previously empty, then refresh fails | Error, never the successful empty message. |
| Navigation/account/course/year changes | Clear/mask old visible options immediately, cancel old waiting attempt, and load the new context. |
| Unmount | Remove subscriptions, cancel deadline/waiting promise and reject all late state writes. |

The lifecycle follows MV009's complete-snapshot, bounded-read and generation-guard pattern. The small embedded error area uses the existing app's alert/button/Retry patterns; large Store ErrorState spacing would not suit a Notes list footer. Retry has an explicit button role/label and disabled/busy state. It stays mounted but disabled during retry to avoid removing the focused control mid-request. Error/freshness text uses polite live-region announcements; no programmatic focus jumps are introduced. Successful completion naturally replaces the error UI. Native TalkBack/focus behavior still requires device acceptance.

## Limitations, production release requirements and manual owner actions

This is local TypeScript/harness and SQL evidence, with no native Android rendering, real network failure/session expiration or real-device verification. Deadline/cancellation is logical: an obsolete transport read can continue until the SDK/network completes, but its result cannot publish or trigger an unhandled rejection. Retained data is only a mounted same-context complete snapshot, not a new persistent offline catalog. Unknown server errors receive generic connection/retry guidance. Successful authorized empty responses cannot reveal rows hidden by RLS. Explicit partial responses and malformed tiers are rejected; an unmarked server-side row cap cannot be inferred from a normal array response. Existing catalog fan-out and Chapter Details non-scroll layout are unchanged limitations; MV011 contrast is outside this fix.

No manual owner action is needed to complete local MV010 or keep purchases disabled. For a separately authorized release, review only this seven-file task delta in a clean reviewed artifact alongside the earlier fixes. MV010 adds no schema/Edge Function dependency. Deliver the mobile changes through the appropriate approved release/update; never deploy the entire dirty workspace. Preserve prices and disabled purchases. Earlier backend rollout/reconciliation requirements remain separate.

Before marking production fixed, perform real-device Android acceptance for offline/reconnect/Retry, successful empty catalog, server/session errors, duplicate taps, rapid chapter/course/year/account changes, logout during requests, back/unmount, stale warnings, small screens/large fonts and TalkBack focus/announcements. Confirm existing owned Notes restoration after login/focus, server-denied PDF handling, free preview/video/quiz access and disabled Buy Now. Catalog reads/device verification do not authorize live payment transactions, production record repair or payment enablement.

## Exact colors and calculated contrast

Calculations use sRGB channel linearization, relative luminance weights 0.2126 / 0.7152 / 0.0722 and `(lighter + 0.05) / (darker + 0.05)`. Tests compare unrounded ratios. These are source-color calculations, not screenshot measurements.

[WCAG contrast guidance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/contrast-minimum.html) uses 4.5:1 for normal text and 3:1 for large text. Inactive controls have an exception, but this fix deliberately keeps their important labels readable at the normal-text threshold too. The original 16-unit bold action label is assessed as normal text; its 3.17:1 ratio is insufficient despite exceeding the separate large-text threshold.

| Use | Foreground | Background | Contrast | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| Original panel/price/Buy action | `#FFFFFF` | `#E2703D` accent | **3.172779:1** | FAIL normal text |
| Corrected panel, poster Buy and product price badge; Store year offer/unavailable | `#100C0A` background token | `#E2703D` accent | **6.134181:1** | PASS normal and large text |
| Original white Owned badge | `#FFFFFF` | `#4C9A6B` success | 3.419166:1 | FAIL normal text |
| Corrected poster/product Owned badge | `#100C0A` | `#4C9A6B` | **5.692148:1** | PASS |
| Retry / enabled shared button | `#FFFFFF` | `#8C3A22` primary | **7.654705:1** | PASS |
| Disabled shared button | `#FFFFFF` | `#5C2716` primaryDark | **11.953175:1** | PASS; solid background replaces 0.4 opacity |
| Store owned year pill | `#F5EDE7` text | `#241C17` surfaceMuted | **14.479661:1** | PASS |
| Scope/subtitle/freshness text on card | `#A6968A` subtitle | `#1B1512` card | **6.322823:1** | PASS; unchanged |
| Subtitle/unavailable explanation on screen | `#A6968A` | `#100C0A` | **6.812705:1** | PASS; unchanged |
| Poster large price on screen | `#FF9D6B` accentLight | `#100C0A` | **9.533885:1** | PASS; unchanged |
| Poster discount text on screen | `#4C9A6B` | `#100C0A` | **5.692148:1** | PASS; unchanged |

No global color or typography token changed. Brand terracotta, maroon, green status backgrounds, prices, ownership copy and scope names remain. Disabled poster labels no longer use 0.6 opacity. Main corrected purchase controls use activeOpacity=1 to retain their text/background pairing while pressed; no pressed-state color variant was introduced.

# MV012 — My Purchases chronological sorting — 2026-11-11

**MV012 LOCAL: FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED.**  
**MV012 PRODUCTION: NOT DEPLOYED / NOT VERIFIED.**

MV008, MV009, MV010 and MV011 remain **FIXED — LOCAL VERIFIED**, with passing local regressions. Their production status remains **NOT DEPLOYED / NOT VERIFIED**. MV003–MV006 regressions **PASS — LOCAL**; their previously documented production rollout/reconciliation requirements remain outstanding. Intentional testing prices **₹1 / ₹2 / ₹5: UNCHANGED**. Buy Now: **STILL DISABLED**. Production data changed: **NONE**.

## Investigation and root cause

Inspected initial Git status, the original production audit, all eight MV003/MV004/MV005/MV006/MV008/MV009/MV010/MV011 local reports, Android instructions, purchase types, repository, service, history screen, Home, Search, refresh hooks and existing regression tests. Followed `mobile-app/AGENTS.md` by reading the exact [Expo SDK 57 reference](https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v57.0.0/) before implementation. `rg` is unavailable; used bounded reads, find, grep and Python searches. No package installation was needed.

The original service retained PostgreSQL ISO timestamp strings in `purchasedAt`, despite the `MyPurchase.purchasedAt?: number` contract, then subtracted those strings in its comparator. ISO subtraction produces `NaN`, so an older-first repository response remained older-first. The original service was reconstructed in `/tmp/mv012/purchasesService-before.ts` and its SHA-256 verified against the start-of-task snapshot. Running that actual starting source with synthetic older/newer records reproduced the defect: **old stayed before new**.

| Investigated concern | Current behavior / result |
| --- | --- |
| Actual database timestamp | Retained baseline schema declares `purchases.created_at timestamp with time zone default now() not null`. `toCamelCase` exposes it as `createdAt` without converting its ISO value. No new live schema query was necessary. |
| Legacy fields | Existing precedence remains: `createdAt.toMillis()` when available; otherwise `createdAt ?? purchasedAt`. An invalid non-null `createdAt` is undated rather than silently selecting a different historical field. |
| Server ordering | `getPurchasesByUser` selects purchases with `user_id=uid` and `status='success'`; it has no ORDER BY. The client must establish ordering. The isolated-package branch can append synthetic catalog purchases; its configuration and behavior are unchanged. |
| Client sorting | One invalid purchase-date subtraction was found in `fetchMyPurchases`. Both My Purchases and Home consume that service. Search copies `purchasedAt` into its purchase result's `createdAt`, which previously also reached its recent-sort subtraction as a string; normalization fixes that input without changing Search code or filters. Search's other date-based sorts already receive normalized numeric catalog fields. |
| Null/missing/invalid timestamps | Normalize to `undefined`; sort after every valid instant, then by purchase ID. Valid pre-epoch dates remain before undated records. |
| Timezone handling | `Date.parse` converts database ISO strings with Z/explicit offsets into epoch milliseconds; equivalent instants compare equally. UI retains device-local `en-IN` date formatting. Tests pass in default UTC, Asia/Kolkata and America/New_York process timezones. |
| Grouping | My Purchases is a flat list keyed by purchase ID. No date/product grouping or deduplication is added. Distinct historical purchases for one product both remain visible. Search retains its existing result-type sections. |
| Filters | Successful-purchase and account predicates remain unchanged. My Purchases has no user-selectable history/status filter. Search filters remain untouched. |
| Pagination | Purchase repository/history has no explicit range/limit/cursor pagination. FlatList virtualization is unchanged. Server response caps can still limit which records are returned. |
| Status and amount display | Existing status labels/fallbacks, order-ID fallback, title/scope fallbacks, numeric amount passthrough and `₹{amount}` rendering remain. Refunded/failed/pending purchases remain excluded by the production repository. Screen branches for supplied failed/pending fixtures are preserved; the pre-existing unknown-status fallback also remains. |
| Refresh/navigation | Existing MV008 guarded resource retains account/course/year isolation, request generation, timeout, focus/resume refresh and unmount cleanup. My Purchases recovery/Play restore callbacks remain unchanged and are mocked in all screen tests. No real recovery endpoint was invoked. |

## Exact implementation and before/after behavior

Only `mobile-app/src/services/purchasesService.ts` changes application behavior:

- Add private `purchaseTime(unknown)` to accept numeric milliseconds and parse timestamp strings. Non-finite values, unparseable strings and values outside JavaScript's Date range become `undefined`.
- Normalize the existing timestamp candidate while creating each display record. Legacy `toMillis()` support and timestamp-field precedence remain intact.
- Sort the newly mapped array by numeric timestamp descending. Undated records follow all valid dates. Equal timestamps and undated ties use ascending purchase ID with plain string comparison, independent of locale and server response order. Identical IDs retain input order through stable Array.sort; duplicate source records are not repaired or merged.
- Return new display records without mutating the repository array or records. Ownership reads/helpers, monetary values, statuses, checkout, entitlement authorization and query count remain unchanged.

Example: input `[old: 2025-01-01T00:00:00Z, new: 2026-11-11T00:00:00Z]` previously returned `[old, new]`; now returns `[new, old]`. `10:00+05:30` is older than `01:00-04:00` on the same date and sorts accordingly. Equal instants `10:30+05:30` and `05:00Z` sort by purchase ID. Invalid dates no longer propagate an invalid display timestamp; the existing screen placeholder renders `—`. Valid historical dates retain the original formatting and instant.

No database query, SQL, schema, payment verification, settlement, recovery, Notes entitlement or price code was changed.

## Policy gate and evidence limits

Review date: 2026-11-11 UTC. Official pages were retrieved before implementation. Paid downloadable educational Notes are digital content; free videos do not exempt paid Notes. The general policy requires Play billing for in-app digital sales unless an applicable exception/program applies. See [Payments policy, sections 2, 8 and 9](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9858738).

**India:** Google's India program describes offering alternative billing alongside Play billing, not replacing it with a Razorpay-only checkout. Enrollment, applicable trust/safety certification, app settings and service fees apply. API-integrated authorized alternative transactions must be reported within 24 hours. The India page still mentions manual reporting; that paragraph is not sufficient evidence of a current exemption from API integration. No owner exception was supplied. Full removal while enabling the intended India Play experience therefore remains blocked. [Official India requirements](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/13306652).

**Required client integration:** user-choice billing uses the Play Billing Library, native choice UI and external transaction token. Product/offer selection and the ability to process a user's Play selection are connected parts of that flow. Alternative-only integration also uses the library, availability checks, Google's information dialog and reporting tokens. Removing only the payment listener is not a valid conversion to an alternative-only program. The existing client implements UCB, not the alternative-only APIs. [API overview](https://developer.android.com/google/play/billing/alternative), [user-choice client guide](https://developer.android.com/google/play/billing/alternative/alternative-billing-with-user-choice-in-app), [alternative-only client guide](https://developer.android.com/google/play/billing/alternative/alternative-billing-without-user-choice-in-app).

**Confirmed distribution:** the owner clarified during this task: **India-only Google Play distribution**. No enrollment or approved-exception evidence accompanied that answer. The India alongside-Play requirement therefore directly applies to the intended architecture; full removal is blocked. EEA/US examples below are background only and do not apply to the confirmed target. The [alternative-only program index](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/topic/16471103) identifies EEA and US programs. EEA terms require enrollment, eligible scope and API integration; [EEA program](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/12348241). The current [US program](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/16497028) allows alternatives in lieu of or alongside Play billing for eligible US users, with enrollment and API integration; its current text requires reporting/service fees from October 1, 2026. Neither regional program proves this app's approval or permits an unqualified India/worldwide Razorpay-only release.

No Console session, enrollment acceptance, package opt-in, country targeting export, exemption, reporting receipt, fee/payment profile or reviewer acceptance was inspected. Build flags with enrollment/acceptance names are configuration inputs, not evidence. Current production purchases stay off regardless of this local policy assessment. An app with no new purchases may retain a disabled integration without thereby proving the requested future paid architecture compliant.

## Repository inspection and exact safe changes

Inspected Git status before editing; the workspace had extensive tracked/untracked work, including mobile and shared-backend fixes. Read the production audit and MV003, MV004, MV005, MV006, MV008, MV009, MV010, MV011 and MV012 reports. Read `mobile-app/AGENTS.md` and the required exact [Expo SDK 57 reference](https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v57.0.0/) before writing code. `rg` is unavailable; used Python, find, grep and bounded source reads.

1. `paymentProvider.ts`: replace standalone `configuredProvider=play` eligibility with approval-gated UCB selection. Unapproved Play config resolves to disabled. Keep the original Android/package predicate separately as `playRestorationEnabled`, so disabling new sales does not suppress restoration previously available for that configuration. This does not broaden restoration to default disabled builds that previously did not invoke it. Approved UCB remains a retained future capability, not a newly enabled release. Razorpay isolated-package identity checks are unchanged.
2. `playBillingService.ts`: gate `createPlayOrder`, `fetchMatchingPlayProduct` and `startPlayPurchase` before HTTP/session/native/storage work when UCB is not enabled. Retain native connection without UCB for historical receipt restoration. Recovery, token/account binding, verification, pending receipt handling, in-flight deduplication and committed-fulfillment checks are unchanged. No legacy pending attempt is deleted by a rejected new purchase.
3. `app.config.js`: production, internal-testing and shrink-test build profiles now reject either enabled provider, including environment overrides. Preserve production EAS profile, package, URL, signing, OTA runtime/version, FCM, R8/resource shrinking and isolated Razorpay behavior.
4. `MyPurchasesScreen.tsx`: use the independent restoration predicate. Existing active/account checks and MV008 refresh semantics remain. No navigation route was removed or renamed.
5. Tests replace obsolete expectations that a production Play override and unapproved standalone new sales are allowed. Historical receipt recovery still runs against the actual retained service. The former staging-specific section of the billing-choice suite is replaced with production gate assertions; no old suite was deleted. New tests execute actual provider selection and history focus callbacks with synthetic fixtures.
6. Correct pre-existing submission/data-safety documentation claiming Billing was already removed. SDK/autolinking/permission retention and the blocked release architecture are now explicit. Historical data inventories remain for release-specific review; no Play Console declaration was changed.

**Dependencies removed: NONE. Native plugins removed: NONE. Permissions removed: NONE. IAP service modules removed: NONE.** No separable unused purchase-only module was found: the order/catalog/purchase implementation is called by the retained UCB summary and recovery. Deleting it would break that integration. `expo-iap@3.4.4`, its matching lock entry and conditional plugin are B/C below; `js-sha256` binds historical and UCB identities. No gratuitous package-lock rewrite occurred. There is no `react-native-iap` dependency, standalone Billing checkout route or direct app Gradle Billing dependency to remove. `usePurchase` is a shared Notes/Razorpay summary hook, not an obsolete Billing-only hook.

## Reference classification and disposition

A = standalone Google purchase processing eligible for separable cleanup; B = connected/required compliance or reporting integration; C = historical financial/receipt/ownership compatibility; D = unrelated Android/Google functionality or broad-search false positive. Mixed classifications describe connected responsibilities, not permission to delete the whole file.

| Reference area | Class | Disposition and reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Unapproved standalone Play provider selection; production enabled-provider override | A | Removed as supported new-sales eligibility. New service guards reject direct invocation; tests updated. |
| `expo-iap` manifest and lock entries; native autolinking; conditional Expo plugin | B/C | Retain: UCB native choice/token APIs and historical receipts. Not an unused purchase-only dependency. |
| `playBillingService`: create order, fetch product, standard offer token, requestPurchase/listeners | B/C | Retain connected UCB execution and historical verification. New entry points gated; no standalone sales capability supported. |
| `playOfferSelection` | B | Retain: exact INR quoted-price validation for Google's choice flow; guards unsupported offers. |
| `attach-external-token`, durable choice/context and `create-order-play-choice` client calls | B | Retain: binds Razorpay checkout to native choice and reporting context; removing it bypasses the required integration. |
| `verify-play-purchase`, receipt tokens, obfuscated IDs, restore/recheck and `get_my_play_fulfillment` | C/B | Retain: old paid receipts and selected Play transactions must not be lost or falsely marked fulfilled. |
| Summary choice branches, buyer-state selector and payment routing | B | Connected, retained behind disabled production gates. No replacement Razorpay bypass. |
| PaymentFailed provider branch, order/navigation historical fields | C | Retain pending/history compatibility and committed entitlement checks. |
| My Purchases restore | C | Preserve, independently gated from permission to start new sales. |

| Secret-name deny lists in build checks | B/D | Retain security checks; names are not client credentials or unused SDK settings. |

| All local mocks/assertions | B/C/D | Retain or update to the selected partial architecture. Tests are not live purchases. |
