## Razorpay integration integrity

The retained path is Notes → usePurchase → OrderSummary → `quote_my_order` server quotation → required native Google choice/token when using the Play UCB path → Razorpay order → existing HTML/WebView → trusted verification → committed purchase → secure Notes authorization. The distinct isolated non-Play package retains its separate synthetic test flow. **The production Play app does not currently have an approved operational Razorpay-only path.** This task did not introduce one.

| Integrity check | Local result / limit |
| --- | --- |
| HTML/WebView | Existing `checkoutHtmlTemplate.ts`, checkout.js loading and WebView remain unchanged; callback nonce/origin/structured-field checks pass. |
| Production configuration | EAS production URL remains `https://xbvenkfmesugrbjuufyw.supabase.co`; package `in.medveda.app`; provider disabled. No fallback to website create-order introduced. |
| Server prices/order identity | Server quote and refreshed totals, INR paise, provider/internal order identities and exact verify endpoint checks preserved; mismatch reconfirmation tests pass. No price write or client-supplied arbitrary amount added. |
| Signed callbacks | `razorpay_order_id`, `razorpay_payment_id`, `razorpay_signature` and actual local HMAC/backend tests pass; callback alone never proves entitlement. |
| MV003 commitment | Actual helper/SQL denies success for missing purchase/incorrect scope/compensation; committed historical ownership preserved; no deployed RPC assumed. |
| MV004 recovery | JWT/owner checks, bounded refresh, account pinning, pending status, polling and restart recovery pass with synthetic transports. |
| Success/failure | Pending/cancel/error/success tests pass; no unauthorized order-status write added. |
| Ownership/restoration | Successful account/scoped purchase repository and secure PDF signer unchanged; old receipts remain verifiable. MV006 and Notes suites pass. |
| Free content | Video data/link/tap and Notes preview/open tests pass; no video purchase gate added. |
| Backend integration | Shared order/verification/webhook/settlement/security source is byte-for-byte unchanged. No function deployed or called remotely. |

The original audit reports `create-order-play-choice` absent from its deployed listing and undeployed choice/reporting/schema dependencies. That remains a blocker, not an invitation to bypass the token path or call a different API. No new production function inventory was requested, and this task does not assert any missing function has since been deployed. ## Prepared flow and limits

The retained future flow is Notes → current catalog price → fresh `quote_my_order` → server Play order and exact matching INR offer → Google's native choice. A Play selection uses Play verification; an alternative callback saves/binds the token and Indian tax state, then the dedicated server reservation computes Razorpay paise. Changed totals require explicit reconfirmation. Razorpay callback alone grants nothing: trusted HMAC/provider capture verification, actual SQL settlement and MV003 commitment must all succeed. Success UI independently reads paid order and successful matching purchase. Notes refreshes authoritative scopes and the server signer still decides PDF access.

Pending/authorized/cancelled/failed/uncommitted/compensation outcomes cannot manufacture success in the tested local flow. Duplicate callback/webhook/settlement delivery is idempotent. Server purchases provide login/restart/reinstall restoration by design; account-scoped native attempts and authenticated order recovery preserve ambiguous checkout outcomes. Physical restart/reinstall and real provider completion remain unverified.

The new account checks prevent stale native choice events opening checkout for a switched account, including storage interruption. The new handler checks reject stale/malformed reservations before provider work, even when a stale/mock RPC response says `ok:true`. They grant no entitlement, change no catalog and activate no checkout.

**The owner-dependent branch remains BLOCKED.** Genuine native tokens, approved enrollment, accepted reporting, correct country targeting, production RPC deployment and native/device evidence cannot be created by local code/mocks. Existing source does not constitute an activation-ready approved release.

| Successful purchases attached to non-paid / failed orders | 2 / 2 |
| Duplicate successful user/product groups | 1 |
| Paid orders without success purchase / success owner mismatch | 0 / 0 |
| Successful NULL-product purchases | 0 |
| Active products at ₹1 / ₹2 / ₹5 | 4 / 7 / 2 |

These are invariants to preserve during a coordinated DDL-only window, not a request to improve historical numbers. No customer/payment identifiers are retained in this review's new evidence. Existing paid-purchase and Notes authorization readers are unchanged. `user_can_access_note` and its AAL variant retain service-only contracts; deployed `get-note-url` remains v20, JWT=true. Live signing/PDF retrieval was not probed. Access preservation is supported by unchanged production reader definitions and actual local Notes predicate/restoration tests, not a claim of live authenticated device acceptance.

Relevant deployed functions are unchanged: `create-order` v23 JWT=true; `verify-payment` v68 and `razorpay-webhook` v50 JWT=false; `get-note-url` v20 JWT=true; `create-play-order`, `verify-play-purchase`, `play-payment-worker` v3. The proof RPC and both new triggers are still absent. Deployed callback/webhook bundles still lack the new commitment call. This review does not certify those old bundles as complete fixes. The inactive Play consume/reconcile/void/catalog schedules remain inactive; existing active `release-wallet-credits` and retention jobs were only observed, not altered or invoked. Default isolation is READ COMMITTED; this does not prove the isolation of every externally managed writer.

Complete SQL/security/locking review:

| Artifact | Behavior and limits |
| --- | --- |
| A1 | Adds `razorpay_entitlement_committed(text,text)` boolean, SQL STABLE SECURITY DEFINER. Requires the exact paid Razorpay order, matching successful payment/purchase, owner/product/amount/provider identities and complete immutable entitlement snapshot. Missing historical capture markers are allowed; existing inconsistent/reversal captures, any compensation and unresolved pending/parked reversal facts deny proof. Applied reversal handling relies on durable purchase state, preserving legitimate partial refunds/disputes won. No settlement, capture backfill, access-reader replacement or historical repair. Snapshot proof does not promise access cannot change after a later refund. |
| A2 | Adds `guard_razorpay_order_transition()` and enabled BEFORE UPDATE OF status trigger `orders_03_razorpay_transition`. Rejects paid → unpaid, refunded → other states, and transitions to unpaid states when a visible capture exists, with 23514 and no privileged bypass. Same-status updates return unchanged; existing failed/success history remains. Order row locking protects against late failure after committed settlement. Capture-only insertion is not a universal cross-table serialization barrier; the trigger neither verifies a provider nor grants entitlement. |
| A3 | Adds VOLATILE SECURITY DEFINER `guard_purchase_success_ownership()` and BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger `purchases_zz_success_ownership`, after current snapshot triggers. New keyed successes/restorations use the existing `wallet:<user>` transaction advisory lock and a fresh READ COMMITTED ownership check, including the historical index exception. Conflicts raise 23505 and abort the caller transaction; higher-isolation keyed grants fail closed with 40001. Existing successful metadata updates, non-success reversals and legacy NULL keys retain semantics. Same-order insert replay relies on the unchanged unique order index; ON CONFLICT updates still pass through the UPDATE guard. Existing historical duplicates remain grandfathered. |

All three functions fix `search_path=pg_catalog, public, pg_temp`, qualify application tables and use no dynamic SQL. PUBLIC/anon/authenticated EXECUTE is revoked. A1 explicitly grants service_role; the production postgres default function ACL also grants service_role to the trigger functions. Expected owner is postgres, matching existing relevant table/function ownership. Clients lack CREATE on public, limiting search-path object shadowing. Service-role direct financial writes and superusers disabling triggers remain trusted administrative boundaries, not protections supplied by RLS or these guards. No new client financial write policy or grant is introduced.

A1 → A2 → A3 is the approved review order; A2/A3 do not intrinsically call A1. Runtime settlement/creation/reversal/dispute routines share wallet-before-order locking; the A3 advisory lock is reentrant for those callers. Direct/bulk privileged writes can instead hold purchase rows before acquiring that lock and can deadlock; the correct outcome is rollback and operator investigation, not guard bypass. A2 executes after existing order identity/entitlement guards; A3 sorts last among the current purchase BEFORE triggers. Trigger ordering must be rechecked after any schema change.

Expected DDL side effects are explicit: new function catalog entries/default owner grants, ACL revocations/grant, new trigger catalog entries and dependencies, normal catalog/WAL/cache invalidation, bounded DDL relation locks, and prevention errors on formerly accepted invalid future writes. Replaying A2/A3 drops/recreates their named triggers transactionally (new OIDs), and CREATE OR REPLACE would replace same-signature functions; unexpected existing objects therefore block the proposed first-install operator wrapper. No table scan for ownership uniqueness/index build, table rewrite, financial DML, new table/column/index/constraint/policy, historical trigger firing or worker activation occurs.

Seven enabled production DDL event hooks were read in full: PostgREST DDL/drop watchers can enqueue schema-reload NOTIFYs at commit; extension-specific pg_cron/pg_net/pg_graphql hooks only act for their matching extension events; GraphQL placeholder restoration only concerns GraphQL drops; the public automatic-RLS hook only acts on table creation. These three function/trigger DDL bodies do not request extension or table changes. No provider network call is made by the inspected hook paths. Evidence: [production-ddl-hooks.json](evidence/phase-a-final-20261111/production-ddl-hooks.json). Hosted hook execution/cache propagation is not reproduced by the local Supabase stub.

Any backup creation or restore requires separate authorization. No account upgrade, recovery change or restore is implied by this report. Supabase documents that database backups exclude Storage object bytes and that restoration capability depends on the configured recovery service; platform documentation is not evidence this project has it enabled. [Supabase backup documentation](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/backups), [restore-to-new-project documentation](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/clone-project).

## 4. SQL dependency matrix

Target is current production **plus exactly** A1 → A2 → A3. No November 8/9 migration or future ownership-index maintenance is included.

| Required object/contract | Candidate | Source / expected behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `razorpay_entitlement_committed(text,text)` | B1/B2 | A1: service-only boolean SECURITY DEFINER exact order/payment/purchase/snapshot proof; no capture marker required for compatible history; compensation and unresolved reversals deny proof |
| `guard_razorpay_order_transition()` / `orders_03_razorpay_transition` | B1/B2/B3 indirectly | A2: terminal/captured order transition protection; conditional B3 updates avoid invalid regressions |
| `guard_purchase_success_ownership()` / `purchases_zz_success_ownership` | B1/B2 indirectly | A3: shared advisory ownership lock, fresh READ COMMITTED check, duplicate/restoration prevention, retained historical exceptions |
| `settle_razorpay_payment(text,text,text,bigint,timestamptz)` | B1/B2 | Existing void-return service-only RPC; delegates to retained legacy routine; normal return alone never proves entitlement |
| `record_payment_capture(text,text,bigint,timestamptz)` | B1/B2 | Existing durable idempotent capture fact |
| `record_provider_event(...)` | B2 | Existing authenticated inbox; immutable payload hash / event identity; no blocks_entitlement column |
| `handle_payment_refund(...)`, `handle_payment_dispute(...)`, `reverse_payment(...)` | B2 | Existing service-only reversible accounting/access contracts; actual status result parsed; unknown outcomes retry |
| `create_priced_order(uuid,text,boolean)` and legacy pricing | B3 | Existing SQL-authoritative catalog/discount/wallet pricing and immutable order snapshot |
| `rate_limit_hit(text,integer,integer)` | B1/B3 | Existing limiter; recovery fails closed when limiter is unavailable |
| orders / purchases / payments | B1/B2/B3 | Existing owner/product/amount/provider references, scope snapshot and statuses; current RLS/service grants and unique provider/order indexes retained |
| payment_captures / payment_compensations / provider_events | B1/B2 | Existing capture states, compensation queue and authenticated inbox; no new columns |
| profiles / products | B3 | Existing profile identity/course/block and active-product/course columns |
| wallet ledger, referral/discount reservations, identity hashes, review flags, webhook event tables | RPC transitive dependencies | Existing baseline accounting routines and triggers, preserved verbatim |
| `user_can_access_note` and publication/MFA helpers | Preservation checks | Existing Notes authorization unchanged; real SQL access/restoration checks pass |

[Full SQL dependency closure](evidence/phase-b-20261111/sql-dependency-closure.json) contains **37** exact normalized definition SHA-256s and call/trigger edges. All 34 pre-existing reachable definitions match fresh production; the other three are exactly Phase A. The scan includes trigger functions attached to written tables, strips comments/literals before finding calls, and the relevant source was also inspected for dynamic execution/external side effects. Managed `auth.uid`/`auth.jwt` and PostgreSQL/pgcrypto primitives remain platform dependencies reproduced with the existing local stub; they are not new SQL objects.

The retained baseline dump's `normalize_phone(text)` lacks production's existing search_path and has different body line endings. `supabase/tests/support/phase_b_production_normalize_phone.sql` reproduces the exact freshly read production definition locally before tests. It is explicitly a **local baseline fixture, not a fourth migration or deployment prerequisite**. The corrected 37-definition comparison passes byte-hash checks. An initial overinclusive scanner treated a `delete_my_account()` comment as a call; stripping comments/literals corrected that false edge. No payment dependency was waived.

## 6. Function configuration and deployment prerequisites

B1 gateway `verify_jwt=false` is required for JWT-free signed callbacks; unsigned recovery authenticates inside the handler. B2 gateway `verify_jwt=false` is required for Razorpay HMAC webhooks. B3 retains `verify_jwt=true` and handler Auth verification. Changing these to a blanket setting would break legitimate callers or weaken authentication. Each archive contains its exact single-function configuration.

Runtime requires injected `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, existing correct live Razorpay ID/secret, and explicit `APP_ENV=production`. `ALLOW_TEST_MODE` cannot bypass that provider-mode check. B2 additionally requires the correct webhook secret and optionally the previous secret for the existing retry window. `UCB_SENTRY_DSN` is optional best-effort alert transport for B1/B2; it has no schema dependency. No secret was set or changed; configured secret correctness is NOT VERIFIED by local fixtures. CORS behavior remains the current shared contract.

Before any future rollout: retain current raw bundle/config preimages privately; independently verify source/config/archive hashes and the complete import closure; inspect fresh production schema/ACL/index/trigger/cache drift; establish service-role execution and READ COMMITTED writer compatibility; verify exact A1/A2/A3 delivery with independent read-only metadata. Current baseline alone is insufficient for B1/B2. A missing proof RPC fails closed but would interrupt verification/recovery, so it is not an acceptable planned deployment state.

## 3. Google Play India integration

India's program permits alternative billing **alongside Play** and requires package onboarding, trust/safety arrangements and reporting authorized transactions within 24 hours. India is distinct from the general pilot; no general-pilot business-registration requirement was inferred for this app. Enrollment/terms/payment profile, package/India opt-in and payment-method branding remain owner evidence. [Official India requirements](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/13306652?hl=en).

The native API flow uses Play product details and offer token, Google-rendered choice, and an alternative-selection callback whose token is persisted before alternate checkout. Unsupported Play countries receive standard Play flow; a hand-built Razorpay selector cannot replace this flow. [Official native integration](https://developer.android.com/google/play/billing/alternative/alternative-billing-with-user-choice-in-app).

For a new November 11, 2026 release, Billing Library 7's extension has expired: a supported **8+** release is required. The historical 5.2 API feature minimum is insufficient for publishing. [Official version timeline](https://developer.android.com/google/play/billing/deprecation-faq).

Actual inspected chain: installed `expo-iap 3.4.4` → its Android Gradle dependency `openiap-google 1.3.26` → exact public Maven POM declares `billing-ktx 8.3.0`. The downloaded source maps init's user-choice program to USER_CHOICE, invokes `enableUserChoiceBilling` via reflection, forwards token/product IDs and launches BillingClient purchase flow. The Expo native wrapper installs listeners before connection; manifest retains BILLING permission. [Pinned native evidence](evidence/phase-c-20261111/native-inputs.json) contains POM/source hashes and URLs. This verifies declared upstream dependencies/source, **not the app's resolved release dependency graph or merged manifest**. Reflection failure is logged by the library, so device acceptance must demonstrate actual native choice.

## 4. Razorpay Notes-choice integration

Existing compatible pieces are retained: Auth-derived identity; Notes-only product/course/scope validation; server quote/reservation and exact INR/paise arithmetic; discount/wallet/tax snapshots; token transfer to one uniquely reserved order; exactly one provider CREATE claim. Ambiguous creation retries search by internal receipt and validate provider ID, receipt, amount, INR and all user/product/order/choice notes; they never issue another CREATE for that reservation. Changed quote requires explicit reconfirmation in Order Summary. ₹1/₹2/₹5 fixtures pass unchanged.

Callbacks validate order/payment HMAC; webhooks validate original raw bytes and support the previous secret for older retries. Provider GET facts then establish captured status, amount/currency and order binding. Durable capture precedes atomic settlement. Strong commitment, not a normal void RPC return, permits success. Authorization only queues reporting and never grants Notes or debits wallet. Refunds/disputes suspend/reverse/restore access through existing routines; compensation remains separate from entitlement. [Razorpay integration](https://razorpay.com/docs/payments/server-integration/nodejs/integration-steps/), [webhook validation](https://razorpay.com/docs/webhooks/validate-test/).

Actual callback/webhook/HMAC with real local SQL PASS; ambiguous-creation/provider snapshots and authenticated authorization use synthetic transport. Actual Razorpay authorization, WebView checkout, provider retry delivery and payment-to-PDF acceptance are **NOT VERIFIED**. No real/test provider order was created.

## 5. External reporting

Stable `medveda_` internal-order-based IDs and database uniqueness preserve identity across retries. One-time token, INR pre-tax/tax snapshots, Indian state and transaction timestamp form the request. GET-before-CREATE and readback after ambiguous failures reconcile the same ID, package, amounts, address and timestamp. Google-derived output-only test markers must agree with server mode; no fake token/test flag is sent to Google. Refunds serialize by durable refund ID, reconcile partial pre-tax balances, and wait for original reporting; lost disputes enqueue a stable full-reversal identity. [Backend reporting requirements](https://developer.android.com/google/play/billing/outside-gpb-backend), [external creation API](https://developers.google.com/android-publisher/api-ref/rest/v3/externaltransactions/createexternaltransaction), [refund API](https://developers.google.com/android-publisher/api-ref/rest/v3/externaltransactions/refundexternaltransaction).

Authorization queues a report even while order is unpaid. Its timestamp is recorded once after provider status/amount validation, and queue deadlines survive later capture/retry. **Timestamp limitation:** existing authorization handling uses provider payment `created_at` as its stable conservative time anchor, not a separately exposed `authorized_at`; lost-dispute reversal time uses first local observation. Do not label these exact provider authorization/resolution instants. Owner/provider/API acceptance must validate timestamp semantics; the creation-time anchor generally advances the deadline rather than extending it. Genuine token validity, Google transactionTime acceptance, tax treatment and genuine refund/dispute acceptance remain **NOT VERIFIED**.

Durable reports/refunds jobs use deadline priority, expired-lease recovery, backoff and parking. Missing/mismatched reporting results and persistence errors do not count as completed jobs. Synthetic acceptance/rejection/timeout/ambiguous retry/duplicate/full-partial refund tests PASS; **no Google reporting API call was made**. The current monitoring helper is best effort, and the reporting queue-metrics read does not itself establish independent alert delivery. Operational monitoring is a production blocker.

Record genuine provider and Google readback; preserve all ₹1/₹2/₹5 prices and existing Notes. No AAB publication follows from build/test approval.
8. Re-evaluate all twelve gates with current evidence. Prepare an exact proposed production activation diff/artifact, backend/build/runtime/gate values, exposure/window, fail-safe and observers. Request the owner's separate explicit approval for that concrete action. Publication and financial workers remain separate approvals. Only after approved execution and independent state verification may any statement say production Buy Now is active.

On drift, missing recovery, unaccepted Google reporting, native failure, inconsistent historical disposition or any remaining mandatory gate: stop that rollout step, preserve financial facts and existing legitimate access, and leave new purchases disabled. A mobile disabled gate is not a global backend kill switch; existing legacy website endpoints were not shut down or probed. No blanket payments-off claim is made for every backend caller.
